## How to use this document

Source: the 2026-07-09 design-review wave (7 parallel review passes over all 15 docs; the
~50 mechanical fixes were applied directly — this file holds only what needed an actual
decision). **Status: D-01…D-39 + all 14 ratifications closed 2026-07-10; D-40…D-55 decided 2026-07-12…2026-07-28 — all CONFIRMED except D-48's Billing countersign (pending that gear's PRD); D-56 added by the 2026-07-28 code-readiness review; D-57…D-68 added by the 2026-07-29 review (3 parallel passes — core model / money+governance+windows / cross-doc+seams; D-58/D-59/D-60 flagged for veto, D-65/D-66 joint with Subscriptions and Rating); **D-69…D-75 added by the 2026-07-30 propagation review** — four decisions of the previous waves (D-58, D-60, D-67) had reached the design but not the FR/AC/DoD that make them testable, and the two PRD↔design contradictions the semantic layer had reported (`fr-invoice-currency-binding`, `fr-plan-phases`) are now closed in favour of the design (D-73/D-74, flagged for veto with the carve-outs they follow). **D-76…D-78 decided the same day** from the review's own findings — the two [H]/[M] items §F.2 had carried (snapshot synthesis, band unit) and one §F.1 product fork (overlay × grandfathered), each taken on its recommended option. **D-79…D-86 added by the 2026-07-30 manual slice review** ([`reviews/2026-07-30-slice-design-review.md`](./reviews/2026-07-30-slice-design-review.md) — single sequential pass over all 12 slices): 3 [H] (the D-01-pattern subscriber-count source D-79, the D-76 temporal half D-81, the supersession unit guard D-82), 4 [M] (D-80/D-83/D-84/D-85), and the read-model storage/retention decision D-86; D-79/D-80 joint with Subscriptions. **Veto round 2026-07-31: the full flagged backlog — D-56, D-58, D-59, D-60 (+D-69), D-73, D-74, D-79, D-80, D-81, D-83 — CONFIRMED per-item by the product owner.** **D-87…D-98 added by the 2026-07-31 manual slice review** ([`reviews/2026-07-31-slice-design-review.md`](./reviews/2026-07-31-slice-design-review.md) — second single-reviewer sequential pass over all 12 slices): 3 [H] — the D-01-pattern consumption gap of tier-2 synthesis (D-87), the missing supersession change unit + changeover-instant floor (D-88), the phase-axis reopening of the D-82 class (D-89) — plus 8 [M] mechanism completions (D-90…D-97) and the D-82 amendment D-98; the wave's shared pattern: recent decisions (D-76/D-81, D-83, D-86, D-06, D-82, the L-4 fix) landed on their cited surface but not on the adjacent one. **Veto round (same day, per-item): D-93, D-94, D-98 all CONFIRMED by the product owner.** **D-99…D-112 added by the 2026-07-31 second manual slice review of the day** ([`reviews/2026-07-31b-slice-design-review.md`](./reviews/2026-07-31b-slice-design-review.md) — third single-reviewer sequential pass over all 12 slices; `spec-check` clean): 4 [H] on the **read side** the previous six waves never audited — window mutations were consumer-visible without being publish units (D-99), the cutover could not commit because nothing flipped its predecessor (D-100), a pinned `CatalogVersion` did not resolve stably under D-91's per-subject markers (D-101), and the `migrated-origin` payload D-87 made self-contained had no read surface (D-102) — plus 10 [M]: injectivity per line (D-103), bundle materiality (D-104), child-table keys (D-105), the last two plan children's revisions (D-106), overlay index scoping (D-107), the contract lock's real subject (D-108), retirement materiality (D-109), the tax-category home + one basis per market (D-110), and the two performance items the wave surfaced (D-111 bulk validation scope, D-112 the overlay access path). **Veto round (same day, per-item): D-103, D-104, D-108, D-109 and D-110 — all CONFIRMED by the product owner** (D-110 on both halves: `taxCategory` moves to the price row *and* the mixed-basis ban lands). Remaining live qualifiers: D-48's Billing countersign, D-67's open stacked-result fork (§F.1), and the owed cross-gear adoptions (Rating: the D-78 cohort filter + the D-113 carry-vs-reset adoption — target-snapshot routing, per-line unit check, absence default, a SEAMS row, and reconciling rating `design/09`'s "fail-closed when absent" to the decided default-reset; Subscriptions: SUB-P7 executor + SUB-P8 presence read + the D-93 read-time classification and D-94 gate-conjunction adoptions, SEAMS SUB-P1/SUB-P5). Product forks still open are in §F.1, findings still carried forward in §F.2. The 2026-07-31b wave also adds a standing question beside D-68's sibling-surface heuristic: **for every consumer-visible fact, name the publish unit that makes it visible and the surface that reads it** — all four of that wave's [H] items were mechanisms completed on the truth side and never given a consumption path, the same class as D-01, D-65, D-79 and D-87.** **D-113…D-122 added by the 2026-07-31 third manual slice review of the day** ([`reviews/2026-07-31c-slice-design-review.md`](./reviews/2026-07-31c-slice-design-review.md) — fourth single-reviewer sequential pass over all 12 slices; the first to read the **consuming side** of every snapshot-frozen contract field in the sibling gears' own documents; `spec-check --auto-context` clean): 3 [H] at the three boundaries the earlier waves stopped short of — the plan-change carry-vs-reset flag Rating and Subscriptions both consume "from the pinned snapshot" existed nowhere in pricing (D-113, the D-01 class **between gears**), pin-eligibility was not prefix-closed so a stuck older version's late warm re-opened the D-101 replay divergence one version out (D-114), and the materiality delta domain was defined only over amount columns so every quantity-semantics and contract-field money lever auto-published below a configured threshold (D-115, the D-50 class through the non-amount door) — plus 7 [M]: the override-ref key-family (D-116), orphan phase overrides forbidden (D-117), bulk import pinned to the draft plane (D-118), the bundle tax-basis sibling of D-110 (D-119), overlay scope-value universes (D-120), the plan-subject projection set + horizon (D-121), and `package_size` joining the preserved set (D-122). **Flagged for veto: D-113 (joint with Rating + Subscriptions), D-115, D-117, D-118, D-119, D-122.** **D-123…D-125 added by the 2026-07-31 billing-domain review** ([`reviews/2026-07-31d-billing-domain-review.md`](./reviews/2026-07-31d-billing-domain-review.md) — a domain-lens pass, deliberately **partial coverage** (two of four verification agents and all six domain-semantics finders were killed by a session limit; the doc records what was and was not verified, three retracted candidates, and six unverified carry-forward items): 1 [H] — the proration/anchor contract was N-valued per plan-market while Subscriptions reads a single cycle clock per subscription (D-123, uniformity per `(currency, region)`, the D-110 shape) — and 2 [M] mechanism repairs: the D-111 aggregate pass moved to commit entry over the plan's full post-run row set (D-124 — the original justification cited `inst-mp-pending` and the bulk lock for a window neither covers) and the gear-wide cursor-pagination contract (D-125 — the export SLO was per 100 records with no page contract anywhere). Cleanup applied in place: the §6 tenant-rule cross-references repointed to Foundation §2.2 + `inst-rb-pep` across ten slices, the S5 taxonomy enumerations widened to D-120's four classes, `inst-co-single-pending` generalized to every unit kind (+ PRD AC #112), `pricing_approval.subject_kind` typed, an observability NFR added to PRD §7.1, `pricing.bulk.run_failed` declared, the D-115 registered enumeration completed with the plan-change contract content, the preview grant's region-set evaluation stated (`inst-rb-preview-scope`), two stale D-109 strings fixed, and the `migrated-origin` read's tenant predicate stated. **Flagged for veto: D-123 (joint with Subscriptions + Rating).** **Veto round 2026-08-01 (per-item, product owner): D-113, D-115, D-117, D-118, D-119, D-122 and D-123 — ALL CONFIRMED.** The D-113 confirmation also unqualifies rating's adoption T-D-29. Entry bodies keep the historical "flagged for veto" wording as provenance — this board is the status source. **D-126…D-138 added by the 2026-08-01 manual slice review** ([`reviews/2026-08-01-slice-design-review.md`](./reviews/2026-08-01-slice-design-review.md) — fifth single-reviewer sequential pass, lensed on **failure and re-entry paths**: what the *second* run of a mechanism does, what a mid-way failure leaves behind, and what each rule costs at scale; `spec-check --auto-context` clean): 4 [H] — the grandfathering **entry** hole nobody had looked at, since the cohort selector only closes from the second cutover (D-126, joint with Rating); the ×24 unit guard's fifth door, the cutover successor (D-127); retirement as a consumer-visible fact with no publish unit *and* no projector source afterwards (D-128); and the compiled `carry` grant bound to a `price_id` that its own change mechanism replaces, so a routine reprice silently stops the allowance (D-129) — plus 9 [M]: the allowance compile destroying its own input (D-130), the D-79 lane's response shape (D-131, joint with Subscriptions), the two newest market-uniformity rules freezing a market forever through an immutable generation (D-132), `overlay_index` as an O(commits × overlays) copy-on-write document on the order-time path (D-133), D-124's aggregate pass resting on an all-or-nothing application the run never promised (D-134), the per-tenant audit chain serialising every mutation (D-135), pin-eligibility as a predicate with no storage/surface/owner (D-136), bulk import's approval machinery outliving D-118 (D-137), and `fixed` as an authorable kind defined in no document (D-138, joint with Rating). Eight Cleanups applied in place: the row-less `sum_of_parts` bundle's exemption from the row-quantified plan rules (S8 `inst-bb-rowless`), "the longest billing cycle sold on **the key**" defined for keys with no frequency (S7 W6), required-add-on coverage over the `depends_on` closure (S4 `inst-cb-addon`), the terminal phase's `kind` made enforceable (S2 `TERMINAL_PHASE_KIND_INVALID`), the `migrated-origin` payload gaining the plan-level descriptor + grant set (S11 `inst-sy-payload`), the D-125 page cap reconciled with the export SLO unit (S12/PRD), the clone's copy/remap set completed (S12 `inst-cl-copy`), and D-113's routing naming `PlanLink` migration (S6). Four candidates were **checked and refuted** and are recorded in the review — including the 20-currency-vs-500-row-cap arithmetic, which closes one of the six items 2026-07-31d carried forward unverified. **Veto round 2026-08-01 (per-item, product owner, same day): D-126, D-132 and D-138 — ALL CONFIRMED as decided**, each against its stated alternatives: D-126 keeps the fall-through to `all_subscriptions` when a pin's closing instant carries no generation (a deliberate, single exception to fail-closed — the alternative made every routine supersession on a key with in-flight subscribers an incident source until re-bind); D-132 accepts that a grandfathered cohort may keep a display basis or anchor the current rows have moved away from, rather than letting one cutover disable tax/anchor migration for a market indefinitely, and **without** widening the published-row mutation whitelist; D-138 keeps `fixed` as a **replacement** rather than cutting it to Future or making it an additive synonym of `markup` — an absolute partner price stays expressible. **D-139 added 2026-08-02 from outside the review cycle** — found while authoring the `reserved` joint fixture, which could not be written because pricing and rating carried two formulas for one charge (`capacityCharge` with and without the covered-duration factor; rating's T-D-25 was adopted and confirmed 2026-08-01 and never propagated here). **Veto round 2026-08-02 (per-item, product owner): D-139 CONFIRMED as decided**, against both stated alternatives — keeping two factors would read a per-granule rate as a period charge, which is a units error rather than a policy choice, and redefining `reservedRate` as a period rate would fix the dimension while breaking the level-billed product D-53 exists for, where the rate must be per-granule to compose with the granule fold. **Nothing in pricing awaits veto.** Owed cross-gear adoptions are thereby unqualified: **Rating** — the D-126 cohort bootstrap (`CohortGenerationSelector`/`CohortPin` + a SEAMS row) and D-138's `fixed` semantics (step-4 line application + the `maxCumulativeMarkup` interaction); **Subscriptions** — D-131's lane response shape (SUB-P8, shape only, not the trigger). **D-140 added 2026-08-02 while scaffolding the gear crate** — the first finding raised from the implementation side rather than by a review pass: every documented REST path was unimplementable under the platform route convention the build enforces (version-first prefix + three colon-suffixed custom methods, both denied by the `DE0801` lint that runs in the gear's phase gate and in CI). Decided by the product owner the same day — `/bss-pricing/v1/{resource}`, actions as sub-resource segments — and applied as a mechanical, contract-neutral rewrite across PRD, DESIGN, this register and all twelve slices; no `(resource_type, action)` pair, payload, status code, error name or SLO moved. **Not flagged for veto** (the owner took the call when shown the lint), so **nothing in pricing awaits veto**. **D-141…D-148 added 2026-08-02 from the implementation side as well** — the **second** wave raised by building the gear rather than by a review pass, and unlike D-140 the same day it did not come from a lint refusing to build the documents: Group **G3**, the gear's draft-authoring plane on `bss/pricing-impl`, was written **against** this set, and fifteen places turned up where the code needed a rule the documents do not state. No `reviews/` document stands behind the wave, so each entry carries its provenance in its own body (*found 2026-08-02 while building G3*). **Twelve** of the fifteen items became **eight** decisions and the remaining three were applied in place as mechanical fixes carrying no id (the cross-reference that makes the shared `pricing_price` column set visible from both slices that declare columns on it, the nullable-tolerant `package_size`/`package_price_minor` check forms, and `max_hold_granules` widened to `bigint` with its upper bound folded into the existing gloss); a sixteenth item was withdrawn on inspection (below). **Three** collapses were taken — they remove four would-be entries, which is what turns twelve items into eight — each because the items collapsed are one call seen from two sides: the price row's missing ETag and the unguarded `DELETE` are one token and one rule (**D-141**); the dedup row's unrepresentable `claimed` state, its semantics-free TTL and the write-once retry are one row's lifecycle (**D-142**); and the two refusals `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` was collapsing are one call about how far that code may generalise (**D-146**). The in-flight duplicate was deliberately **not** folded into D-142 (**D-143**): it carries a different severity, its own wire code, and the wave's only product flavour. **Flagged for veto: D-143** — naming a discriminated, retriable code narrows a stated **MUST**, `fr-mutation-idempotency` gaining "once the original has completed"; everything else was taken as technical, so **D-143 was the wave's only flagged item**. **D-144 is joint with Rating**, and its owed adoption is one clause on the already-owed D-126 cohort bootstrap — the comparison is at the millisecond quantum — not a new adoption. Two decisions are taken **against** what G3 already built and are owed back to `bss/pricing-impl` as follow-through rather than lost between branches: D-144 refuses sub-millisecond instants where the code truncates, and D-145 replaces deletion with an `abandoned` tombstone where the code deletes. The dedup store's **retention** is deliberately not decided here and is recorded as a new open fork (§F.1). **The consolidation pass over the finished wave added three things and settled one**, all recorded in the entries themselves: D-145 gained a fifth statement of its fact that the first draft had not named (the PRD's `fr-plan-retirement` still said the open draft revision is *deleted*) and gained the **caller** its transition lacked (`POST …/plans/{planId}/abandon`, S2 §5 — the author-driven arm of `inst-pl-abandon` had been a state change nothing invoked); D-141's `**Decision**` gained the row-version freeze the Foundation had already stated normatively, so the register is not the weaker text; and D-148's one open assumption — that no staged composition can put a **second** draft on a canonical scope key — was **verified against `inst-su-compose`, `inst-co-copy`/`inst-co-successor`, `inst-co-single-pending`, `inst-bk-phase1` and D-118, and holds**, so the index needs no narrowing. One reported item was **withdrawn** and is kept as provenance because it was reported once: nothing, it said, names who deletes a retired plan's open draft revision — but S11 `inst-rt-cancel` names the actor (`cpt-cf-bss-pricing-actor-catalog-admin` under `plan × retire`), the transaction and the endpoint in full, so no document owed anything; the engineering gap is an unimplemented Slice-11 instruction, not a silence in the design set. Its lesson joins D-68's sibling-surface heuristic and the 2026-07-31b publish-unit-and-reader question as a standing one: **a silence in the section you are reading is not a silence in the design set — check the slice that owns the mechanism before recording an omission.** **Veto round 2026-08-02 (per-item, product owner): D-143 CONFIRMED as decided**, against the stated alternative — **block-and-replay**, holding the duplicate until the original commits and then answering it, which preserves the **MUST** whole and was rejected because a client can act on the discrimination (retry shortly, and the answer is the stored response or `IDEMPOTENCY_PAYLOAD_MISMATCH`) and can do nothing at all with a held connection, which pins one open for the length of the guarded mutation and needs a timeout policy this design set does not have. **The `abandon` endpoint was put to the owner in the same round and kept** — the consolidation pass minted `POST /bss-pricing/v1/plans/{planId}/abandon` because D-145 forbids deleting a draft revision while an operator still needs to discard one, and flagged the new capability as the owner's to reverse; the alternative, narrowing D-145 to the retirement case only, was rejected because it would leave the authoring path with neither a deletion nor a state, so a draft an operator wanted to abandon could only be overwritten. **D-145 was then amended the same day, from outside the wave that wrote it** — an independent review of what G3 actually built found the entry's "so a new draft opens immediately" false for exactly one plan, the one that has **never published**: its only revision is `abandoned`, so it has no current revision to open a successor from and no free revision `0` to create one at, and the two authoring arms answer 500 and 404 for a plan id that is simply spent. The owner **kept the state and made the refusal honest** — a new Foundation-owned code, `PLAN_ABANDONED_NO_SUCCESSOR` (422), owed by the authoring REST surface that does not exist yet (G7) — against two alternatives: minting `max(revision) + 1` in the create path as well, which would turn a retried create naming the same id into a silent second revision of an existing plan where creation idempotency requires a refusal; and letting revision `0` alone be re-minted, which reintroduces precisely the unstable name D-145 exists to remove. Entry bodies keep the historical "flagged for veto" wording as provenance — this board is the status source. **D-149…D-154 added 2026-08-03, the third wave raised from the implementation side** — Group **G4**, the *shape of a plan* (Slice 2's four validator sets and the three revision-scoped child tables), built on `bss/pricing-impl` against this set; as with D-141…D-148 no `reviews/` document stands behind it and each entry carries its provenance in its own body (*found 2026-08-03 while building G4*). Fourteen findings became **six** decisions, **four** mechanical fixes carrying no id, and one obligation recorded rather than decided. The wave's centre of gravity is different from the previous one's: G3 found rules the set did not state, G4 found rules it **states and cannot enforce** — §5's problem-response list naming no code for a §3 requirement, or a §6 `CHECK` standing where a rule belongs. **Three collapses were taken**, removing seven would-be entries: the four codeless cycle-shape requirements plus the undeclared `billing_cycle` that made the whole step vacuous are one call about what §5 owes (**D-149**, two codes and one code-assignment, not five entries); the required add-on's `maxQty ≥ 1` and the two bounds the implementation had to add itself are one defect — *these bounds admit no selection* — on one row (**D-150**); and the descriptor required-set's absent config carrier and the four ratified caps whose "tenant-configurable" named no store are one question about where per-tenant configuration lives in this gear (**D-152**). **D-151** is the wave's `display_trial_days` finding and grew on inspection: the reported half was that the drift `CHECK` is NULL-satisfied when the duration is absent, and the half nobody had named is that nothing binds the field to the `trial` **kind** at all, so the `evergreen` terminal phase — where `inst-ph-duration` and the C-4 terminal-kind rule are each *correctly* silent — could publish a trial length for a plan with no trial phase. **D-153** is the price plane's half of the hole D-145 closed on the plan plane, carried forward from G3 and still true: a column whitelist cannot constrain a **draft** row's transitions, and `draft → superseded` took a row outside *both* scope-key partial `UNIQUE` predicates, undoing D-148's guarantee with one UPDATE. **D-154** came out of the descriptor rule covering three of D-48 v1's five elements: `billingTiming`'s owner and code exist (S6 `inst-bt-required`), while `taxCategory` was a **pinned** contract element two documents answered differently about — a publish-blocking MUST here, a tenant policy with an allowed `warn` mode in Slice 4 — and was frozen as a raw column whose fallback half lives on a mutable taxonomy row, against the same contract's own no-re-query promise. **Flagged for veto: D-152 and D-154** — the first fixes **tenant** scope for a required-set inside Billing's pinned contract, the second removes an arm from a tenant-configurable policy the PRD says covers both; everything else was taken as technical. The **four mechanical fixes** carry no id by design: `tenant_id` stated in the three Slice-2 child tables' §6 column lists (the section preamble already declares them tenant-scoped, so a reader building the tables from the lists alone omitted it); the injectivity index predicate gaining `AND meter IS NOT NULL`, which is **semantically inert** — `meter` is NULL on every non-usage row and a NULL never collides — and states the "usage rows" scope the same sentence already claims; the add-on edge columns' `[]` glossed as a JSON array on both backends, `SQLite` having no array type (the `included_allowance` transform); and the `custom_every_n` token named as what the `frequency` column holds for the custom case, which neither §6 nor the PRD had said and which storage has now frozen into a column `CHECK`. One obligation is **recorded rather than decided**: the descriptor rule's coverage of three of five is **total, not deferred** — the two row-borne elements are checked by nothing in the crate because neither Slice 4's nor Slice 6's storage exists — and it is written into D-154 so the slice that lands them inherits it. Four decisions are owed back to `bss/pricing-impl` as follow-through rather than lost between branches (D-149's three rules, D-150's rule, D-151's rule, D-152's per-tenant reads); **the gear's code is correct as written and is deliberately not changed by this wave.** **Veto round 2026-08-03 (per-item, product owner): D-152 and D-154 — BOTH CONFIRMED as decided.** **D-154 without qualification**, against both stated alternatives — making `tax_category_ref` unconditionally required per row, which deletes the coalesce rule S4 has confirmed twice and makes every author restate a region-level default on every row, and narrowing D-48 so `taxCategory` is not publish-blocking, which removes a pinned element from a contract Billing has not countersigned: publish resolves the effective category and freezes the **result** with the row, and a row whose effective category resolves to nothing fails publish **whatever the tenant tax-display policy says**, the policy keeping its `ratePresent=false` arm (a rate is a fact no one in this gear owns while Tax Engine is pre-GA) and losing the category arm (a pinned contract element is not a display preference). **D-152 CONFIRMED with one qualification the owner added and which is recorded in the entry**: `pricing_policy_object` carries the descriptor required-set extension and the four ratified caps **for now** — the owner expects these settings to move to a **settings gear** later — so the carrier is **provisional**, and D-152's body and the `design/01-foundation.md` §3.7 bullet both say so, because that bullet is what an implementer reads when they wonder why a per-tenant cap lives in a pricing table. No such gear exists in this repository today and `gears/simple-user-settings` is **not** it (a per-**user** `theme`/`language` store, checked 2026-08-03), so the note names a destination that has to be built rather than one to migrate to now; the reasons and the preconditions are in the entry. Entry bodies keep the historical "flagged for veto" wording as provenance — this board is the status source. **Nothing in pricing awaits veto.** **D-155…D-161 added 2026-08-03, the fourth wave raised from the implementation side** — Group **G5**, the **publish commit** (the pipeline re-run inside the commit transaction, the lifecycle flips, the transactional outbox, the fail-closed `CatalogVersion` request and the segmented audit chain), built on `bss/pricing-impl` against this set; as with the three waves before it no `reviews/` document stands behind it and each entry carries its provenance in its own body (*found 2026-08-03 while building G5*). Ten findings became **seven** decisions, **two** mechanical fixes carrying no id, one open fork, and three findings recorded as **needing no change to the design set**. The wave's centre of gravity is different again: G3 found rules the set did not state and G4 found rules it states and cannot enforce; **G5 found the set contradicting itself, and promising values with no producer.** Two contradictions and two producer-less promises account for four of the seven entries. **D-155** is the wave's [H] and the one a reader should start from: nothing said **which** row set the commit flips, so a commit that re-derives the draft set at flip time is a conforming reading of §4.2 — and it publishes rows the rule set never judged, through a window that contains a network round-trip. It is now pinned by `(price_id, row_version)`, D-141's token spent on the thing it was minted for, with the rule set's other inputs enumerated and **one entry in that list stated as a premise rather than a mechanism**: the *membership* of the published row set is held today only by the fact that this gear has no producer for `published → superseded`, and the group that builds D-88's supersession unit or D-100's cutover deletes that premise and owes the published half a guard. **D-156** settles the ordering contradiction between §3.6 and §4.2 in §4.2's favour — the registry request goes inside the transaction, after re-validation and before the writes, with a `request_id` derived from `(tenant, plan, revision)` so a retried commit re-requests the same handle — and names the cost it buys with, a network round-trip inside an open transaction. **D-157** gives the pending-ref row the `(subject_kind, subject_ref)` pair without which no path leads from a committed handle back to what it published, and records the widening an overlay unit's two subjects will owe. **D-158** declares the audit log's two discriminator vocabularies, `subject_kind` **being the approval store's enumeration verbatim** rather than a parallel one, and `action` an additive `snake_case` verb set seeded from the records this set already requires — never a frozen event name, and never a token with no writer. **D-159** mints **`CONCURRENT_MUTATION` (409)** for the loser of a same-aggregate race, which had been landing as an internal fault indistinguishable from a dead connection; it is the sixth member of §3.3's retriable-conflict class and is neither `STALE_VERSION` (nothing the caller presented was stale) nor `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_IN_FLIGHT` (the duplicate there is the caller's own). **D-160** makes the advisory channel code-carrying and gives its two stated advisories their codes — `TIER_BAND_PRICE_INCREASE`, which the crate had been raising under a token no document declared, and **`PLAN_SIZE_SOFT_CAP_EXCEEDED`**, without which §14's ratified soft caps could not be reported at all and were therefore built as nothing. **D-161** is the second [H] and the one that leaves here unclosed: `pricingSnapshotRef`'s catalog-side stamp has three parts and the commit can produce two, no document naming a producer or a format for the third. The decidable half is decided — publish stamps **no placeholder**, because Rating's composer fail-closes on a missing pre-stamp (correctly) and would be *satisfied* by a fabricated one (fatally, and years later) — and the rest is an **open fork for the owner, jointly with Rating** (§F.1), which records the three readings, their differing consequences for an already-posted period under a catalog semantic change, and the recommendation. It is a **launch blocker**: while pricing stamps nothing, Rating's fail-closed composer rates no charge. **Three collapses were taken**, removing five would-be entries: D-155 carries the pinned judged set, the closed input enumeration and the Slice-7 premise as one call about what "the commit re-validates" means; D-159 carries all three of the gear's per-aggregate serialization points under one code, by D-146's line (the remedy is identical at each); and D-160 carries both soft caps under one advisory, by the same line. The **two mechanical fixes** carry no id by design: the outbox's `(tenant_id, aggregate_id)` ordering named as the unique sequence that enforces it (§3.7 asserted the ordering and named nothing that holds it, which is what D-159 needed a reference point at), and §1.2's NFR-allocation row un-blurring "publish-time size validation" — the two soft caps and the two interval caps had been listed as one item while the first pair is advisory and the second blocking (`INVALID_CUSTOM_INTERVAL`). **Three findings were checked and are recorded as needing nothing**, because a reported finding that turns out to be the design set being right is worth the same sentence as one that is not: `ROUNDING_POLICY_UNRESOLVED` has two renderings in the **code** and exactly one in the documents (§3.3 already says it is "registered into the pipeline by the Foundation itself"), so the pipeline rendering the implementation kept is the documented one; `inst-tp-distinct` is a step of the **approval-decision** algorithm (S5 §2's two-person enforcement, whose input is "an approval decision request" and whose store carries `CHECK (approver_principal <> submitter_principal)`), so the publish commit consuming an approval rather than re-deciding it is the single-owner arrangement the set already specifies, and G5 declining to enforce half a rule whose other half (`inst-tp-selfaudit`) it cannot write is correct as it stands; and **D-153 is closed in code on both backends**, the plan that reported it open having been wrong about what the trigger did. Decisions owed back to `bss/pricing-impl` as follow-through: D-158's remaining vocabulary tokens as their records land, D-159's distinguishable refusal, and D-160's soft-cap advisory rule. **Flagged for veto: nothing** — every entry is a technical call, and the one item carrying commercial weight is not decided here at all but forked (§F.1). **D-162 closed that fork the same day**, the product owner taking §F.1's recommended option **(a)**: the evaluation-policy segment is a **vocabulary generation**, `ep-<n>`, a declared constant of the gear naming which evaluation-policy field set a snapshot's frozen row content is read under. Writing it required writing down the field set itself, which no document had: the phrase appears sixty-odd times as an unexpanded token and is expanded exactly once, in the PRD glossary, as three fields that D-40, D-44 and D-45 have each added to since — so the entry declares a **nine-field roster** before it declares the version naming it, and declares what sits outside it and why (identity, money, and quantity *origin* as against quantity *derivation*). The entry's weight is not the constant but the guard: a generation nobody remembers to bump asserts on posted money a stability that is not there, so the bump is a **mechanism** — §4.4 carries a normative append-only log of `ep-<n>  <decision>  ± <field>` lines, the roster is what replaying that log produces, the last generation is the declared one, and the gear's build reads the block. A field cannot enter the roster without a log line, a log line is a generation, and the last generation is the constant. What the generation deliberately does **not** claim is recorded beside it — it tracks the field *set*, not the meaning of a field, which is the coverage option (b)'s digest would have bought and this does not — and `ep-1` claims **no retroactive history**, since minting generations for the four decisions that moved the set before anyone wrote it down would produce versions no snapshot was ever stamped with, which is D-161 clause (1)'s fabrication one artifact over. D-161 clause (1) is otherwise unchanged: publish stamps a real generation or none. **Rating's adoption is owed** — equality comparison only, fail-closed on an unrecognised generation, and the "pricing pre-stamps its two segments" count corrected in `design/01` §4.3 and `design/11` §4 — and one hole is recorded rather than rounded off: D-113's plan-scoped `usage_counter_on_plan_change` is an evaluation input outside this roster only because the plan-content type it would be classified against does not exist in the crate yet, and the slice that lands it joins the roster **under a bump**. **Nothing in pricing awaits veto, and this wave's fork is closed** — §F.1's remaining rows are the ones that were already open before it. **D-163…D-168 added 2026-08-03, the fifth wave raised from the implementation side** — Group **G6**, the **read side** (turning a pending `CatalogVersion` handle into a committed number, projecting the frozen per-subject read model, advancing the pin frontier and running the degraded path), built on `bss/pricing-impl` against this set; as with the four waves before it no `reviews/` document stands behind it and each entry carries its provenance in its own body (*found 2026-08-03 while building the read side*). Fourteen findings and three review rounds became **six** decisions, **three** mechanical fixes carrying no id, one open fork, and several items recorded as code debt rather than as design gaps. The wave's centre of gravity is different again from the four before it: G3 found rules the set does not state, G4 found rules it states and cannot enforce, G5 found it contradicting itself and promising values with no producer — **G6 found rules that are decidable only under premises nobody had written down, and clauses that cannot be satisfied with what exists.** **D-163** is the wave's most consequential entry and the one to start from: §4.4's pin-eligibility counts "every subject row that version projects", which is decidable only if a version's subject set is **closed** once any of its refs commits — i.e. only if a batch commits atomically into one version — and §3.6 says the registry "batches approved publishes" and never says that. It is now **normative and the registry's**, because a premise living only in the consuming gear's code is deleted by the first change to the producing gear that nobody thought to check, and the registry is vendored (D-47) so there is a document to write it in; the detection stays beside it, because a property stated across a gear boundary is a promise and not an enforcement; and the **price** of that detection is stated with the guarantee rather than as a footnote — a ref resolving into a version the frontier has passed is refused permanently, so that publish becomes unresolvable at any version and needs an out-of-band re-request. The entry's second clause is what keeps that price attached to a contract violation rather than to an ordinary outage: the projector may decide a version complete only from a pass that could have seen the whole subject set, which a saturated pending scan and a per-ref registry error both deny it. **D-164** settles two readings of D-136's advance sentence that are each unbuildable and fail the same way — the frontier stops forever: pin-eligibility is **this tenant's** (the `CatalogVersion` sequence is cross-tenant, so read globally no frontier could ever advance past a version another tenant's publish consumed), and the advance **walks forward** through every already-complete following version, without which §4.4's own D-114 worked example strands `V6` behind `V5` permanently. **D-165** is the wave's money entry: the projector sourced the plan's **current** revision, so a second publish inside D-47's five-minute batching **maximum** froze its content into the earlier version, permanently, in an INSERT-only store whose whole contract is that a completed version never changes — the ref row now pins the revision **and** the lifecycle state the publish's own judgement produced, the state column admitting only the two tokens D-128 sanctions. **D-166** gives the three publish-visibility signals disjoint predicates and the one instant they all needed: `commit_observed_at`, without which the 5s post-commit warm SLO has no start (`committed_at` is stamped by the finalize, which never runs on the failing path) and measuring it from the publish's own age marks **every** publish degraded, including every one behaving exactly as D-47 budgets; the degraded state itself has no column and needs none, and `pin_eligibility_overdue` fires on the frontier's `advanced_at` only in conjunction, a stale frontier alone being a tenant that has not published. **D-167** dates the read model's own promises: the delta's field list is declared nowhere (the D-158 shape one store over), D-121's horizon filters a window set Slice 7 has not built — so the projection proceeds under the stated premise that the plan's accumulated history is empty while no producer for `published → superseded` exists, which the **D-88**/**D-100** group deletes and then owes the horizon and `H`'s own input — and `inst-sg-pinned`'s six sellability predicates are a claim about the **finished** gear, a version produced today answering three. **D-168** is the wave's fork: Slice 6 requires a **pair** of read-model fields on every plan subject row and names the value of one, so the pair is stamped as a pair or not at all (D-161 clause (1) one artifact over, and worse, because a frozen version can never be corrected) and where the warning **text** comes from is the owner's, with the options and a recommendation in §F.1. **Three collapses were taken**, removing five would-be entries: the batching index that made D-47's normal case impossible, the completeness bound and the straggler refusal are one call about when a version may be called complete (**D-163**); the per-tenant scope and the forward walk are one call about what D-136's advance sentence means, failing the same way and repaired in the same sentence (**D-164**); and the degraded mark, its missing threshold and both Critical alarms are one call about the single clock this gear has (**D-166**). The **three mechanical fixes** carry no id by design: `pricing_catalog_version_ref`'s instants named in its §3.7 bullet (§3.6 conditions `commit_overdue` on a ref's age and named no column to measure it from); §4.4's "consumers observe completion via the marker" repointed at the **pin frontier** the marker advances, the marker itself being on no read surface since D-136 gave that job a surface; and the statement that a **projection writes no audit row**, the trail being the actor/before-after record of a mutation and a projection having neither. **`commit_overdue` alone needed nothing** — §3.6 states its predicate precisely, on the ref's age and nothing else, and it is recorded here because a reported finding that turns out to be the design set being right is worth the same sentence as one that is not. Decisions owed back to `bss/pricing-impl`: D-163's completeness bound and per-tenant scan, D-165's two columns (built), D-166's instant and its two narrowed predicates, and one contradiction filed rather than fixed (a refused frontier advance renders as a lifecycle refusal where D-146 calls it an internal fault; nothing on a wire path can reach either rendering today). **Flagged for veto: nothing** — every entry is a technical call, and the one item carrying commercial weight is not decided here but forked (§F.1). **One owed cross-gear adoption is new**: the registry gear (`gears/bss/products`, docs-only, vendored) owes batch atomicity as a property of `CatalogVersionPublished` on its own side, and the commit instant carried with the committed version. **D-169 closed that fork the same day**, the product owner taking §F.1's recommended option **(c)**: the catalog publishes the K3 marker (`crossBoundaryChangePolicy = cancel_plus_new`) and **no copy** — `crossBoundaryWarningText` leaves the contract, and the surface that renders the warning owns its wording. Writing the answer turned up the fact the fork statement did not have: the warning already had an owner in this set — PRD **AC #66** requires the preview/migration UI to warn that in-place credit is forfeited and to take an explicit confirmation, and §17.6 points at AC #66 for exactly that — so the field was a *second* home for a sentence the PRD had already placed on the surface that displays it, and the second home is the one with an INSERT-only ≥ 7-year store behind it. **(a)** lost on the store rather than the sentence (a customer-visible string frozen in one language, permanently, for every version already stamped, in a set with no localization story anywhere) and **(b)**, per-tenant on `pricing_policy_object`, lost because re-authorability is going forward only — every already-stamped version stays frozen against what the tenant had at the time — on a carrier D-152's own confirmation marks provisional. D-168 clause (1) is **discharged rather than overridden**: with one field there is no half to stamp, so the S6 AC it recorded as unsatisfiable is satisfiable, and the read side gains a field it can stamp today (owed back to `bss/pricing-impl`, which stamps neither half). **So a second owed cross-gear adoption is new, and the standing list gains it: Subscriptions owes the countersign** — the warning's **wording** is theirs, their `SEAMS.md` SUB-P1 already carrying the plan-change classification contract and D-93's change-time boundary class, their `design/03-plan-changes.md` already routing cross-boundary changes to cancel+new while naming no text field, and nothing in their set saying today who authors that sentence. Removing a field from a published consumer contract is a counterparty transaction, the D-162 shape one gear over. The full standing list of owed cross-gear adoptions is therefore: **Rating** — the D-126 cohort bootstrap (with D-144's millisecond clause), D-138's `fixed` semantics, and D-162's equality-only generation comparison with the "two segments" count corrected; **Subscriptions** — D-131's lane response shape (SUB-P8), the D-93/D-94 and SUB-P7 items, and now **D-169's warning copy**; **the registry gear** — D-163's batch atomicity and the carried commit instant. **D-170…D-174 added 2026-08-03, the sixth wave raised from the implementation side — and the one that closes Phase 2** — Group **G7**, the gear's **REST surface**: the nine authoring routes, their preconditions, their authz gate and the `OpenAPI` registration a client is generated from; as with the five waves before it no `reviews/` document stands behind it and each entry carries its provenance in its own body (*found 2026-08-03 while building G7, the gear's REST surface*). Roughly twenty-three divergences and two independent review rounds became **five** decisions, **three** mechanical fixes carrying no id, **no** new fork, one correction to the standing list, and several items recorded as **code debt** rather than as design gaps. The wave's centre of gravity is different again from the five before it: G3 found rules the set does not state, G4 rules it states and cannot enforce, G5 the set contradicting itself and promising values with no producer, G6 rules decidable only under premises nobody had written down — **G7 found the set stating a contract and never stating its transport.** Four of the five entries are that: what a precondition contains, what carries it, which surfaces owe a refusal, and what a digest is taken over — each a thing the documents name and never render, and each one a client cannot be written against. **D-170** is the wave's most consequential entry and the one to start from: a plan route resolves to one of **two** revisions — the open draft when there is one, the current revision otherwise — and no document gave a plan tag a shape at all, so a token carrying only a row version could not say which revision it came from; the counters being unrelated (a freshly opened successor stands at the initial version, exactly where a first draft stands), a tag read from revision *N* satisfied the compare-and-swap on *N+1* **with no race window at all** — the lost update D-145 removes from storage, reopened in transport by addressing a plan without naming a revision. The tag now names both, `STALE_VERSION` covers a mismatch in either component (**nothing minted**), and the tag is declared **opaque**: the rendering is stated so the set says what a client receives, not so a client may parse it. **D-171** gives the preconditions their wire names — `If-Match` and `Idempotency-Key`, both **required** — after the audit that neither string appears anywhere in the PRD, DESIGN, this register or any of the twelve slices, while nine slice §5 tables carry an Idempotency column naming the concepts; the mapping is stated once in §3.3, as the D-125 pagination contract and the D-140 route shape are, and it covers the cells naming a **natural** idempotency (`per revision`, `per decision`), which require no header at all. **D-172** corrects D-145's amendment: its spent-plan paragraph opens "every surface above names a `planId`" and then enumerates one that does not — `POST /bss-pricing/v1/plans`, whose stated collision mechanism presupposes a caller-supplied plan id that §4.3's own "a plan id is minted server-side" rules out — while being silent about `GET`, which does name one and answers **404**. Three arms, not four; D-145's substance is untouched, and what falls away is one rejected alternative's *argument* rather than its conclusion. **D-173** settles the `PATCH` that named four facets in one verb while the storage versions all four against one row version each of them advances, so a two-facet request can match the caller's tag on the first and never on the second: one facet per call, and the coherent multi-facet operation is **named as a capability nobody has designed** rather than quietly dropped. **D-174** decides what the idempotency digest is taken over — the request as this gear models it, not the bytes it arrived in — because the byte reading refuses a **correct** retry with a 409 the caller cannot fix whenever anything in the path re-serializes, and the parsed reading's own consequence (a retry adding an unmodelled member replays) is stated rather than discovered. **One collapse was taken and one was deliberately refused.** D-170 carries the authoring read's answer on **two** surfaces and the tag's shape as one call — what a plan route addresses — because the tag needs a revision component precisely *because* the read resolves to one of two revisions, so they are one call seen from two sides. D-170 and D-171 were **not** collapsed although one act of writing the registration found both: each would stand whole without the other, D-170 deciding what a plan tag contains and D-171 what carries a precondition on every surface of the gear including the slices nobody has built. The **three mechanical fixes** carry no id by design: the undecodable **cursor** named as a malformed request under the validation envelope (§3.3's D-125 block, the settled D-141 rule applied to its sibling; *a count of "some twenty-five such gaps, and no code minted for any of them" stood here and is **struck** 2026-08-03 — no report in this program carries the list, the figure escalated 17 → 20 → 25 across three plan preambles on its own, and it is not even true of the waves it summarises, two of which did mint codes (D-159, D-160, and D-145's amendment). What is true and checkable is the **rule**: since D-141 an absent or malformed precondition is a malformed request under the validation envelope, and every entry that has applied it — D-171, D-173, D-174, D-177 — says so in its own body*); the statement that a cursor walk is **not a snapshot** (a row deleted behind the cursor was already returned, one deleted ahead is never returned, and neither skips or duplicates a row at or before the cursor, which is all D-125 promises); and the **discharge** of the "this gear has no authoring REST surface yet" sentences in §4.3 and S2 §5, G7 having landed and raising `PLAN_ABANDONED_NO_SUCCESSOR` in process and on the wire. **Three findings are recorded as code debt rather than as design gaps**, because the design set is right about each and the crate is behind it: draft authoring is **unaudited** while D-135 requires the record inside the mutation's own transaction and only two of six mutating paths have one (the group declined to audit two of six deliberately, a trail that is *silently* incomplete being worse than one visibly absent); **nothing pins the header declarations**, so deleting one fails no test — a guard with no test, which is what the group's own second review round was about; and the authz scan **bans a spelling rather than a constructor** (`use … AccessScope as Reach`, `<AccessScope>::…`, a line split, a future subdirectory), catching the accidental case it was written for and no other. **Flagged for veto: nothing** — every entry is a technical call, the two that touch a wire format take the standards' spellings or a rendering declared opaque, and none narrows a stated **MUST**. **No cross-gear adoption is new**, so the standing list above is unchanged in substance — but it needed **one correction**, made at the phase boundary and recorded because a standing list that has drifted is worse than none: §F.1's **`fixed` arithmetic** fork was answered by **D-138** on 2026-08-01 (`fixed` **replaces** the running line amount, confirmed the same day) and stood in the open-forks table for two days and five waves regardless; it is struck, and the half of it that was riding along — the **stack sort direction**, the total order `precedence → class order → overlay id` being stated without ascending or descending, which D-138 makes strictly consequential since a `fixed` line discards every layer beneath it — is given its own row, as a joint contract with Tariffs rather than a commercial fork. Everything else in the standing list was checked at the same time and is accurate: D-48's **Billing countersign** is still pending and §F.2 still records why (that gear has no doc set to disagree with), D-67's **overlay-stack floor** is still open, and the three owed adoptions above are unpaid and unchanged. **Nothing in pricing awaits veto.** **D-175…D-178 added 2026-08-03, the seventh wave raised from the implementation side — and the first raised by a group whose whole purpose was closing debt rather than building a plane.** Group **G8** took the register's own owed-back clauses, six waves of them, and built what they oblige: thirteen commits, 761 → 847 tests, five audit writers where there had been one, four plan-shape rules, the soft-cap advisory, the contention refusal, the degraded instant, the completeness bound and the cross-boundary marker. As with the six waves before it no `reviews/` document stands behind it and each entry carries its provenance in its own body (*found 2026-08-03 while closing the implementation's debt against this set*). Sixteen numbered divergences and two review rounds became **four** decisions, **five** mechanical corrections carrying no id, **no** new fork, **two** corrections to the standing list, and several items recorded as code debt. The wave's centre of gravity is different again from the six before it: G3 found rules this set does not state, G4 rules it states and cannot enforce, G5 the set contradicting itself and promising values with no producer, G6 rules decidable only under premises nobody had written down, G7 a contract whose transport was never stated — **G8 found the set's own accounts of itself untrue**: an enumeration read as closed that was only provenance (D-175), a mechanism named without its evaluation point (D-176), a primitive judged by nothing while three normative artifacts advertised it (D-177), a field required on two stores with no producer anywhere (D-178), and — outside the four — a register entry claiming a discharge on a surface that does not exist. **D-177** is the one to start from and the wave's only [H]: `includedAllowance` and `tierQualificationWindow` were storable, echoed, projected and rostered in `ep-1` while **none** of their ten refusals and neither their compile nor their rate-lock existed, so the only thing that read either field was a guard asserting it must not *change*; they are refused at authoring on **every** path — bulk import included, that being the second path onto the same draft plane — the two members stay modelled because D-174 clause (1) would otherwise make an authored allowance *silently ignored*, and the refusal is named **load-bearing**, removable only in the change that lands the rules, because the day the publish route mounts it is the one thing standing between an unjudged allowance and an immutable ≥ 7-year version. **D-175** declares the three draft-authoring verbs `create` / `update` / `delete` that five of six mutating surfaces had been obliged to write records under and had no token for, and — the load-bearing half — states the vocabulary's **closure rule**: D-158's opening list stated provenance and was read as closure, so "no token without a writer" gains its companion, **"no writer without a token"**. The implementation minted the three verbs itself and reported them as minted; **the product owner ratified that act on the single condition that the debt land in S5 §6 in this wave**, which this entry is. **D-176** states where a precondition is evaluated — inside the transaction that writes the mutation it guards — after the `PATCH` arm whose write is an *insert* was found comparing the caller's tag outside it, so a concurrent publish could leave a successor revision copied from a revision the caller never read while the call answered as though its precondition had held: D-170's own defect, on D-170's own arm, one window further out. It also makes the fusion of that arm's two transactions normative rather than a judgement left to whichever group arrives next. **D-178** gives the correlation id a producer: `inst-au-complete` and the `pricing_outbox` bullet both *required* one and the string occurs twice in the entire design set, both times as a field in a list of fields, so the join that says two records on two D-135 chains were one operator action rested on nothing. The **five mechanical corrections** carry no id by design, and four of them are corrections to this register rather than additions to the design set — which is itself the wave's shape. (i) **D-172's owed-back clause was false**: it claimed the refusal is raised "on all three surviving arms" when the third arm's surface, `POST …/plans/{planId}/publish`, is not a mounted route; two arms and the read are paid and the third is owed by whoever mounts that route. This is the **second** register entry in this program to overstate its own discharge, and it survived a full group because a code group is forbidden to reconcile a divergence by editing a document — which is the constraint working as intended provided the next documentation wave actually looks, so **looking is now part of the wave**. (ii) The **unsourced count** of "some twenty-five codeless gaps, and no code minted" is struck from this preamble: no report carries the list, the figure escalated 17 → 20 → 25 across three plan preambles unaided, and two of the waves it summarises did mint codes. (iii) A **standing review heuristic** is added below. (iv) §F.2's projector-fairness item is **narrowed to what is actually open** — the implementation took the per-tenant-cap shape, so the round-robin is not a live candidate, the value is provisionally 250 and the owner's, and the order is ascending tenant id and **not rotating**, which means the tail past the bound is not swept at all rather than swept late, and making it rotate needs a cursor no §3.7 table carries. (v) D-164's filed contradiction gains the concrete owner the implementation identified (the group that lands a read-model resolution surface). One design-set clarification rides with them: **`OPEN_DRAFT_REVISION_EXISTS`'s caller is named** in §3.3 — under D-170 no authoring request *asks* for a second draft, so the refusal answers the loser of the race for the plan's editable slot, which is exactly the index conflict its own gloss already named and is why it is not `CONCURRENT_MUTATION` (the loser's remedy is to re-read and edit the draft that now exists, not to replay the call). **Recorded as code debt rather than as design gaps**, because the set is right about each and the crate is behind it: that same index's violation currently lands as an internal fault, so the race loser gets a 500 where §3.3 says 409 and where the refusal the set declares is sitting unused two lines away; `correlation_id` is `None` on every authoring record (D-178's owed half); the **aggregate unexercised surface** — six things no gate exercises, of which two are the whole publication path (`PublishService::precheck`/`commit` have zero production callers, `PricingCatalogClientV1` has no `impl` anywhere in the repository, `catalog_version_ref_repo::find` has zero callers including tests, `DomainError::ReadModelUnavailable` has zero producers, and `idx_pricing_read_model_resolve` matches no query that exists) — whose halves were each documented and whose **sum** was recorded nowhere; and one test combination that is missing rather than a property (no walk over a segment holding two publish records). **Flagged for veto: nothing** — three entries are technical calls, and the fourth, D-175, was put to the owner *before* this wave and ratified. D-177 weakens no `MUST`: it decides which surface refuses what while a slice is unbuilt, and D-40/D-45/D-59/D-60/D-130 stand exactly as decided. **The standing list needed two corrections, and both are omissions rather than drift.** First, it has never carried the **`trailing_tier` joint fixture** — `inst-tt-fixture`'s publish block names a `FixtureGate` variant that exists in this design set and in **no** conformance registry, `bss_fixtures::Variant` having four members and none of them that one, so the block cannot fire; it is joint with **Rating** by D-60, Rating's own SEAMS M12 carries it as open on their side ("plus the joint fixture") *while asserting that pricing carries the variant*, and it has been owed since 2026-07-29. It is added. Second, the list gains **Slice 12's arm of D-177** — the bulk-import refusal — which is owed inside this gear rather than across a boundary. The full standing list of owed cross-gear adoptions is therefore: **Rating** — the D-126 cohort bootstrap (with D-144's millisecond clause), D-138's `fixed` semantics, D-162's equality-only generation comparison with the "two segments" count corrected, and now the `trailing_tier` joint fixture; **Subscriptions** — D-131's lane response shape (SUB-P8), the D-93/D-94 and SUB-P7 items, and D-169's warning copy; **the registry gear** — D-163's batch atomicity and the carried commit instant. Everything else was re-checked against the documents on 2026-08-03 and is accurate: **no** Rating artifact cites D-126, D-138, D-144 or D-162 and **no** Subscriptions artifact cites D-131 or D-169, so all six adoptions are genuinely unpaid; the registry gear's PRD still states D-47's batching SLO without the atomicity property or the carried instant; D-48's **Billing countersign** is still pending with §F.2's reason intact; and §F.1's five open rows are all still open, the two closures in that table (D-138's `fixed` arithmetic, D-162's and D-169's forks) being the ones already struck. **A standing review heuristic joins the four this register carries**, and it is a test-shape rule rather than a design one, earned twice in one group: **a test that rebuilds its expectation from the data it is checking cannot see that data replaced.** Three audit writers had a correct before/after pair; blanking all three to null left every suite green, because the only readers were digest-recompute walks that derive each record's preimage from the stored columns and therefore stay self-consistent under any substitution. **Connectedness and content need different tests**, and the content one must hold its expectation independently of the row it checks. **D-180 and D-181 added 2026-08-03 by the phase-closing documentation wave** — the wave that lands what the phase's code groups reported rather than absorbed, and the first in this program whose whole content is other groups' reported debt. Two entries, two facts recorded without an id, one carried-forward finding, and two corrections to this register. **D-180** applies D-175's closure rule to the store D-175 did not sweep: that rule was written against the *authoring* plane and audited against six mutations there, and the **approval** plane opens a change unit and withdraws a pending one under two tokens no document declared. `withdraw`'s warrant is strong — `inst-as-void` names the surface and calls it audited — and `submit`'s is deliberately stated at its real strength, §8's `dod-audit` and nothing wider, the `inst-tp-record` citation the minting group first offered having been **withdrawn** on inspection (§6 assigns that instruction to `approve`/`reject`, whose records satisfy it with or without a `submit` token). The entry also makes the **machine-driven** void's silence normative rather than an omission: the TOCTOU guard has no principal, and a synthetic one would be the trail asserting an actor that did not act. **D-181** amends D-178 on the two questions building its edge surfaced. D-178 left the transport's spelling to the platform on the reading that this gear had nothing to consume; it has — `toolkit`'s canonical-error middleware already extracts a W3C trace id, this gear already mounts it, and the primary spelling parses into the `uuid` column. The value is nonetheless **minted, not adopted**, on granularity rather than shape: a trace names a whole distributed trace and not one operator call, only one of the three spellings fits at all, so adoption would give a trail-to-trace join that holds for some callers and silently does not for others — and the key rides `pricing_outbox` to four consuming gears. The platform-side change that would make it a real choice is named (`toolkit` exposing the extracted id as a request extension, so nobody grows a third copy of `parse_w3c_trace_id`) and is **new on the standing list**, owed by the platform rather than by any gear here. **Two facts carry no id by design**, being statements about evidence and not changes to any rule: D-159's contention refusal and D-135's segmentation benefit now hold **by execution** — two real concurrent transactions choreographed on `pg_locks` observations rather than on hope, with whole-transaction rollback proved by a witness segment holding zero rows (S5 §6); and the physical enforcement S1 §3.7 claims is proved on the backend it targets, 68 CHECKs, 10 trigger functions behind 10 triggers and 8 partial indexes each answering an executed refusal across 171 Postgres tests, where every one had previously been verified by reading statement text beside an executed `SQLite` mirror (S1 §3.7). **The two corrections to this register are both omissions.** **D-179 had no status-board row** — it landed the same day as G8's four and was never boarded, on a board this document calls the status source; the row is added. And §F.2 gains a finding the approval-surface build reported and this wave verified: **no default role can withdraw an approval unit** — `inst-as-void` names the submitter or a `CatalogAdmin`, the endpoint map makes a `POST` on that path `approval × approve`, and the role matrix withholds `approve` from `CatalogAdmin` in as many words, so the only principal who can reach the transition is the reviewer it was not written for. Recorded rather than decided: all three closures move either the catalog or the transition, which is the owner's. **Flagged for veto: nothing** — D-180 is additive on a vocabulary D-158 makes explicitly additive and enables a MUST rather than narrowing one, and D-181 changes no clause of D-178, only replacing an implicit premise with what was checked. **Nothing in pricing awaits veto.** **D-182 added 2026-08-04 by the phase that builds the window plane, and it is the first entry in this register written *before* the code it governs rather than after it** — the ordering being the point, so that the implementation cites a decision that exists instead of a decision documenting code already written. `inst-fg-trailing`'s only exemption is decided by the D-79 Subscriptions inbound lane, which in this repository has no client, no contract type and no counterpart gear; and the counterparty's own SUB-P8 still specifies the pre-D-131 **union count** rather than the per-price-id presence map every consumer of the lane needs, so the exemption is not waiting on plumbing over an agreed contract but on the contract D-131 replaced. D-131 already makes a silent lane the subscribers-present case, so the window routes mount and **refuse** every cancel and shorten that would leave a key uncovered — including the ones the set exempts — under the already-declared `WINDOW_TRAILING_VOID`: **no second code is minted** and no dead exemption branch is written, the exemption's shape being recorded in the entry instead. Slice 11's retirement inherits the same refusal with the **opposite sign** (it cancels nothing, where the set would let it cancel on empty keys), which is one absent lane producing two opposite-signed consequences and is why the sibling surface is named rather than discovered. Recorded because the refusal is a standing rule rather than a temporary gap. **Not flagged for veto** — it enforces a MUST on more inputs rather than narrowing one, and the one operator-visible consequence is named in the entry rather than buried. **D-183…D-193 added by the 2026-08-05 phase-4 close review** — the first wave taken against the *implementation as built* rather than against the documents, and the first in which this register's own entries were re-derived before minting: two of the twelve findings the review handed over were counterfactual as written and one would have made things worse if implemented (D-192 clause (3)). Two [H]: the window approval unit that pinned the world *before* the act and so let one act's approval authorize any other (**D-184**, clause (1) landed, clause (2) flagged), and the threshold policy's `effective_from`, which is stored, validated, pinned and signed for and compared against nothing (**D-188**, fail-open). Nine [M]: the unreachable per-currency comparison (D-183), the write-once "unset" (D-185), the precondition dropped on a premise its own 200 disproves (D-186), the `materiality` payload that stores only its token (D-187), the adjustment whose dedup key had to learn the act (D-189), the act identity that repeats under an oscillation (D-190), three window routes requiring headers none of them reads (D-191), the unguarded version mint and the unreadable version that used to abort the whole effective-policy walk (D-192), and the second-signature rule's three spellings (D-193). Four landed in code the same day (`0b8247a97`, `b65201b52`); the rest are owed. **D-184 clause (2) was decided by the product owner the same day** — a schedule's approval subject is the **act**, the window id becoming an outcome minted at the commit, and the act is rendered to the reviewer, which closes D-61's half for this surface; landed `5bcfe617f`. Everything else in this wave enforces or records what the set already declares. **Nothing in pricing awaits veto.**
and propagated into the named docs; each entry records the decision, rationale, and the

## A. Design-lock blockers

#### D-01 [H] S4 tax-completeness check has no data source

- **Where**: [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) C4 + `TaxDisplayValidator` + §6; PRD §17.4 "Tax-inclusive" row.
- **Problem**: C4 blocks publish on "`taxInclusive=true` without a region tax rate" and "`taxInclusive=false` in a region with no configured `taxCategory`" — but no table/config anywhere stores per-region tax rates or per-region `taxCategory` (Tax Engine, which owns rates, is post-MVP). The validator's key predicate has no input. Worse, under the default fail-closed policy the flagship AC ("mixed plan publishes with `not_sellable_ga` on the EU rows") is unreachable — at MVP *every* region lacks a rate, so the row would block, not flag.
- **Options**: (a) add a per-region tax config (rate-present marker + `taxCategory`) to the region-taxonomy rows (or a `pricing_policy_object` entry), name it as the C4 input, and state the ordering — C4 completeness evaluates against tenant config; a *passing* `taxInclusive=true` row still publishes flagged `not_sellable_ga` (C3); (b) drop C4's rate predicate at MVP entirely (keep only `taxCategory` completeness), reinstate when Tax Engine lands.
- **Recommendation**: (a) — keeps the PRD §17.4 predicate honest and makes the AC constructible.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-10** — as the `RegionTaxReadiness` **port**: `(tenant, region) → { taxCategory, ratePresent }`, fail-closed on unknown. MVP provider = tenant-declared columns `tax_category` + `tax_rate_present` on `pricing_region_taxonomy` (CatalogAdmin, `config × write`, audited; catches configuration mistakes — rate correctness is unverifiable pre-Tax-Engine). Post-GA provider = Tax Engine-backed (sync vs event-fed mirror + the contract land in the Tax Engine PRD); tenant markers are then reconciled — divergence flags published rows + `pricing.tax.readiness_divergent` (Warn), remediation = re-publish. Ordering: C4 readiness gate precedes the C3 `not_sellable_ga` flag. **Propagated**: S4 C4/§1.7/`algo-tax-display` (`inst-td-readiness`)/§6/§9/§10; PRD fr-tax-display-basis + §17.4 (both rows).

#### D-02 [H] Second grandfathering cutover on the same scope key is impossible

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-co-copy`/`inst-co-bounds`, W2; Foundation §4.3; PRD §17.5.
- **Problem**: the copy always lands on the single `existing_grandfathered` eligibility value. A second cutover on the same key (annual reprice, each cohort keeps its price) must schedule copy #2 overlapping copy #1's open-ended window → non-overlap violation, and every escape hatch (supersede the copy, shorten its window under `grandfatherUntil`) is explicitly forbidden. The design supports exactly one grandfathered generation per key and never says so; there is no error code for the rejected second cutover.
- **Options**: (a) make the limit normative — "at most one grandfathered generation per canonical scope key"; a cutover on a key whose grandfathered sibling holds an active/scheduled window is rejected (`CUTOVER_GRANDFATHERED_OCCUPIED`, 409) unless that row's `grandfatherUntil` is at/before the new cutover instant; (b) add a generation discriminator to the eligibility axis (multi-generation cohorts) — needs an ADR + PRD scope-key change.
- **Recommendation**: (a) for launch; (b) is a real product capability question (stacked legacy cohorts) — take it only with a product mandate.
- **Decision**: **(b) in full, laid in now — 2026-07-10** (product call: multi-cohort retention is table stakes; no N=1 launch boundary). **ADR-0002** (`cpt-cf-bss-pricing-adr-grandfathering-cohort-axis`): the canonical scope key gains the additive `cohort` axis = the cutover instant (`none` on non-grandfathered rows; publish-enforced `cohort ≠ none ⇔ existing_grandfathered`). Every cutover creates a **new coexisting generation**; prior generations untouched. Within the grandfathered class, Tariffs selects the row by the **cohort of the subscription's pinned price id** (`pricingSnapshotRef` — no new binding store); most-specific-wins stays class-only. Default-move renewal semantics preserved (the Stripe-model analysis: full per-subscription binding was rejected for inverting the renewal default; industry mapping in the ADR). Note: D-04 (window bound at `grandfatherUntil`) and D-05 (retirement vs pending cutover) now apply **per generation**. **Propagated**: ADR-0002 (new) + ADR-0001 amendment note; Foundation §1.2/§3.7/§4.1/§5; DESIGN §1.2/§2.2/§3.6/§5; PRD glossary (Price row, priceEligibility, Grandfathering, grandfatherUntil, new `cohort` entry), §2.2, fr-grandfathering-eligibility, fr-supersession, AC #8/#71 key tuples, AC #25 selection rule (2026-07-28 review fix — the AC had kept the rejected `activatedAt` mechanism), §17.4 duplicate-scope, §17.5 cutover row, §13 Tariffs row; S7 W2/W3, cutover flow/algo (`inst-co-copy`/`inst-co-atomic`/`inst-co-single-pending`), eligibility (`inst-el-fields`/`inst-el-generation`), state machine, DoD, ACs.

#### D-03 [H] Cutover atomicity across the external window SoR

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-gc-commit`/`inst-co-atomic`, W1 (windows live in the effective-dating UC; the slice holds an event-driven mirror).
- **Problem**: the design claims "shorten + two schedules commit as one transaction", but the three operations execute in another component via the UC contract — a local ACID transaction cannot span them. Partial failure (shorten committed, copy-schedule failed) opens exactly the coverage gap the atomic unit exists to prevent; no compensation, no atomic multi-op UC API, no re-validation at commit is specified.
- **Options**: (a) require the UC contract to expose an **atomic batch window operation** (shorten+schedule+schedule accepted/rejected as one) — cross-team ask; (b) define a saga: schedules first, shorten last, explicit compensation on failure, plus a gap-freeness re-check against fresh UC state inside the commit step.
- **Recommendation**: (a) if the UC team accepts (cleanest, and other batch users exist — D-28); otherwise (b) written out normatively.
- **Decision**: **(c) — consolidation, 2026-07-10.** Investigation of the source UC (`vhp-architecture/docs/bss/prd/PRD-product-catalog-marketplace-202601120119/UC-effective-dating-price-windows-202601121200.md`) showed: it targets the defunct monolithic "Catalog Service"; its content is 100% price windows (no other consumers — registry unmentioned); its normative deltas were already superseded by this PRD; the atomicity requirement was already assigned to it via the consolidate-UC open question. Given the one-gear topology (all slices = one deployable, one PostgreSQL; Tariffs separate), **window ownership moves into the pricing gear**: S7 owns the `pricing_price_window` store + state machine (`scheduled/active/expired/cancelled`), the scheduling/cancellation API, the coordination-lease activation/expiration job, and `PriceWindow*` event production (frozen manifest names). The cutover's multi-window unit becomes a **local ACID transaction** — D-03 dissolves; **D-26 dissolves** (no UC-side mutations exist; mirror + `mirror_lag`/`coverage_gap` alarms removed); D-05 simplifies (single transactional domain). Legacy-UC dispositions: FX rate-lock rejected (no FX in catalog); impact preview out of scope (needs Subscriptions data); `suspended` state not adopted. PRD §15 consolidation question **answered** (formal Architecture ack pending). **Propagated**: S7 (§1, W1, names, flow, `inst-fg-when`, new `state-price-window` + window API/codes/table/events/alarms/DoD/ACs/§10), S11 (retirement invokes S7 flow; table refs), S12 §10 (bulk window ops local), S5 (window-endpoint authz rows), Foundation (§1.2 events, §3.4/§3.5, §4.3), DESIGN/README, PRD (12 spots incl. §2.1 boundary, §9.2 contract internalized, §13 row, §15 answer), legacy UC banner (vhp-architecture). **Formalized as ADR-0003** (`cpt-cf-bss-pricing-adr-pricewindow-consolidation`).

#### D-04 [H] Coverage hole between `grandfatherUntil` expiry and renewal re-bind

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-co-bounds`/`inst-el-expiry`; PRD fr-grandfathering-eligibility.
- **Problem**: the copy's window must cover only **through** `grandfatherUntil`, but re-bind happens at the **next renewal** — a subscription renewing N days after expiry is bound to a key with no window for N days; usage/arrears rating on that key fails closed for a legitimate subscriber. Alternatively, if the intent is that an expired subscription immediately eligibility-matches the successor, its price changes mid-cycle before any re-bind — stated nowhere.
- **Options**: (a) the copy's window MUST cover `grandfatherUntil` **plus the longest billing cycle sold on that key** (tighten `inst-co-bounds` + PRD); (b) after expiry, Tariffs eligibility-matching resolves the subscription to the successor row even before the Subscriptions re-bind (re-bind is bookkeeping) — mid-cycle price change, needs product sign-off.
- **Recommendation**: (a) — no mid-cycle surprises, purely catalog-side rule.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-10** — a generation's window MUST cover `grandfatherUntil` + the **longest billing cycle sold on that key** (open-ended when null); enforced at cutover and on every `effectiveTo` adjustment. The margin keeps every bound period rateable until its renewal re-bind; it leaks nothing (new subscriptions never bind grandfathered rows). Post-D-02 the old cost (key occupancy delaying the next cutover) is gone — generations coexist. **Propagated**: S7 `inst-co-bounds` + key constraints + unit AC; PRD fr-grandfathering-eligibility.

#### D-05 [H] Retirement cancels the scheduled halves of an approved cutover
