- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.3 (new normative paragraph); [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-he-read` + `inst-he-export` + §5 + `dod-history-export`; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-au-read` + §5; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-price-history-export` + AC #40.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31d billing-domain review, M-3): no pricing document declared pagination for any collection surface — a grep over the PRD, DESIGN.md and all 12 slices returned zero hits for `paginat|cursor|$top|$skip` — while the export SLO was stated as *"p95 ≤ 5s / 100 records"*: an SLO expressed **per page with no page contract anywhere**. Affected: `GET /bss-pricing/v1/history` over ≥ 7 years of append-only rows, `GET /bss-pricing/v1/audit`, the plan and overlay lists, batch reports. Not a platform convention the gear may lean on: the sibling rating gear declares pagination in its own PRD/SEAMS, and `guidelines/` has no pagination standard.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — a Foundation API convention, inherited by every slice surface: every collection, history and audit GET returns pages — `limit` (default 100, hard cap 1,000; the SLO's own unit) + an **opaque `cursor`**, `next_cursor` until exhausted. Ordering is **stable and append-consistent**: commit/append order on history/audit (a cursor walk concurrent with writes never skips or duplicates a row at or before the cursor), deterministic key order on catalog lists. Offset/`$skip` is not offered — unstable over append-only stores at the ≥ 7y retention. Exports stream the same order in bounded chunks; the p95 ≤ 5s / 100 records SLO applies per page/chunk, now with a contract defining the page.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.3; S12 ×4 sites; S5 ×2 sites; PRD `fr-price-history-export` + AC #40.

#### D-126 [H] The first grandfathered generation is unbindable — the cohort selector resolves zero rows for the population grandfathering protects

- **Where**: [`ADR/0002`](./ADR/0002-cpt-cf-bss-pricing-adr-grandfathering-cohort-axis.md) (decision outcome + Confirmation); [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-el-bootstrap` (new) + `dod-grandfathering` + AC ×3; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.1 (`cohort` bullet).
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, H-1): ADR-0002 and `inst-el-generation` select the generation by the `cohort` of the subscription's **pinned price id**, adopted verbatim by Rating (`CohortGenerationSelector`; rating PRD §475/§549/§1312). A subscription predating a key's **first** cutover is pinned to a non-grandfathered row whose `cohort` is `none` — publish enforces `cohort ≠ none ⇔ existing_grandfathered`, so no generation can ever carry `none`. The selector therefore keeps **zero** rows, and rating's "no eligible price ⇒ evaluation MUST fail" applies. The class filter makes it worse: `existing_grandfathered` "includes only subscriptions activated before cutover" *promotes* the subscriber into a class whose generation selector then rejects every candidate, after the class order has already excluded the successor. The rule is self-consistent only from the **second** cutover onward; no rule defined the bootstrap, the snapshot is immutable so the cutover cannot re-pin, and rating's `CohortPin` contemplates *absence* (a torn-pin failure) but never the value `none`. Failure: at the first cutover on any key, every pre-cutover subscriber's next resolution fails closed — or, on a looser fall-through, silently resolves the `all_subscriptions` successor at the **new, higher** price, the exact outcome ADR-0002 exists to prevent. Note the asymmetry with D-04, which closed the *exit* hole (re-bind lagging `grandfatherUntil`); the entry hole was never examined.
- **Options**: (a) `cohort = none` ⇒ the generation whose `cohort` equals the pinned row's window `effectiveTo` (deterministic, snapshot+projection only, no new store); (b) first hop by `activatedAt` vs cutover, cohort-pin thereafter (a second input into a rule ADR-0002 deliberately built on one); (c) re-pin every affected subscription at the cutover (rejected — snapshots are immutable and the catalog holds no subscription state).
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-08-01** — when the pinned price id's `cohort` is `none`, the bound generation is the one whose `cohort` equals the **pinned row's window `effectiveTo`**, which by construction of `inst-co-shorten` is the instant of the cutover that closed that row, i.e. the cutover that grandfathered the subscriber. **If no generation carries that instant** the pinned row was closed by a supersession, not a cutover: the `existing_grandfathered` class contributes **no** candidate and resolution continues down the class order — the correct "superseded, not grandfathered" outcome, and the clause that stops an empty class from failing closed. From the first renewal after the cutover the subscription re-pins to its generation and `inst-el-generation` applies unchanged. Owed adoption: **Rating** (`CohortGenerationSelector` + `CohortPin`, a SEAMS row). **Flagged for veto · joint with Rating.**
- **Propagated**: ADR-0002 (decision bullet + Confirmation test); S7 `inst-el-bootstrap` + `dod-grandfathering` + integration AC (first cutover, second cutover, supersession-closed pin); S1 §4.1.

#### D-127 [H] The cutover successor escapes the D-82/D-98/D-122 unit guard — the guard binds the key, not the mechanism

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-tb-supersession-units` + §5 + `dod-tier-bands` + AC; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-co-successor` + `dod-grandfathering` + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3 (both mechanism bullets); [PRD](./PRD.md) §17.5.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, H-2): D-100 made the cutover the **second** sanctioned producer of `published → superseded` on the *same* canonical scope key, and the tier counter `Q` is keyed `(subscription, meter, dimensionKey, window)` — row-blind — so it continues across a cutover changeover exactly as across a supersession. But the guard was written as *"a **usage-row supersession** MUST NOT change …"* and invoked from exactly one place, `inst-su-compose`; `algo-cutover` never cited it, the successor arrives as a client-authored "successor row ref", and nothing said it sets `supersedes_price_id`, so even a generically-registered pipeline rule had no predecessor to compare against. A cutover successor flipping `per_hour → per_day` (or `graduated → volume`, or `package_size`) applies an hours-denominated continued `Q` to day-denominated bands — the ×24 class closed four times already (D-82 supersession, D-98 kind flip, D-89 phase axis, D-113 plan change), reopened through the fifth door, on the one path that is *always material* and therefore felt safe.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — the guard binds the **key**, not the mechanism: it applies to **every** usage row landing on an occupied published canonical scope key, i.e. both sanctioned producers, and **both set `supersedes_price_id` on the successor**, which is the guard's comparison referent. The rule is renamed the **succession** unit guard accordingly; `SUPERSESSION_UNIT_MISMATCH` keeps its code and gains the cutover case. The supersession-continuity fixture gains the cutover negative scenario beside the existing supersession and phase-conversion ones.
- **Propagated**: S3 `inst-tb-supersession-units` (scope clause) + §5 error text + `dod-tier-bands` + integration AC; S7 `inst-co-successor` + `dod-grandfathering` + AC; S1 §4.3 supersession and cutover bullets; PRD §17.5 normative-relationship paragraph.

#### D-128 [H] Plan retirement is a publish unit, and the projector sources the plan's current revision (`published` **or** `retired`)

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.2 (new paragraph) + §4.4 + §3.7 (`pricing_plan`); [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-event` + `dod-retirement` + AC; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-pl-retire` + `inst-pl-supersede`; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-sg-surface`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-retirement` + §17.5 increment table.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, H-3): sellability predicate (4) reads the plan's **lifecycle state**, and `inst-sg-pinned` requires all six predicates to be evaluable from the *pinned* read model — but `inst-rt-event` discharged it as *"the read model flags the plan not-sellable"*, an in-place mutation of a frozen version that D-85/D-99 forbid, and PRD §17.5's increment table listed **no** retirement class, so nothing made the fact addressable. It cannot self-heal: a retired plan can never publish again (no `retired → draft`/`published` edge; its draft revision is deleted in the same transaction), so no later publish could ever re-project it and the read model advertised a retired plan as sellable **permanently**; the D-99 window-cancel path does not cover it either, since under D-51 a plan with in-flight subscribers on every key cancels nothing. And in the other direction, had it re-projected, §4.4 sourced the projection from *"the **published** revision's own truth rows"* — after the flip there is none (D-90's own text names "the published revision" as the input of the sellability lifecycle predicate) — so the plan subject would project **empty** and break rating resolution for exactly the in-flight subscribers D-51 preserves coverage for.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — three clauses. (1) Retirement is a **publish unit** through the engine: validation → pending `CatalogVersion` ref → plan-subject re-projection → warm, exactly the D-99 treatment of window mutations. (2) The plan-revision `lifecycle_state` is a **projected plan-subject field** — that is what predicate (4) reads at the pin. (3) The projector's source is the plan's **current** revision, `published` **or `retired`**, and the partial `UNIQUE` widens to `WHERE lifecycle_state IN ('published', 'retired')` so "the current revision" keeps a referent after retirement.
- **What was already built before Slice 11, so a reader estimating the work does not double-count (added 2026-08-07 from the Slice 11 hand-back)**: the **projection** half of this decision pre-dates the slice — `PlanSubjectDelta::lifecycle_state`, sellability predicate (4), and `LifecycleState::is_current` spanning `published | retired` were all in the tree at `60e967749`. What Slice 11 added is the **act**: the flip, the event, the audit token, the reference guard, the orchestrator and the route. The entry as written reads as though the whole of D-128 were Slice 11's.
- **Propagated**: S1 §4.2 (new normative paragraph) + §4.4 (projection source + lifecycle field) + §3.7 (`pricing_plan` predicate, ×2 sites); S11 `inst-rt-event` + `dod-retirement` + integration AC; S2 `inst-pl-retire` + `inst-pl-supersede`; S7 `inst-sg-surface` predicate (4); PRD `fr-plan-retirement` + the §17.5 increment table's new retirement row. **Amended by D-165** (2026-08-03): clause (3)'s "the plan's **current** revision" is the projector's source only when the version being projected is the current one — it freezes a later publish's content into an earlier version otherwise — so the source is the revision and lifecycle state the version's **own** publish judged, pinned on the ref row. Clause (2) and the two-token state set are untouched and are what make that read safe.

#### D-129 [H] The compiled `carry` allowance grant binds the row's scope key, not its `price_id`

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) `inst-ac-carry` + §6 (`pricing_plan_grant`) + `dod-included-allowance` + AC; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-tb-supersession-units` + §5 + `dod-tier-bands` + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, H-4): `inst-ac-carry` binds issuance to the row — *"Billing issues … **only** the compiled grant whose source row is the subscription's bound `(currency, region)` row"* — but `pricing_price` is attached to `plan_id`, **not** to a revision (S2 §6 states this explicitly), so a supersession mints a new `price_id` **without** opening a plan revision, while `pricing_plan_grant` is keyed `(grant_id, plan_revision)` and is physically immutable once its revision publishes (D-106 + Foundation §3.7's extended trigger discipline). The successor's commit could therefore neither re-point nor replace the grant: after any routine reprice of an allowance-carrying row the grant references the **superseded predecessor**, Billing's source-row test stops matching, and the included allowance **silently stops being granted** — zero price delta, no validation failure, no alarm. The same block hits every allowance-carrying row of a mass-repricing run, S12 being built on supersession units.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — two clauses. (1) The compiled grant carries **`source_scope_key`** (the row's canonical scope key) as its issuance binding, matched against the key the subscription resolves on its bound market; `source_price_id` is retained as **lineage only**. The scope key is stable across supersession by construction, so the grant survives a reprice. (2) On a `carry`-allowance row, **`included_allowance` joins the succession-preserved set** (`SUPERSESSION_UNIT_MISMATCH`): its artifact is plan-scoped and revision-immutable, so changing it is structural and routes through plan revisioning. A `none`-policy allowance carries no plan-scoped artifact and stays a free row-local lever. This is the D-116 shape (bind the key family, not the id) applied to the one reference that had not received it.
- **Propagated**: S10 `inst-ac-carry` + the `pricing_plan_grant` columns (`source_scope_key` added, `source_price_id` demoted) + `dod-included-allowance` + AC; S3 `inst-tb-supersession-units` + §5 error text + `dod-tier-bands` + AC; S1 §4.3 supersession bullet.

#### D-130 [M] The allowance compile is a projection, never a write-back

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) `inst-ac-band` + `inst-ac-deterministic` + §6 + `dod-included-allowance` + AC ×2; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) Q6 + `inst-tb-first` + §6; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-cl-copy` + `dod-clone`.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-1): `inst-ac-band` rewrote the row **in place** — prepending `[0, N) @ $0`, offsetting every authored band by `+N`, and (untiered) rewriting `model_kind` to `graduated` with `amount_minor → NULL` — while retaining only the authored *kind* and the declaration. The authored **band set** was not preserved (`pricing_price_tier_band` holds one set per row), so `inst-ac-deterministic`'s *"re-publish recompiles identically"* had no stored input and every re-entry path was undefined: a supersession or repricing successor built from the published row carried already-offset bands **plus** the declaration and tripped `ALLOWANCE_DOUBLE_FREE`, **blocking the reprice of every allowance-carrying row**; dropping the declaration instead kept the price but silently lost the D-45 marker (the display and included-vs-billed reporting split D-45 exists for). Clone had the same ambiguity, and its reset list never mentioned compiled artifacts.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — the compile is a **projection**: the row's truth stays exactly as authored (declaration, kind, `amount_minor`, band rows), and the compiled artifacts — the `$0` band, the offset set, the presented `graduated` kind, the marker — are materialized into the read model / `pricingSnapshotRef` at publish and **never written back**. Publish still validates the *compiled* band set against the Slice-3 rules and fixture-gates on the compiled kind, so compile-equivalence (AC #90a) is unchanged. This supersedes D-59's "kind rewrite in the same publish transaction" **mechanism** while keeping its `volume` ban intact — no band row is ever inserted against a `per_unit` `price_id`, so the structural-exclusivity trigger D-59 was routing around is never approached. `ALLOWANCE_DOUBLE_FREE` recovers its literal meaning (an **authored** `$0` first band beside a declaration) and can no longer fire against the compiler's own output.
- **Propagated**: S10 `inst-ac-band` (rewritten) + `inst-ac-deterministic` + §6 `included_allowance` note + `dod-included-allowance` + two integration ACs (projection shape; re-entry via supersession/repricing/clone); S3 Q6 + `inst-tb-first` + the `pricing_price_tier_band` header; S12 `inst-cl-copy` + `dod-clone`.

#### D-131 [M] The D-79 lane answers per price id, in one call

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) §9.2 Subscriptions contract, inbound lane 3; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-fg-trailing`; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-cancel` + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-2): the lane returns *"the **count** of non-terminal subscriptions whose pinned `pricingSnapshotRef` references **any of** a submitted price-id set"* — one aggregate over the union — while every consumer decides **per canonical scope key**: D-51 keeps or cancels each window per key, the D-62/D-80 exemption is per key, `inst-sg-conjunction` walks keys. A union count answers only "does this plan have any subscriber at all", under which retirement cancels nothing whenever one key is occupied — D-51 inverted. The only other implementation is one call per key, i.e. **N synchronous cross-gear round trips inside an ACID transaction** (both `inst-rt-cancel` and `inst-fg-trailing` re-resolve the predicate *inside* the mutating commit), N = keys × markets, holding the price/window row locks and the audit chain segment across the fan-out.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — the lane returns a **per-price-id presence map** over the submitted set. A mutating unit makes **one** call over the union of its touched keys' price-id sets and derives per-key presence from the map; the call carries a stated timeout whose expiry is the existing fail-closed case (subscribers presumed present). **Joint with Subscriptions** (the SUB-P8 presence read, already owed — this fixes its response shape, not its trigger).
- **Propagated**: PRD §9.2 lane 3; S7 `inst-fg-trailing`; S11 `inst-rt-cancel` + a mixed-occupancy integration AC.

#### D-132 [M] The market-uniformity rules exclude `existing_grandfathered` generations

- **Where**: [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) `inst-td-basis-uniform` + `dod-tax-display` + AC; [`design/06-consumer-contracts.md`](./design/06-consumer-contracts.md) `inst-pi-uniform` + §6 + `dod-proration-inputs` + AC; [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-bc-taxbasis`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-tax-display-basis` + `fr-proration-input-contract`.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-3): D-110 (*"every published row of a plan on one `(currency, region)`"*) and D-123 (*"every published recurring row …"*) both range over the whole market row set. An `existing_grandfathered` generation is a published row on that market, is immutable in price and content, MUST NOT be superseded, and never leaves `published` (expiry is read-derived, `inst-gs-expire`). So one cutover **permanently** froze `tax_inclusive`, `billingAnchorPolicy`, `prorationBasis` and `creditOnDowngrade` on that market: every later publish failed `TAX_BASIS_MIXED_MARKET` / `PRORATION_CONTRACT_MIXED_MARKET` naming a divergent row nobody can fix. The set is otherwise consistent about this — `inst-bc-coverage`, `inst-sg-conjunction`, `inst-sg-bundle`, `inst-mp-grandfathered` and `inst-cl-resets` all carve grandfathered generations out explicitly; these two, the newest of the family, did not.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — both rules evaluate over rows with `priceEligibility ∈ {all_subscriptions, new_subscriptions_only}`. A grandfathered subscriber reads its display basis and cycle clock from its **own frozen snapshot**, so "an invoice is one document" and "a subscription is one cycle clock" hold unchanged per subscription. D-119's bundle analogue already inherits the narrower coverage set and is stated explicitly. **Flagged for veto** — the product-visible consequence is that a grandfathered cohort may keep a display basis or anchor the current rows have moved away from.
- **Propagated**: S4 `inst-td-basis-uniform` + `dod-tax-display` + AC; S6 `inst-pi-uniform` + §6 constraint note + `dod-proration-inputs` + AC; S8 `inst-bc-taxbasis`; PRD both FRs.

#### D-133 [M] `overlay_index` is sharded by scope and horizon-bounded

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §7 (D-112 paragraph) + §10 + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 (`pricing_read_model`).
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-4): D-112 re-projects, on every overlay publish unit, a **single tenant-wide** `overlay_index` row carrying the live overlay id set with each overlay's scope, interval and precedence — and read-model deltas are retained on the ≥ 7-year truth horizon. D-112's accounting ("two delta rows per commit — still O(publish units)") counted **rows, not bytes**: the row is O(live overlay count), rewritten **whole** on every commit, so storage is O(commits × overlays) and write amplification O(overlays) per commit. It is simultaneously the order-time hot object (p95 < 100 ms) with no size bound — the plan subject received the D-121 horizon for exactly this reason; the index received no analogue.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — `subject_ref = (scope_class, scope_value)` (a `global` sentinel for the classless one), and each shard carries only overlays whose interval intersects `[projection_time − H, ∞)` on the D-121 horizon. A commit rewrites exactly one shard (two when a revision moves the scope value); enumeration becomes ≤ 6 point reads — the classes a payer context can match — plus one document read per matching overlay. D-91's "one row per publish unit" property is unchanged (it remains two).
- **Propagated**: S9 §7 + §10 + the enumeration AC; S1 §3.7 `pricing_read_model`.

#### D-134 [M] A repricing run commits per plan, not per row (D-111/D-124 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-mr-apply` + `inst-mr-validate-scope` + `inst-bs-approval`/`inst-bs-commit` + `dod-mass-repricing` + §10 + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-5): D-124 runs the plan-level aggregate pass once, at entry to `committing`, *"over the plan's full row set **as it will stand post-commit**"* — a premise that only holds if the whole set actually lands. `inst-mr-apply` explicitly allows a per-row validation failure to mark that row `failed` while its siblings apply, and the journal makes partial application a first-class outcome. So the post-commit row set can differ from the set the pass evaluated, with nothing re-checking: a run that would violate per-market completeness, phase coverage or injectivity under its **actual** outcome commits anyway — the stale-verdict shape D-124 removed from the approval window, displaced into the commit window.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — the run's transaction unit is the **plan**: all of a plan's selected rows commit together, the aggregate pass runs **inside that transaction** over the state it is landing, and any per-row failure fails **every** row of that plan with the shared reason (the treatment `inst-mr-validate-scope` already gave an aggregate-pass failure) — never a partial plan. Per-row isolation is not lost where it earns its keep: it belongs to **bulk import**, whose domain is the draft plane (D-118) and whose rows are independent, while a repricing run holds the bulk lock over its rows so no concurrent editor exists for a per-row ETag to protect against. Throughput is unaffected in aggregate (the SLO is rows/sec), and the pass stays amortized O(plans) per run.
- **Propagated**: S12 `inst-mr-apply` (transaction unit) + `inst-mr-validate-scope` (timing clause) + the two state-machine transitions + `dod-mass-repricing` + §10 + a partial-failure integration AC.

#### D-135 [M] The audit hash chain is segmented per `(tenant, chain_id)` with a per-tenant roll-up

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) G4 + `inst-au-tamper` + `dod-audit` + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 (`pricing_audit_log`); [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §10.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-6): D-14 commits a hash-chained audit row **inside** the mutation transaction, and the 2026-07-31 fix scoped the chain **per tenant**. A chain is a strict sequence — row *N* needs row *N−1*'s hash — so every audited mutation of a tenant contends on one head and all authoring on that tenant serialises by construction. S12's ratified ≥ 50 rows/s repricing figure is a per-row-transaction throughput, and its per-row cost enumeration (§10) **omitted the audit row entirely** — the one write in the list that cannot proceed concurrently — while interactive authoring contends on the same head for the duration of a run.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — chains are segmented within a tenant by `chain_id` = the audited subject's aggregate (plan, overlay, payer, policy, bulk operation), with a periodic per-tenant **roll-up** row chaining the current segment heads. Tamper-evidence is preserved end to end: deleting a row breaks its segment, deleting a segment breaks the roll-up; the verification job walks both, and the roll-up head is the WORM anchor. Concurrent mutations of different aggregates no longer contend, while a repricing run's rows — one plan, one `chain_id` — extend sequentially inside that plan's own transaction anyway (D-134). The audit write joins the per-row cost enumeration and the O3 perf test.
- **Propagated**: S5 G4 + `inst-au-tamper` + `dod-audit` + the tamper AC; S1 §3.7; S12 §10 per-row cost.

#### D-136 [M] The pin-eligibility frontier is materialized and published

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.4 + §3.3 + §3.7 (`pricing_pin_frontier`); [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) AuthZ endpoint map; [PRD](./PRD.md) §9.2 Tariffs contract.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-7): D-101 + D-114 define pin-eligibility as *"committed, every subject row of that version warm-complete, **and** every earlier version itself pin-eligible"*, and the ≤ 5s pin-lag rule refers to "the newest pin-eligible version". Nothing stored it (`pricing_read_model` carries a per-row `warm_completed`; `pricing_catalog_version_ref` carries pending-vs-committed), no component owned advancing it, and **no API returned it** — yet every consumer must obtain it before the first read of a rating run, and `pricing.readmodel.pin_eligibility_overdue` must evaluate it to fire. Computed literally it is a recursive scan over every subject row of every version since the last known-good one, on a path budgeted at p95 < 100 ms. This is the D-112 defect one level up: a resolution rule with no access path — the standing "name the surface that reads it" question.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — a per-tenant watermark `pricing_pin_frontier(tenant_id, catalog_version, advanced_at)`, advanced **only forward** and **only** by the ReadModelProjector, in the same transaction that sets the last outstanding `warm_completed` of the frontier's next version in order (so D-114's prefix holds by construction rather than by re-derivation). It is the sole definition of "the newest pin-eligible version": consumers read it from `GET /bss-pricing/v1/catalog-version/frontier` (`plan × read`, service identity) and pin its value, the ≤ 5s lag rule is measured against it, and the overdue alarm fires on its age. Nothing recomputes the predicate at read time.
- **Propagated**: S1 §4.4 (new normative paragraph) + §3.3 (read-model contract) + §3.7 (new table); S5 AuthZ endpoint map; PRD §9.2 Tariffs "Compatibility". **Amended by D-164** (2026-08-03): the advance sentence taken literally strands a version completed out of order and, read over the cross-tenant `CatalogVersion` sequence, strands every frontier in a deployment — the walk and the per-tenant scope are D-164's. **Completed by D-166**: `advanced_at` is `pin_eligibility_overdue`'s referent but not its predicate.

#### D-137 [M] A bulk import is never material and pins no key (D-118 completion)

- **Where**: [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-bi-governed` + `inst-bk-approval-subset` + `inst-bs-approval`/`inst-bs-commit` + §4 states + `dod-bulk-import` + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-8): D-118 pinned bulk import to the **draft plane** ("import rows land as draft rows"), but materiality is evaluated at the submit of a *publish*, against a published baseline (`inst-ap-materiality`, `inst-mat-*`) — a draft edit produces no consumer-visible delta. So `inst-bi-governed`'s "a **material** batch routes through the Slice 5 policy before any commit", the `awaiting_approval` state, and the per-row content-hash pin were unreachable residue. The last clause was worse than dead: `inst-bk-approval-subset` had a submitted batch *"count as the pending approval unit for **every contained scope key**"*, so a draft-plane import pinned **published** keys it cannot change and bounced interactive supersessions on them with 409.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — a bulk import opens **no approval unit** and **pins no scope key**; its drafts reach consumers through the ordinary per-plan publish, which carries the full materiality and approval policy. `awaiting_approval` and the per-row hash pin are retained for **mass repricing**, whose rows are published. Phase 1's **per-row** rejection of a row whose key already holds a pending interactive unit is unchanged and still applies to imports — a held key is a poor target even for a draft.
- **Propagated**: S12 `inst-bi-governed` (rewritten) + `inst-bk-approval-subset` (scoped) + both state-machine transitions + the §4 state list + `dod-bulk-import` + an integration AC (an interactive supersession on an imported draft's key is accepted).

#### D-138 [M] `fixed` replaces the running line amount — the overlay adjustment kinds get their semantics

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `inst-plv-adjustment` + §5 (new warning) + §6 (`adjustment_kind`) + `dod-priceoverlay` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-priceoverlay-authoring`.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, M-9): pricing authors `adjustment_kind ∈ {markup, discount, fixed}`, forces `fixed` to `magnitude_kind = amount`, bounds it only by `≥ 0` (D-67's ranges cover `discount` and `markup` alone) — and defines what any of them **does** nowhere, deferring to "evaluation is Tariffs'". Rating's step-4 design enumerates exactly two line behaviours ("a percentage line scales the amount; an absolute amount line applies its published value") and never names `fixed`. In a stack-all sequential model (SEAMS O3) "set to X" and "add X" differ in result *and* under reordering, and rating's `CompositionCapGuard` (`maxCumulativeMarkup`) is defined over *cumulative markup/discount*, which a replacement has no interpretation under — so the anti-drift cap silently would not bind it. The D-01/D-113 class between gears: a field pricing publishes and a consumer must evaluate, defined by neither.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — `markup` **adds**, `discount` **subtracts**, **`fixed` replaces** the running line amount with the line's per-currency value. Replacement is forced by the rest of the model: `markup`/`discount` already express additive absolute money via `magnitude_kind = amount` (D-08), so an additive `fixed` would be a duplicate with no distinct meaning. Consequences stated normatively: a `fixed` layer discards the layers beneath it, so authoring **warns** when it is not the lowest-precedence layer able to match its target (`FIXED_LINE_DISCARDS_STACK`), and it **resets** the cumulative-markup accumulator, which then measures compounding from that layer onward. Owed adoption: **Rating** (step-4 line application + the composition cap). **Flagged for veto · joint with Rating** — the commercial reading of "fixed" is a product call.
- **Propagated**: S9 `inst-plv-adjustment` (new normative lead) + §5 warning + §6 column note + `dod-priceoverlay` + a stack-order AC; PRD `fr-priceoverlay-authoring`.

#### D-139 [M] `capacityCharge` accrues per covered granule — adopting rating T-D-25

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) `inst-rv-level` + §5; [PRD](./PRD.md) §17.7 `reservationFlavor` row; the D-53 body above (its formula gloss).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while authoring the `reserved` joint fixture): pricing states `capacityCharge = reservedRate × reservedQuantity` (D-53, 2026-07-28; S10 `inst-rv-level`; PRD §17.7). Rating **re-derived the same formula with a time factor** on 2026-08-01 — `capacityCharge = reservedRate × reservedQuantity × coveredGranules` (T-D-25, adopted and confirmed in that gear's veto round the same day) — and the pricing set carries no trace of `coveredGranules` or T-D-25. Two gears publish two different formulas for one charge. Rating's argument has no counter here: `reservedRate` is denominated in the row's **billable unit** — level unit × granule duration for level-billed rows — so `reservedRate × reservedQuantity` is money **per granule**, not a period charge. Without the multiplier the charge has no proration story at all: recurring proration (rating `design/09` §4.2) does not cover usage rows, and usage is never prorated, so a disk allocated on day 20 or resized mid-period bills as if it had been held all period. The D-01 class between gears, in the direction pricing has not yet been the one to travel.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — pricing adopts rating T-D-25 verbatim. `capacityCharge = Σ reservedRate × reservedQuantity_i × duration_i` over the reservation's covered sub-intervals within the `AnchorPeriod`, expressed in the row's billable-unit granules. The frozen field set is **unchanged**: `reservedRate`, `reservedQuantity` and `reservationFlavor` stay the catalog's and stay in `pricingSnapshotRef`; `coveredGranules` is runtime coverage, computed by Rating, and the catalog neither authors nor freezes it. So this decision changes pricing's **statement of the formula** and nothing about what it publishes. The capacity-on-level fixture scenario `inst-rv-level` already requires (D-53) is written against this formula. **Flagged for veto · joint with Rating** — "does a mid-period allocation bill pro-rata" is a commercial reading, even though the arithmetic points one way.
- **Propagated**: S10 `inst-rv-level` + §5 risks; PRD §17.7 `reservationFlavor` row; a note on the D-53 body marking its gloss superseded here.

#### D-140 [M] REST paths carry the gear service prefix — `/bss-pricing/v1/{resource}`

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.3; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `algo-authz-catalog` endpoint map; [PRD](./PRD.md) §9. Every slice §5 API surface carries the rewritten paths as cleanup applied in place.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while scaffolding the gear crate): the design set wrote every endpoint as `/v1/pricing/{resource}` — version first, gear second — and three as colon-suffixed custom methods (`…/migrations/{id}:start`, `:complete`, `…/bulk-imports/{id}:abort`). The platform route convention is `/{service-name}/v{N}/{resource}`, and it is **machine-enforced**: the workspace's `DE0801` lint denies a path whose first segment is a version (`/v1/users` is that lint's own worked "Bad" example) and denies a segment that is neither a `{param}` nor kebab-case, which `{id}:start` is not. That lint runs in the gear's phase gate and in CI, so **not one endpoint in this set was implementable as written** — the whole documented surface was unreachable, in a set otherwise reviewed six times. The sibling ledger serves `/bss-ledger/v1/…` and spells its custom methods as sub-resource segments (`/approvals/{id}/approve`).
- **Decision**: **(product owner), 2026-08-02** — the wire path is `/bss-pricing/v1/{resource}`, where `bss-pricing` is the registered gear name and the crate name, as ledger does; an action on a resource is a sub-resource segment (`{id}/start`, `{id}/complete`, `{id}/abort`). The rewrite is mechanical and one-for-one: no resource renamed, no `(resource_type, action)` pair, payload, status code, error name, idempotency rule or SLO changed. The alternative — a crate-level allow of the lint, keeping the documented notation — was rejected: it would place this gear alone outside the convention the gateway and the OpenAPI aggregation key off, to preserve a notation no consumer has yet called. Recorded as a decision rather than applied silently because the URL is **externally visible**: every §9.2 lane (the Tariffs/Rating read model, the Subscriptions migration handshake, the `migrated-origin` read, the D-136 pin frontier) is addressed by these paths, and a consumer reading this set would otherwise call a URL the gear does not serve.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.3 (the normative route-shape statement every slice inherits, beside the D-125 pagination contract); S5 `algo-authz-catalog` endpoint-map preamble; PRD §9 preamble. The path strings themselves were rewritten in place throughout the PRD, DESIGN, this register and all twelve slice designs — cleanup, not a citation surface; D-65's title, which named the two colon methods, follows the same mapping. Review documents keep the old strings as provenance.

#### D-141 [M] The price row's ETag is per row, and every draft mutation presents it

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 (`pricing_price`); [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) §5 (the `DELETE` row); [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-bk-phase2`; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-co-single-pending`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-concurrent-edit`.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3, the gear's draft-authoring plane): §3.7 lists "ETag/row-version" on the `pricing_plan` bullet and on no other, while three surfaces already require the token to be **per price row** — S3 §5's `PATCH /bss-pricing/v1/plans/{planId}/prices/{priceId}` names `ETag` in its idempotency column; `inst-bk-phase1`/`inst-bk-phase2` (D-118) edit "existing **draft** rows under **their** ETags" and fail "**only that row**" on a conflict, which is expressible only if the tag is per row; and `inst-co-single-pending` rests its whole distinction on "**ETag protects rows**, this rule protects change units". A reader building the table from §3.7 alone gives `pricing_price` no version column at all, and all three rules become unimplementable. In the same S3 table the `DELETE …/prices/{priceId}` idempotency cell is **empty**, so a draft row can be destroyed under an unknown version — the lost update `fr-concurrent-edit` closes for `PATCH`, reopened on the one verb that leaves nothing behind to reconcile.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — one token, one rule. (1) `pricing_price` carries an **ETag/row-version** column of its own, named in §3.7 beside `pricing_plan`'s; it is never derived from the plan's, because a per-row bulk conflict (D-118) means nothing if every row of a plan shares one version. (2) **Every** mutating verb on a draft price row presents it: `PATCH` (unchanged) and `DELETE` (new). A mismatch is `STALE_VERSION` (409, Foundation-owned); an absent precondition is a malformed request under the existing validation envelope. **No new code** — both failures are ones the set already names. (3) The column **freezes with the published row's content**: it joins §3.7's append-only trigger whitelist beside the price/scope/model columns, and **neither** sanctioned in-place mutation on a published row moves it — not the `lifecycle_state` flips (`published → superseded` on supersession/cutover), not the monotonic `grandfather_until` tightening. An entity tag that moved under a representation the engine forbids changing would tell a client its cached copy is stale when it is not; and the tag exists for the **draft** plane, where content really does change and clause (2)'s precondition binds, while on the published plane there is no caller-driven mutation for a precondition to guard — which is why clause (2) is scoped to draft rows in the first place. Rejected: leaving `DELETE` unconditional on the argument that a draft row is cheap to recreate. What a blind delete destroys is not the row but a concurrent editor's uncommitted work, and D-118's per-row bulk conflicts and the interactive editor meet exactly here. The scope is stated deliberately: this rule is about **`pricing_price` draft rows**. `DELETE /bss-pricing/v1/price-windows/{windowId}` (S7 §5) carries an empty idempotency cell too and is **not** moved by this decision — window cancellation is an always-material publish unit (D-62, D-99) whose concurrency is governed by `inst-co-single-pending`, and no implementation evidence has reached it.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 (`pricing_price` bullet); S3 §5 (the `DELETE` row's idempotency cell + `STALE_VERSION` in the problem-responses block); S12 `inst-bk-phase2`; S7 `inst-co-single-pending`; PRD `fr-concurrent-edit`.

#### D-142 [H] The dedup row has two states, and the idempotency TTL is evaluated at claim time

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 (`pricing_idempotency_dedup`) + §1.2 (the NFR-allocation idempotency-TTL row); [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-mutation-idempotency` + AC #100.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3): §3.7 describes the row as a request-payload hash beside the replay-response source — a hash next to a stored response. But the at-most-once gate **is** the primary-key INSERT, so the row must exist **before** the guarded operation has produced any response, and the documented shape cannot represent that instant; the only way to force it is to seed a fabricated status into a column whose meaning is "this is what the caller was told" when nobody has been told anything. The TTL is worse than incomplete: §1.2 and §3.7 give the number **24h** and nothing else — no actor forgets a key, no moment is named, no answer is specified for a request arriving past the window — so three rules had to be invented before the number meant anything. One of the three the PRD does sanction (`fr-mutation-idempotency`: "a key reused after the TTL **MAY** be a new request"); the ordering and the takeover's concurrency control it does not. With the digest compared **before** expiry the first payload to touch a key owns it forever — nothing deletes a dedup row — so the ratified 24h value is not a bound at all. Separately, tightening `record_response` to write-once (so an answer can never be overwritten) gave "no claim found" a second, reachable meaning: an already-answered claim hit again by an ordinary retry, which reaches the wire as a not-found refusal to a caller whose request both exists and succeeded.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — five clauses over one row. (1) The row has exactly **two** states: `claimed` (both response columns null) and `answered` (both set), with `(response_status IS NULL) = (response_body IS NULL)` enforced physically on both backends. The claim INSERT is the at-most-once gate and precedes the guarded operation; no fabricated response is ever seeded. (2) Expiry is evaluated **at claim time**, against the row as read. There is no reaper, and nothing in this gear deletes a dedup row — its **retention** is deliberately not decided here and is recorded as an open fork (§F.1); answering that fork cannot disturb this clause either way, since a row that has vanished is indistinguishable from a key never claimed and the INSERT path simply wins. (3) Expiry is evaluated **before** the payload-digest comparison — the reverse order makes the TTL unreachable, contradicting the ratified value being a bound. (4) A takeover of an expired row is a **compare-and-swap on the row as read**, so two racing takeovers cannot both win; the loser claimed nothing and executes nothing. (5) `record_response` is **write-once**: a second answer against an `answered` claim is neither an error nor an overwrite — it is the replay path, and it returns the **stored** response, which is precisely what `fr-mutation-idempotency` promises a retry. Rejected: a **reaper** as the expiry mechanism, which turns expiry into a background-timing property that must then race the compare-and-swap; and **seeding a synthetic `202`** at claim, which writes a value into the column meaning "what the caller was told" before any caller was told anything, so the replay path would serve a response the gear invented.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 (`pricing_idempotency_dedup` bullet, rewritten) + §1.2 (the idempotency-TTL NFR-allocation row); PRD `fr-mutation-idempotency` + AC #100.

#### D-143 [M] A duplicate arriving while the original is still in flight has its own code

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.3 + §3.7 (`pricing_idempotency_dedup`); [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-mutation-idempotency` + AC #100.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3): §3.7 promises exactly two outcomes for a duplicate — "a replay with a matching hash returns the stored response; a mismatching hash is rejected with `IDEMPOTENCY_PAYLOAD_MISMATCH`" — and a third is reachable **with no contract violation by anyone**. Two callers whose transactions hold the claim and the guarded mutation together both clear the INSERT-conflict check, both read the same expired row, and both attempt the D-142 takeover; the compare-and-swap picks one, and the loser is left holding a request against a key that is claimed and unanswered. The loser's payload may differ from the winner's, so the `IDEMPOTENCY_PAYLOAD_MISMATCH` promise does not hold on that path either. At-most-once is never violated — the loser executes nothing — but §3.3's catalogue names no code for the case, and it cannot be closed by stating the one-transaction contract normatively, because the case needs no violation to arise. The surface layer will have to answer a client and has nothing to say.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — the case is named: **`IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_IN_FLIGHT` (409)**, Foundation-owned, meaning *this key is claimed and not yet answered; retry, and you will receive either the stored response or `IDEMPOTENCY_PAYLOAD_MISMATCH`*. 409 by §3.3's own status rule — a retriable conflict on mutable state stays a conflict rather than collapsing into the 400 bucket, which is exactly the class the three existing 409s occupy. Rejected: **block-and-replay**, in which the loser waits for the winner's answer and returns it, so `fr-mutation-idempotency`'s "a duplicate returns the original outcome" holds without qualification. It is the more literal reading of the FR, and it is rejected because it holds a request open for the whole duration of an unrelated caller's transaction, needs a timeout policy this gear has nowhere to put, and converts a client-visible retry into a server-held one. Kept **out** of D-142 deliberately: it is that decision's wire consequence, but folding it in would bury a narrowed **MUST** inside a storage-shape entry. **Flagged for veto** — choosing the code narrows a stated **MUST**: `fr-mutation-idempotency` gains "once the original has completed", and that is a product reading of the gear's idempotency promise, not a technical one. **CONFIRMED as decided by the product owner 2026-08-02**, against block-and-replay: a client can act on the discrimination — retry shortly, and the answer is the stored response or `IDEMPOTENCY_PAYLOAD_MISMATCH` — and can do nothing at all with a held connection, which is what the more literal reading of the FR would give it.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.3 (the new code) + §3.7 (the dedup bullet's third outcome); PRD `fr-mutation-idempotency` + AC #100.

#### D-144 [M] Instants are UTC at millisecond resolution — the `cohort` axis inherits it

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §2.2 ("Author-driven mutation; UTC time") + §3.3 + §4.1 (`cohort`); [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) §5; [PRD](./PRD.md) §4.1 Time bullet + §17.4 + §1.4 glossary (`cohort`).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3): §4.1 defines `cohort` as "the UTC cutover instant" and states no quantization, while `cohort` is a **scope-key axis matched for equality** on the resolution path — and D-126's bootstrap case compares it against the pinned row's window `effectiveTo`, an instant produced by a different code path in a different gear. Two instants denoting the same moment at different resolutions are not equal, so an unquantized axis makes a generation unfindable by exactly the subscribers grandfathering exists to protect. The gear's whole treatment of time is one line — "all effective dating, window boundaries, `grandfatherUntil`, `availableFrom`/`availableTo`, and anchor math are **UTC**" (PRD §4.1) — which fixes the zone and leaves the resolution open. G3 renders at milliseconds because something had to be chosen.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — every instant this gear **authors, carries in a contract field, publishes or compares** is **UTC at millisecond resolution**: cutover instants and the `cohort` axis derived from them, window `effectiveFrom`/`effectiveTo`, `grandfatherUntil`, `availableFrom`/`availableTo`, and the D-88 changeover instant. An authored instant carrying finer precision **fails validation** (`TIMESTAMP_PRECISION_EXCEEDED`, 422) rather than being truncated — the money-side sibling `PRECISION_EXCEEDED` (§3.3) takes the same posture, for the same reason. Rejected: **truncate at the boundary**, which is what an unstated quantum degenerates into. Truncation silently moves the instant that a scope-key axis, a window bound and an approval-time floor are all derived from, and D-126's bootstrap equality is only safe if both sides were already at the quantum — a truncating producer and a non-truncating consumer agree until the day they do not, with no failure in between. Storage bookkeeping the operator never authors — `created_at`, audit-chain and outbox timestamps — is outside the rule: it enters no contract field and is compared with nothing. **Joint with Rating**: the already-owed D-126 cohort-bootstrap adoption gains one clause — the comparison is at this quantum. The published field set is unchanged.
- **Propagated**: S1 §2.2 + §3.3 (the new code) + §4.1 (`cohort` bullet); S7 §5 (window, cutover and changeover instants); PRD §4.1 Time bullet + §17.4 (new rule row) + §1.4 glossary `cohort`.

#### D-145 [M] `revision` is an identity, never reused — an abandoned draft consumes its number

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.2 (`DraftStateMachine`) + §3.7 (`pricing_plan`) + §4.2 + §4.3; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) the plan-lifecycle state machine + §5; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-cancel`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-retirement` (§6.8).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3): §3.7 and §4.3 say `revision` "increments monotonically", and the set treats `(plan_id, revision)` as a **durable name** — `pricing_plan_grant` is keyed `(grant_id, plan_revision)` (D-52/D-106), every revision-scoped child table copies on new revision (D-83/D-92/D-106), the plan revision row carries its own ETag, and the audit trail records the revision it mutated. But a never-published draft revision is deletable (S2's plan-lifecycle initial state; D-128 deletes a retired plan's open draft inside the retirement transaction — §4.2, S11 `inst-rt-cancel`), and after the delete `max(revision)` is what it was before, so the next opened draft mints the **same** number. `plan/2` then names two distinct rows over a plan's lifetime. Nothing observes it while the current revision never regresses — but the plan row's ETag does: a client holding the deleted `plan/2`'s row version `PATCH`es the **new** `plan/2` believing it is editing the old one, and the precondition passes — most obviously at a fresh row's initial version, which every newly minted revision carries. That is the lost update optimistic concurrency exists to refuse, arriving through the key instead of the version.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — `revision` is an **identity**, not a counter: a number minted for a plan is never minted again. The mechanism is the smallest one that keeps that true — an open draft revision is **not deleted** but flipped to a terminal `abandoned` `lifecycle_state` (its child copies are dropped; the flip is audited exactly as the deletion was), so the row survives as a tombstone and revision minting is `max(revision) + 1` over the plan's own rows, consulting nothing else. `abandoned` sits outside the partial `UNIQUE (plan_id) WHERE lifecycle_state = 'draft'`, so a new draft opens immediately **on any plan that has published at least once** — the never-published plan is the one exception, and it is the subject of the amendment below (2026-08-02, same day) — and outside `IN ('published', 'retired')`, so "the current revision" (D-90, widened by D-128) is untouched. Rejected: (a) keeping deletion and forbidding `(plan_id, revision)` as a reference — refuted by the set itself, since `pricing_plan_grant`'s primary key *is* that reference; (b) keeping deletion and sourcing the next number from the audit log, which puts an append-only forensic store on the authoring path. Consequence stated plainly: a plan's revision numbers may have **gaps** (rev 1 published, rev 2 abandoned, rev 3 published), which the Slice-12 history surface shows an operator. Not flagged for veto — the gap is presentational and the ETag hazard leaves no second technical reading. The scope is the **plan revision row**: price-row deletability is untouched, so §4.3's "only never-published `draft` rows are deletable", S3 `inst-ps-nodelete` and their DESIGN restatement keep their meaning for `pricing_price` and are **not** restated here. **Follow-through** on the plan-revision statements: §3.2's `DraftStateMachine`, the §4.2 D-128 paragraph's "its open draft revision is deleted in the same transaction", S2's plan-lifecycle initial state ("draft (mutable, deletable)"), S11 `inst-rt-cancel`'s "**deleted** in the same transaction (draft rows are deletable; the deletion is audited)" and its two cross-references (`inst-rt-event`, `inst-re-governed`), and the PRD's `fr-plan-retirement` irreversibility clause ("the plan's open draft revision is **deleted** with it" — §6.8; found by the consolidation pass, 2026-08-02, on a surface the first draft of this entry did not name) all read **abandoned** under this rule; D-128's own three clauses are unchanged. **The transition needs a caller on both arms.** Retirement's arm has one (S11 `inst-rt-cancel`); the author's did not — S2 §5's table offered no surface for discarding an open draft revision, so `inst-pl-abandon` was half an instruction. Narrowing the rule to retirement was rejected: the ETag hazard above is identical whichever actor discards the draft, and a narrowing would leave the author-discard path with neither a state nor the deletion this decision removes. S2 §5 therefore gains `POST /bss-pricing/v1/plans/{planId}/abandon` (D-140's route shape; `plan × write`, already covered by S5's `POST/PATCH …/plans*` endpoint-map row; `ETag`, since what it discards is a concurrent editor's uncommitted work — D-141's argument for the price plane's `DELETE`). Because that is a **new capability** minted by the consolidation pass rather than a restatement, it was flagged as the owner's to reverse and **put to the product owner in the 2026-08-02 veto round beside D-143 — and kept**. The alternative on the table there was the narrowing above, D-145 confined to the retirement case; it was **rejected** for the reason stated, that it leaves the authoring path with neither a deletion nor a state, so a draft an operator wanted to abandon could only be overwritten.
- **Amended 2026-08-02 (same day, by an independent review of the implementation — the claim above is false for exactly one plan)**: "a new draft opens immediately" is true of every plan that has published at least once, and of no plan that has **never** published. Revision minting has two entry points and only one of them is `max(revision) + 1`: a plan's **first** draft is minted `0` literally — there is no row to take a maximum over — while opening a **successor** presupposes a current revision to succeed from. So a plan created and abandoned before its first publish holds exactly one row, revision `0`, `abandoned`: no current revision, no open draft, and no path to either, because creating collides on the `(plan_id, revision)` primary key and opening a revision finds nothing to succeed. The plan id is **spent**. Before this decision the deletion freed revision `0` and a retry simply worked, so the state is new and the decision made it. **The state is nevertheless kept, and the refusal is made honest instead** — what is wrong today is not the state but the *answer*: the collision reaches the caller as `RepoError::Db` → `DomainError::Internal` → **500** and the open-revision arm answers **404**, an internal fault and a not-found standing where a precondition refusal belongs. Rejected: (a) **minting `max(revision) + 1` in the create path as well**, which closes the hole and costs more than it saves — a retried `POST /bss-pricing/v1/plans` naming the same id would then silently open a **second revision of an existing plan** rather than refusing, and refusing is precisely what creation idempotency rests on (`fr-mutation-idempotency`, D-142/D-143); (b) **narrowing this decision so revision `0` alone may be re-minted**, cheap in code and a straight reintroduction of the unstable reference the decision exists to remove — `plan/0` would again name two rows over a plan's lifetime, carrying the same ETag hazard on the one number every plan starts at. The trade is stated plainly: a plan id is minted server-side, so a spent id costs an operator nothing, while a silent second revision costs a great deal.
- **The refusal is named, and its owner is named with it (same amendment, 2026-08-02)**: no Foundation-owned code in §3.3 discriminates this state, so the register mints one — **`PLAN_ABANDONED_NO_SUCCESSOR` (422)**, named after the sibling it stands beside and declared in the catalogue that owns the class (S1 §3.3), referenced and never redefined by the slice that raises it (S2 §5, R-11). `PLAN_RETIRED_NO_SUCCESSOR` is the near neighbour and is refused for the same structural reason — no further revision is reachable — but it must not absorb this case: it asserts a retirement that never happened, and the two states differ in everything the operator does next, since a retired plan still holds a current revision, a resolvable read-model delta for the in-flight subscribers D-128 keeps projecting, and a clone route forward (S12), where an abandoned-only plan holds none of the three and offers nothing to clone. `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` is what the state machine answers by default, and **D-146's own line puts this refusal outside it**: that code retains the refusals with *no alternative action to describe*, while this one describes a specific and different action — the id is spent, so mint a new plan and stop retrying this one — and on the create arm it is not a state-machine transition at all but a primary-key conflict. Left on `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` a client would be told "the state machine says no" about a plan it can still work with, which is the one reading that is wrong. **422 and not 409**, by §3.3's own status rule: the five 409s are retriable conflicts on mutable state, and nothing here is retriable — `abandoned` is terminal, no edge leaves it, and no mechanism in this gear can give the plan a current revision. **Owner of the fix**: the gear has no authoring REST surface yet, so nothing raises the code today and the code below it is correct as written — the repositories are deliberately **not** changed. It belongs to the group that builds that surface, **G7 (authoring REST)** on `bss/pricing-impl`, which owes the 500 and the 404 above their honest replacement. **Paid, and the arm list corrected, 2026-08-03 (D-172).** G7 landed and raises the code on the three surfaces that can reach the state; the **create arm named above does not exist** — it presupposed a caller-supplied plan id, which §4.3's own "a plan id is minted server-side" rules out, so the primary-key collision is real in storage and reachable by no caller, and with it goes rejected alternative (a)'s *argument* (a retried create names no id, so it cannot open a silent second revision) though not its conclusion, which rests on the untouched half. `GET …/plans/{planId}` on such a plan answers **404** and is a fourth surface this paragraph never mentioned. See D-172.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.2 (`DraftStateMachine`) + §3.7 (`pricing_plan` bullet: `abandoned` joins the enumeration; both partial `UNIQUE` predicates restated) + §4.2 (the D-128 paragraph's draft clause) + §4.3 (the plan-revision paragraph); S2 §4 (the States line, the initial-state clause, and the new `draft → abandoned` transition `inst-pl-abandon`) + §5 (the rule prose **and** the `POST …/plans/{planId}/abandon` row that calls the transition); S11 `inst-rt-cancel` + `inst-rt-event` + `inst-re-governed`; PRD `fr-plan-retirement` (§6.8). **Amendment (2026-08-02)**: S1 §3.3 (`PLAN_ABANDONED_NO_SUCCESSOR` declared beside `PLAN_RETIRED_NO_SUCCESSOR`) + §3.7 (the opens-immediately clause qualified) + §4.3 (the never-published exception, the code, and the owner of the fix); S2 §4 `inst-pl-abandon` (the same clause) + §5 (the code referenced, and the surface layer that owes it).

#### D-146 [M] The two refusals `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` was hiding get their own codes

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.3 (`LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN`) + §4.3; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-pl-norollback` + §5; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-api`.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3, and again in the independent audit of the squashed group): `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` was named in §3.3 on 2026-08-02 for the transitions §4.3 refuses, and the authoring plane immediately collapsed four distinct refusals onto it. Two of them a consumer must be able to tell apart without parsing prose, because the operator's next action differs: "this plan is retired and takes no successor revision" is a **stop** — a retired plan can never publish again, so any successor is unpublishable by construction — while "you already have an open draft at revision N" is a **different and available action**: go and edit it. The second is not a state-machine transition at all. It is a uniqueness conflict on the `(plan_id) WHERE lifecycle_state = 'draft'` partial `UNIQUE`, so even the 422 category was a compromise.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — two Foundation-owned codes, both **narrowings** of `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` rather than additions beside it, so no refusal ends up with two codes. (1) **`PLAN_RETIRED_NO_SUCCESSOR` (422)** — opening a revision on, or re-publishing, a retired plan; it **replaces** the "re-publishing a retired plan" clause of the `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` gloss. (2) **`OPEN_DRAFT_REVISION_EXISTS` (409, naming the open revision)** — a second draft on a plan that already holds one; 409 because it is a uniqueness conflict on mutable state, the `DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY`/`PENDING_CHANGE_UNIT_EXISTS` class, and 409 is what §3.3's status rule keeps out of the 400 bucket. `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` retains mutating a published row's content and superseding a grandfathered row — refusals with no alternative action to describe, which is the line. Rejected: giving **every** internal refusal its own code. The read model's forward-only pin frontier (D-136) is the case that draws the line: it is advanced by the ReadModelProjector and is unreachable by a caller, so a regression there is an internal fault, not a lifecycle refusal, and a wire code for it would document an API a client cannot provoke. Both codes stay Foundation-owned — S2 and S11 reference them and neither re-declares them, per the §3.3 rule that slices reference and never redefine.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.3 (the two new codes + the narrowed `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` gloss) + §4.3; S2 `inst-pl-norollback` + §5 (referenced, not re-declared); S11 `inst-rt-api`.

#### D-147 [M] `grandfatherUntil` is a grandfathered-row field

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.1; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-el-fields` + the grandfathering state machine + §5; [PRD](./PRD.md) §1.4 glossary (`Price row`, `grandfatherUntil`) + §6.5 `fr-grandfathering-eligibility` + §17.4.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3): the pairing — `grandfatherUntil` set implies `priceEligibility = existing_grandfathered` — is enforced physically by a column check and stated in no rule. §4.3's only-permitted-mutation clause, `inst-gs-bound`/`inst-gs-tighten` and `inst-cl-resets` (which resets the two together) all read as if it held, but `inst-el-fields` publishes `grandfatherUntil` "per row" with no qualification and the PRD glossary lists it among a **Price row**'s optional fields. So the constraint had no rule and therefore no code: its violation reached the caller as an internal fault — a 500 for a request the operator could have reshaped.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — the pairing is normative and one-directional: `grandfatherUntil` is non-null **only** on a row whose `priceEligibility = existing_grandfathered`; a value anywhere else fails publish (**`GRANDFATHER_UNTIL_FORBIDDEN`**, 422), declared in S7 §5 beside the slice's other grandfathering refusals, since S7 owns the eligibility machinery. It is the `cohort` rule's sibling — §4.1 already enforces `cohort ≠ none ⇔ existing_grandfathered` (`COHORT_ELIGIBILITY_MISMATCH`) — and it takes the same shape: one axis-conditioned field, one code. Rejected: reading `grandfatherUntil` as a **general per-row availability bound**, which `inst-el-fields`' unqualified wording admits. That reading is rejected because the set already carries two mechanisms for "this row stops being sellable at `T`" — the window's `effectiveTo` and the plan's `available_to` — and a third that only the eligibility machinery derives a signal from (`inst-gs-expire`'s read-derived `EligibilityExpirySignal`, whose entire meaning is "re-bind at the next renewal") would give one fact three unreconciled homes. The converse is deliberately **not** stated: a grandfathered row with a null `grandfatherUntil` is indefinite, which the glossary already says.
- **Propagated**: S1 §4.1; S7 `inst-el-fields` + the grandfathering state machine + §5 (the new code declared); PRD §1.4 glossary (`Price row` + `grandfatherUntil` rows) + §6.5 `fr-grandfathering-eligibility` + §17.4 (the new **Grandfathering horizon** rule row).

#### D-148 [M] Scope-key uniqueness is guarded on the draft plane too

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 (`pricing_price` partial `UNIQUE`); [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) §6 key constraints; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-bk-phase1`.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-02 while building G3): §3.7 specifies **exactly one** partial `UNIQUE` on the canonical scope key, gives its predicate (`WHERE lifecycle_state = 'published'`), and argues at length that this is the only expressible form — an argument entirely about the *published* plane. Nothing covers the draft plane, while D-21 puts scope-key duplication in the **save-time** row-local set: a check decided by a read. Two concurrent creators on one key both read "absent", both insert, and the duplicate goes undiscovered until one of them publishes — at which point the operator is told, correctly and uselessly, that a row they authored days ago collides with one they cannot see. The whole point of D-21's save-time placement is that the author learns immediately.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-02** — a **second** partial `UNIQUE` over the same eight scope-key columns, predicated `WHERE lifecycle_state = 'draft'`, guards the draft plane. The two indexes are independent by construction: their predicates are disjoint, so a draft successor legitimately coexists with the published row it will supersede, and §3.7's only-expressible-form argument is untouched — it was an argument about which rows the *published* index can see, never an argument against a second predicate. A violation renders as the existing Foundation-owned **`DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY` (409)** — **no new code**; D-21's save-time check becomes the fast, explanatory path and the index becomes the guarantee, which is exactly the read-then-index arrangement the published plane already has. Rejected: (a) leaving the check read-only and relying on publish, which is D-21 reversed and defers a collision past the point where its author can attribute it; (b) widening the existing index to `IN ('draft', 'published')`, which would make a draft successor collide with the very row it supersedes. Consequence for bulk import (D-118, draft plane, per-row commits): a batch carrying two rows on one key now fails the second **on the index** rather than admitting both, and `inst-bk-phase1`'s per-row report gains that case.
- **Verified against the staged-composition paths (2026-08-02, same day)**: the entry's disjointness argument answers only the *published* predecessor, and three of this wave's own passes flagged what it leaves open — whether the D-88 supersession unit or the D-100 cutover can put a **second draft** on a key that already holds one — as an assumption nobody had traced. It has now been traced, and it is **not reachable**, on four documented interlocks. (1) **A supersession never composes on an unoccupied key**: `inst-su-compose`'s step (b) shortens *"the predecessor window's `effectiveTo`"*, and *"a key with no active/open window covering that instant (dormant; coverage already ended) **fails compose**, the unit presupposing current coverage"* — so the composed successor's only same-key neighbour is a `published` predecessor, which the predicate does not see. (2) **The key cannot simultaneously hold a hand-authored draft**, because it holds a published row: S3's authoring flow refuses *"Duplicate active scope key without supersession → `DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY` (409)"*, D-21 having put scope-key duplication in the save-time row-local set (`inst-pr-return`), and the bulk plane refuses the same shape from its own side — D-118: *"A row addressing a **published** row's key with changed content fails Phase 1 per-row (`IMPORT_TARGETS_PUBLISHED`)"*. (3) **No second unit can compose on a held key**: `inst-co-single-pending` returns 409 `PENDING_CHANGE_UNIT_EXISTS` to a second submit touching it, and the two bulk mechanisms fail per-row against it rather than racing — `inst-bk-phase1` (*"A row whose canonical scope key holds a pending interactive approval unit (supersession/cutover) fails Phase 1 per-row, naming the pending unit"*) and `inst-mp-pending` (D-35). (4) **The cutover's two staged rows are not draft rows at all, and do not share a key.** [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) §6 states the unit is *"**not a table**: an approval-unit composition over existing rows/windows, recorded in `pricing_approval` (Slice 5) with the three-operation payload"*, and D-100 has the commit flip the predecessor *"before **inserting** the successor"* — the row enters `pricing_price` inside the ACID commit, on the published plane, which is why the collision D-100 fixes was against the *published* index. Their keys differ regardless: `inst-co-copy` lands the `existing_grandfathered` copy *"on `cohort` = the cutover instant, i.e. a **new generation key**"* — rejecting a duplicate generation at compose (`DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY`) — while `inst-co-successor` lands the `all_subscriptions` successor *"on the **predecessor's own canonical scope key** (only the copy moves)"*. The same holds for a repricing run, whose per-row supersessions commit *"the new rows … in one transaction"* (S12 `inst-mr-apply`). The index therefore refuses exactly the state D-21's save-time read cannot: two concurrent hand-authored drafts on a key no published row occupies. **The decision therefore needs no narrowing, and the index as built matches it.** Recorded here rather than left implicit because an assumption that has been checked and written down stops being one — and because the four interlocks are what a later wave would have to break before this index becomes wrong.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 (`pricing_price` partial-`UNIQUE` paragraph); S3 §6 key constraints; S12 `inst-bk-phase1`.

#### D-149 [M] The cycle-shape step's four codeless requirements — and the undeclared cycle that made all of them vacuous

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §3 `inst-cs-declared` (new) + `inst-cs-onetime` + `inst-cs-recurring` + `inst-cmp-addons`, §5, §6 (`pricing_plan`), §8 `dod-billing-cycles` + `dod-composition`, §9; [PRD](./PRD.md) §17.1 (the one-time and recurring rows) + §17.3 + `fr-billing-cycles` + `fr-addon-rules`.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-03 while building G4, the gear's plan-shape validators): §3 states the cycle matrix rule by rule and §5's problem-response list names a code for most of them. **Four it does not name at all** — the *at least one* half of "exactly one `one_time` base row per sold `(currency, region)`"; "recurring-only add-ons rejected"; "≥ 1 recurring base row per sold `(currency, region)`"; "frequency metadata present" — so the pipeline could not report any of them, and Global Constraint 3 correctly stopped the implementers minting a code or stretching a neighbour: `HYBRID_INCOMPLETE` means a hybrid missing a whole **part**, `USAGE_MARKET_INCOMPLETE` is D-84's rule over **usage lines**, and widening either to say "this market has no base row" would have given one code two meanings on the axis a consumer reads. The consequence is worth stating on its own: **`inst-cs-recurring` ended up registering no rule whatsoever** — two of its three requirements are in that list and the third, `billingTiming`, is Slice 6's single-owner rule cross-referenced here — so an instruction the set treats as normative had no enforcement anywhere. The *at most one* half of the one-time rule was never at risk: `chargeKind` is a scope-key axis (§4.1), so the published-plane partial `UNIQUE` has always carried it. **A fifth gap, found from the other side and the reason the four matter:** nothing requires a plan to have chosen a `billing_cycle` at all. The column is nullable because a plan is authored incrementally in `draft` (§4.2 step 1), **every** rule of this step is conditioned on the cycle, and a rule that reads no cycle correctly declines to judge rather than reporting a fault the author did not make — so a plan carrying a `NULL` cycle passed the entire cycle-shape step **vacuously** and reached publish unjudged. The step's own coverage was the thing nobody checked.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-03** — §5 owes a code to every rule §3 states, and the four are one call about what that list owes rather than four separate ones. Three clauses. (1) **`BASE_MARKET_INCOMPLETE` (422**, cycle, `chargeKind` and market named**)** carries **both** cardinality rules — the one-time and the recurring — because they are one rule over two `chargeKind` values: a sold market with no base row of the kind the cycle mandates. It is the base-side sibling of `USAGE_MARKET_INCOMPLETE` and takes its shape deliberately; `HYBRID_INCOMPLETE` keeps its own meaning, a part missing **anywhere**, and never names a market, so the two never contest a case. (2) **`CYCLE_METADATA_MISSING` (422**, the field named**)** carries the absent `frequency` **and** the absent `billing_cycle`, which is also where the missing rule is stated (`inst-cs-declared`, evaluated before every other step). One code for two fields, by D-146's own line: a code discriminates when the operator's next action differs, and here it does not — author the field the plan is missing, which the report names. (3) The **recurring-only add-on** moves to `inst-cmp-addons` under the existing **`ADDON_INCOMPATIBLE`**, with the term it never had: an add-on SKU **none** of whose published plans carries a `one_time` base row. It was unenforceable where it stood for a reason no code would have fixed — the cycle-shape step reads the plan, while deciding this needs the *add-on SKU's own plans*, the registry read the composition step already makes for the published-and-compatible check; so it joins that step's registry-dependent set and is unbuildable until the registry read model is wired, which is honest rather than deferred. Rejected: minting a fourth code for it. `ADDON_CYCLE` already means **dependency cycle** in this catalogue, and a second sense of "cycle" on one add-on object is the one collision no consumer could parse — while `ADDON_INCOMPATIBLE`'s gloss ("published + compatible with the base SKU") widens to "and sellable under the base plan's billing cycle" without straining. Also rejected: **one** code over all five faults, which would have made the report's code column carry no information at the exact place an author reads it. Not flagged for veto — every clause names a refusal the documents already required, and the widening in (3) is a placement, not a new product rule.
- **Owed back to the implementation** (`bss/pricing-impl`): three rules the gear does not have — the two `BASE_MARKET_INCOMPLETE` arms and `CYCLE_METADATA_MISSING`'s two fields — plus, when the registry read model lands, `ADDON_INCOMPATIBLE`'s cycle arm. G4 built and reported the gap rather than minting vocabulary, which is why this entry exists.
- **Propagated**: S2 §3 (`inst-cs-declared` — new — plus `inst-cs-onetime`, `inst-cs-recurring`, `inst-cmp-addons`) + §5 (the two new codes + the widened `ADDON_INCOMPATIBLE` gloss) + §6 (`billing_cycle` nullable in draft, required at publish) + §8 (`dod-billing-cycles`, `dod-composition`) + §9; PRD §17.1 (the one-time and recurring rows) + §17.3 (add-on compatibility) + `fr-billing-cycles` + `fr-addon-rules`.

#### D-150 [M] An add-on rule's quantity bounds are a rule with a code, not three column checks

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §3 `inst-cmp-addons`, §5, §6 key constraints, §8 `dod-composition`, §9; [PRD](./PRD.md) §17.3 + `fr-addon-rules`.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-03 while building `pricing_plan_addon_rule`): §3 states "a required add-on has `maxQty ≥ 1`" and §5 names no code for it, so §6's `max_qty >= 1 WHERE required` CHECK was its only enforcement — and a physical constraint answers an author with a **driver error**, which truncates the report at the first fault and names a constraint instead of a rule, in a slice whose whole contract is "the 422 report enumerates **all** violations" (§9 API). That is the same asymmetry the injectivity restatement was written to remove.
