  - **The pure half is built** (`b4edfc0ff`, `31ad543e2`, `04d98d3f8`) as `domain::supersession`. `check_changeover_instant` is `inst-su-instant`'s two floors — the moment is an argument rather than two similarly-named functions, so a caller cannot pick the lenient one at the strict moment — and it mints `SUPERSESSION_INSTANT_PASSED`, which the design set declared and the gear had nowhere. `compose_windows` is `inst-su-compose`'s window half, refusing **dormancy** and **collision** because those are precisely the two refusals the instruction promises a *committed* unit can never produce. `plan_supersession` is the whole compose-time judgement, called at both of the instruction's moments, and it is what finally gives the D-82/D-98/D-122/D-127/D-129 unit guard a caller — the guard has been built, pure and correct since before this phase with **no production caller at all**.
  - **Two clauses this entry did not state, decided while building and pinned by tests.** (1) **The successor's window is open-ended even where its predecessor's was not.** A predecessor's `effectiveTo` was a fact about the *old price's* planned end, and inheriting it plants a trailing void at an instant nobody chose in this act; if coverage should end, that is a `PATCH` on the successor's window with its own materiality. (2) **The plan carries the changeover instant, not the composed intervals**, because `inst-su-commit` applies both window operations at commit — so compose writes nothing on the plane, and the commit re-derives the intervals against the plane as it then stands. A payload carrying only intervals would leave the commit re-validating numbers it could not re-derive.
  - **The three refusals are ordered, and the order is a decision** the instruction leaves open: the instant, then the plane, then the content. The instant is the only one wrong about the *request* rather than about what the request would do, and its remedy (recompose) is what produces a request the other two can be asked of; the plane decides whether a supersession is the right *operation* at all, a dormant key's remedy being an entirely different act; the content guard presupposes there is a supersession to do. The reverse order has a real argument — the guard's violations are fixable in the caller's own payload while the other two send them elsewhere — and it loses because validating the content of an act that must not be attempted tells someone to correct a payload they should not send. Both choices are pinned by cases staging several faults at once.
  - **`OCCUPYING_STATES` moved from `window_repo` into `domain::window`**, not copied: compose needs the same set `refuse_overlap` uses and DE0301 forbids the domain reaching into infra, while the set's whole justification is a chain of domain facts. A copy is how two mechanisms come to disagree about one set — the drift the unit guard's shared field list exists to prevent.
- **D-183's obligation rides clause (2)** and is **not** paid: the candidate set being `[published, draft]` with `published_baseline` on the other side is what gives `thresholdReached` its first authored subject, and reaching it needs the unit, not just the door. The end-to-end test is owed by the group that finishes it; `materiality_tests`' headline case is still a unit test over a hand-built input and now says so.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 (the two-door reading of the D-148 argument); S7 `inst-su-compose` (the door's precondition) + `inst-su-commit` (the ordering clause); S3 §2 `inst-ps-supersede` (the authoring-door note gains its other half — which door does author the successor).

#### D-196 [H] A plan may price several meters in one market by decision and cannot store them — the canonical scope key has no meter axis

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 (`pricing_price`, the canonical scope-key columns and `uq_pricing_price_scope_key_current`) + §4.1; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-cs-usage` + the meter-injectivity rule; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) §2. **Flagged for the owner: it cannot be closed here.**
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-05 by an agent dispatched to verify unrelated carry-forward items, and **measured by the controller rather than inferred**): **D-103** is normative and was **CONFIRMED by the product owner in the 2026-07-31 veto round** — *"a PaaS plan pricing cloudlets, storage and egress is one plan, not three"* — and the canonical scope key carries eight axes with **no `meter` and no `dimensionKey`**. Every usage line of one plan in one `(currency, region, phase, priceEligibility, cohort)` therefore renders **one key**, because they share `chargeKind = 'usage'`. The second line is refused at authoring.
  - **The measurement.** Two `graduated` usage drafts on one key differing only in `meter` (`cloudlets`, `egress_gb`): the second answers `DuplicateScopeKey("…|USD|EU|base|…|all_subscriptions|usage|none is held by draft price …")`. Not a race, not a Postgres nuance — the save-time check refuses it on the first attempt.
  - **Three sites bless the shape the store refuses.** The meter-line index deliberately **excludes** `charge_kind` so that several meters may coexist; `MeterInjectivity` in the publish pipeline permits it; and `plan_rules/cycle_shape_tests.rs`'s `a_usage_only_plan_pricing_two_meters_owes_every_market_for_each_of_them` **asserts** that a plan with `cloudlets` and `egress_gb` both on `("eur", "EU", terminal)` publishes — a domain test over a subject the store cannot hold.
  - **The crate already worked around it once, in a comment, without recording it.** `tests/postgres_schema_price.rs` says of its own fixture: *"The two rows differ in `charge_kind`, which is **out** of this index and **in** the scope-key one … A test that left the charge kinds equal **would have been refused by `uq_pricing_price_scope_key_current`**"*, and the accepting-side case gives its second `egress` row a different `region` so it does not collide. So the collision was known at the point of writing that test and reached no register entry. **No repository test authors two same-market usage lines**, which is why every suite is green.
- **Decision**: **(a), taken by the product owner 2026-08-06.** The canonical scope key is extended with **`(meter, dimensionKey)`, conditionally, on `chargeKind = 'usage'` rows**. D-103 stands as confirmed, its worked example becomes storable, and the three sites that already blessed the shape — the meter-line index, the publish pipeline's `MeterInjectivity`, and `cycle_shape_tests`' two-meter case — stop disagreeing with the store. The rejected **(b)** — one meter per market per plan — would have narrowed a decision the owner confirmed per-item, and its own worked example with it.
  - **The axis set is now conditional, which is genuinely a new shape here**, and the gear already owns the pattern to express it: `check_cohort_eligibility`'s biconditional. The pairing is a **checked function** rather than a type-level guarantee for that rule's exact reason — the axes are separate columns read back as independent values, so the pairing must be re-established on every rehydration and not only at first construction. The rule to check is one-directional rather than a biconditional: `meter` or `dimensionKey` set **implies** `chargeKind = 'usage'`. The converse does not hold and must not be asserted — a usage row with no meter is representable today and no rule in this gear refuses one (the meter/usage-type binding, `inst-cmp-usagetype`, is registry-dependent and deferred), so making the implication a biconditional would refuse rows the authoring plane admits.
  - **The rendering keeps fixed arity: ten segments, always, with `none` for both axes on a non-usage row.** A variable-arity rendering would be a parsing hazard on a string this gear embeds in three places it cannot afford to get wrong — the `DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY` message, the approval register's held-key rows, and `unit_request_id`, the cross-tenant registry idempotency key (D-202(3)). `none` is chosen over an empty segment for the reason `Cohort::None` already renders `none`: an empty segment between two separators is indistinguishable from a rendering bug.
  - **The index mechanism, and it is the part that would have gone wrong silently.** `meter` is **nullable** (`dimension_key` is already `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`), and in a `UNIQUE` index NULLs are **distinct** — so adding the column to the two scope-key partial `UNIQUE`s as written would not merely fail to constrain usage rows, it would **lose the uniqueness the indexes have today on every non-usage key**, since every one of those rows carries `meter IS NULL`. **Measured, not reasoned** (2026-08-06, SQLite): a naive `UNIQUE (a, meter, dim)` admitted **two** rows on one key with `meter` NULL on both. The mechanism is therefore an **expression index over a sentinel** — `COALESCE(meter, '')` beside `dimension_key` — which the same measurement showed refusing the second row while still admitting `cloudlets` and `egress_gb` as two keys. It keeps **one** index per plane rather than splitting each by charge kind, and it leaves the one place a NULL `meter` is meaningful untouched: `uq_pricing_price_meter_line_current`'s `WHERE meter IS NOT NULL` conjunct, which is what makes "not a metered line" different from "a line with no meter". The empty string is safe as a sentinel only because the `Meter` type refuses a blank value the way `Region` does — that refusal is part of this item, not an assumption about it.
  - **The meter-line index is not subsumed and stays.** It deliberately excludes `charge_kind` (D-43/D-103's per-line reading), so it forbids one `(meter, dimensionKey)` line being priced twice across *different* charge kinds — which the scope key, now carrying `charge_kind`, still permits. The two overlap without either implying the other.
- **Owed back to the implementation** (`bss/pricing-impl`): **all four clauses paid 2026-08-06**, with one named coverage gap inside clause (4) — a publish-through-the-engine case for two usage lines of one market. The entry is too large for one commit and is paid in clauses, each naming which: (1) **paid** — the `Meter` / `DimensionKey` types with the blank refusal, the two key fields, the implication check and its code, and the ten-segment rendering. **It amended §4.1 while being built**: the rule as stated admitted a `dimensionKey` with no `meter` on a usage row, which names nothing and would give one meterless usage line two keys, so the check refuses it and §4.1 now says so. Two shapes were confirmed by probe rather than assumed — `with_usage_line` not storing the meter reddens the two-lines-are-two-keys case (so the axis really is in the key's identity and not only in its rendering), and a rendering back at eight segments reddens three. The first attempt at that second probe was a **broken probe**, not a finding: dropping the two format placeholders without dropping their arguments does not compile, so nothing ran, and reading *that* rather than the empty failure list is what told them apart; (2) **paid** — `m20260802_000023`, both engines, the two scope-key partial `UNIQUE`s dropped and recreated over the `COALESCE` sentinel, with the Postgres `--ignored` proof that the NULL-distinctness hole is closed on *that* engine rather than inherited from the SQLite measurement. **The probe is the evidence worth keeping**: replacing the sentinel with the bare nullable column reddens **four** Postgres cases, and two of them — `two_published_rows_on_one_scope_key_cannot_coexist` and `two_draft_rows_on_one_scope_key_cannot_coexist` — **predate D-196 entirely**. That is the predicted destruction of existing uniqueness, demonstrated on the production engine rather than argued from the standard. Dropping `dimension_key` from the key reddens the dimensioned case alone, so both new axes carry their own weight. **One cost, recorded rather than absorbed:** an expression index changes what SQLite says on a violation — `UNIQUE constraint failed: t.a, t.b` becomes `UNIQUE constraint failed: index 'ix'` (measured on both forms) — so `sqlite_price_repo`'s duplicate case can no longer assert the axis list and asserts the index name, which is what both engines now have in common. A reader who wants to know which axes the guarantee covers reads the migration, not the error. The `uq_pricing_price_meter_line_current` index is untouched and unsubsumed; (3) **paid** — the callers that build keys. `price_repo::to_scope_key` attaches the pair from the same columns the indexes read, `scope_key_filter` gains both axes, and the authoring door derives the key's line from the content (`resolve_authored_usage_line`) because `ScopeKeyRequest` has no member that could carry it. **It found two defects, neither of which existed before the pair became axes.** (i) `update_draft` rewrote `meter` and `dimension_key` as ordinary content columns, so a `PATCH` moved a draft onto a **different canonical scope key with no occupancy check on the path** — the only thing that would have noticed is the partial `UNIQUE`, arriving as a driver error rather than a refusal; the door now refuses a line move and names the remedy this key already names for every other axis. (ii) the wire's key-immutability check compared the named key against the stored one raw, which after clause (3) refuses **every** `PATCH` echoing its own key on a metered row — a caller told their key moved by naming the key they are on; the comparison now carries the stored line onto the named key first, so it ranges over the axes the wire can express. **The probe that matters**: spelling the filter's absent-meter arm as `eq(None)` rather than `is_null` reddens **eleven** cases, the whole supersession door among them — the index-level NULL trap repeating one layer up, and now pinned. **The read side is paid too**: `domain::projection`'s `scopeKey` object carries `meter` and `dimensionKey`, and `read_model_repo::read_scope_key` reads them back — as `null`-or-absent rather than the rendering's `none` sentinel, because a JSON member needs no fixed arity and only the embedded *rendering* does. A consumer resolving a metered plan needs the line there or it cannot tell one published usage row of a market from another, which before this decision could not arise. Pinned by extending the existing round-trip corpus with a third, metered key rather than by a new case — the pin compares **every** projected key, so a line-less corpus left the two axes asserted by nothing and the projector and the reader could have dropped them in step with the file green; the probe confirms it (silencing the projector reddens that pin alone); (4) **paid, with one proof named as still owed** — `cycle_shape_tests`' two-meter case carries the amendment that matters: it asserted publishability for a shape no plan could reach, and it no longer does, with `sqlite_price_repo::two_usage_lines_of_one_market_both_author` as the same pair going through the door it used to bounce off. **Owed**: a publish-through-the-engine case for two usage lines of one market. Authoring is proven at the repository, both indexes admit the pair (clause 2), the publish pipeline's own `MeterInjectivity` rule always permitted it, and a metered row publishing at all is proven by `sqlite_supersession_unit`'s fixture — so what is missing is the composition of facts each of which is pinned, which is a coverage gap rather than an open question.
- **What was safe while it was owed, kept as the record of it.** Before the clauses landed nothing published was wrong, because the store refused the shape at authoring rather than storing an incoherent row; `cycle_shape_tests`' two-meter case was a unit test over a subject the authoring door refused, and nothing was to be built presuming multi-meter storage. **All of that is discharged**: the door admits the pair, both indexes hold it, the loaded key carries it and the delta publishes it.
- **Not flagged for veto.** The owner took it directly, and it narrows nothing — it widens the key so that a confirmed decision becomes reachable.
- **What the second run reads.** A second authoring attempt on a usage line now reads a key that carries the meter, so the `DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY` it may answer is about the *same line* rather than about a sibling meter — which is the whole defect this entry opened on.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 (the scope-key columns, the two partial `UNIQUE`s and the sentinel they index over); S1 §4.1 (the normative axis list and the conditional pair); S2 `inst-cs-usage` (the per-line reading, now storable).

#### D-197 [M] The advisory-warning channel has no wire, and the doc that says it does is the only thing carrying it

- **Where**: nowhere yet, deliberately. The two codes are already declared where they belong (`01-foundation.md` §3.3's `PLAN_SIZE_SOFT_CAP_EXCEEDED`, S3's `TIER_BAND_PRICE_INCREASE`) and the rules already produce them; what is missing is a **response field**, so there is no design-set text this entry changes and no citation it may claim. The `Propagated` line below says so rather than naming a document that would then not cite it — which is the P1 finding this entry would otherwise be.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-05, same dispatch): D-160 made the §14 soft caps **advisory** rather than blocking, on the express argument that a ratified `SHOULD` enforced as a `MUST` is a plan an operator could author yesterday and cannot publish today. The advisory rides `warnings[]` on the validation report, and `is_publishable()` correctly does not read it. **Nothing renders it.** `grep warnings` across `api/` returns nothing: all three publish success bodies carry `{ outcome, materiality, approval, receipt }`, and the pre-check's report is used only to build the *rejecting* envelope. So `PLAN_SIZE_SOFT_CAP_EXCEEDED` and `TIER_BAND_PRICE_INCREASE` are computed and discarded, and D-160's whole point — the ratified `SHOULD` finally having a reporting channel — is unbuilt at the surface.
  - **And `infra::error_mapping`'s doc asserts the opposite**: *"a warning must reach the author on the succeeding path too — **it rides the validation report on the response body**, not the error."* That is a false claim about this crate, and it is the reason the gap survived: a reader checking whether warnings reach anybody finds a sentence saying they do.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-05, the false claim only.** The comment is corrected to say the channel does not exist and to name what owes it. **The channel itself is not built here**: it is a response-envelope change on three success paths plus a field in the view type, and the group that owns the publish surface should land it with the DTO change rather than have it arrive as a side effect of a supersession wave. Recorded as owed rather than done, which is the distinction the entry exists to make.
- **No propagation, and the field is omitted rather than emptied.** The codes are already declared where they belong and the rules already produce them; what is owed is a *response field*, so there is no design-set text this entry changes. The `Propagated` field is left off entirely: spec-check's P1 resolves that field against document paths, so prose in it reads as a claim it cannot verify — which is itself a finding. An entry with nothing to propagate says so in a sentence like this one instead.

#### D-198 [M] The TOCTOU void reaches `plan_revision` units only, so an authoring edit orphans a window or supersession unit instead of voiding it

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) §4 `inst-ap-pin` / `inst-as-void` (the void's subject scope).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 while building D-88's orchestrator, and found by a **probe that reddened the wrong test**): `inst-ap-pin` is stated as *"a mutation of the pinned subject voids the record"*, and the implementation voids by plan prefix over `subject_kind = 'plan_revision'`. Three kinds of unit pin the **plan shape** — a plan-revision unit, a D-62/D-99 window unit, and now D-88's supersession unit — because `content_hash` is taken over the assembled shape in all three cases. So an authoring edit to any price row of the plan invalidates all three pins and voids exactly one of them.
  - **It fails closed, and that is why nothing noticed.** The other two are caught at their own commit: `authorizing_unit` compares the pin against the shape the mutating transaction assembled, so a stale unit authorizes nothing. What survives is a **record**: a `submitted` unit whose pin can never match again, sitting in the reviewer's queue asking for a decision that cannot lead to an act, and re-deriving to `APPROVAL_CONTENT_MISMATCH` for the rest of its life. That is precisely the orphan condition the publish commit's own `void_pending_for_subject` was added to clean up, one subject kind over.
  - **How it was found is the part worth keeping.** The reversal probe over the supersession unit — open the unit, *then* stage the successor — was written to prove that the door would void the unit being composed. It left the case asserting *"the unit is still submitted"* **green**, and reddened the approve instead. Reading what answered instead of the expected guard is what located the `subject_kind` filter; the claim that the door voids "every `submitted` unit of the plan" had been written into three doc blocks and was false for the one unit it was warning about.
- **Decision**: **not taken here, deliberately.** Widening the filter changes which units an authoring edit destroys on the **window** plane too, which is another surface's behaviour and not a side effect a supersession commit may carry. Two options, both stateable: (a) void every unit whose pin is the plan shape, which makes `inst-ap-pin` mean what it says and costs a window reviewer their in-flight unit whenever an unrelated row is edited; (b) declare the narrow scope normative and rely on the commit-time pin comparison, which is what the code does today, and give the orphan a reaper — the publish commit already has one for its own subject. What is **paid** is the correction of the false claim wherever it was asserted (`8cb41ab60`).
- **Not flagged for veto.** Nothing is narrowed: the code is unchanged and the entry records a gap plus two options.
- **What the second run reads.** A second run of an act whose unit was orphaned reads a pin that cannot match, so it opens a *new* unit and leaves the old one standing — which is the accumulation this entry is about rather than a second fault.
- **Propagated**: S5 §4 (`inst-ap-pin`'s void scope).

#### D-199 [M] A `price_unit` approval's subject is a published plan's key, and it was re-derived through the open draft — so no supersession unit could ever be approved

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) §6 (`subject_kind` enumeration and what each kind's pinned content is).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 by the first test that decided a supersession unit): S5 §6 declares `price_unit` in the `subject_kind` enumeration and says nothing about **which revision** such a unit's content pin is re-derived against. The gear grouped it with `plan_revision`, which resolves the plan's **open draft**. D-88's unit is opened over a *published* plan's canonical scope key, and a published plan nobody has revised has no open draft — so the re-derivation answered `NotFound`, `content_matches_pin` answered `false`, and **every** supersession unit was `APPROVAL_CONTENT_MISMATCH` on the first attempt to decide it. A unit that could be opened and never approved.
  - Landed in `678ecad64` with the unit's opening and invisible to every gate: nothing decided a `price_unit` unit until the orchestrator gave one a decider. It is the same failure the code's own comment describes for a **window** unit one match arm along — *"assembling that way would answer `NotFound` for every window unit in existence"* — reached by the identical route and not recognized, because the arm was chosen by kind rather than by resolution.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06** — a `price_unit` unit's pin is re-derived against the plan's **current** revision, which is the same resolution the `window` kind takes and for the same reason: what the two share is not the kind but the *fact* their subject is about a plan as it currently stands. The two arms are therefore **one** arm rather than two copies. A future `price_unit` writer over an open draft — a bulk batch, say — would be the case that splits them again, and it would split them on the resolution and not on the token.
- **Not flagged for veto.** It fixes a defect against S5 §6's own reading of what a `price_unit` subject is; nothing is narrowed and no rule changes meaning.
- **What the second run reads.** The re-derivation is against the current revision at the reader's instant, so a reviewer's read and the commit's `authorizing_unit` compare the same digest — which is what makes the approve→commit sequence terminate at all.
- **Owed back to the implementation** (`bss/pricing-impl`): **paid** (`8cb41ab60`), with the removal proof: putting the arm back on the open-draft resolution reddens the commit case with `ApprovalContentMismatch` at the approve.
- **Propagated**: S5 §6 (`price_unit`'s pinned content and the revision it resolves).

#### D-200 [H] The publish route's materiality reads rows the publish will not write, so a revision can be material because of somebody else's supersession

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) §3 (`inst-mat-percurrency`, the per-row delta step and the change set it ranges over).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 by review of D-183's own amendment, which had the distinction backwards): two different row sets are in play at a plan-revision publish and the design set names only one. `api::rest::publish` builds the evaluator's [`ChangeSet`] from the **assembled shape** — `CANDIDATE_ROW_STATES`, the plan's `published` rows plus its `draft` rows — while `infra::publish::validated_draft_rows` decides what the commit actually **flips**, and since D-195 that set deliberately excludes a draft standing on a key a published row occupies, because such a row belongs to the supersession unit that staged it.
  - Before D-88 the two sets could not differ: nothing could author a draft on an occupied key. The supersession door is the first producer of that state, so the gap opens with it.
  - The consequence is a **reviewer shown the wrong reason**. A plan carrying a staged supersession successor yields, to the plan-revision evaluator, exactly the pair `inst-mat-percurrency` compares — a moved row against a published predecessor on one key — so the unit's stored verdict can read `thresholdReached` naming a row **the revision will not publish**. What the reviewer is being asked to agree to is a revision that does not contain the change the verdict describes.
  - It fails *safe* in the direction that matters — the verdict is over-material rather than under-material, and D-88's own unit still requires its own two principals for the row in question — so this is a legibility and governance-quality defect rather than a money one. It is [H] because the two-person rule's whole value is that the second person is agreeing to something specific.
  - **The window is narrow only while the supersession unit is `submitted`**: `refuse_held_key` then refuses the plan-revision submit outright. It is wide open once that unit is rejected, withdrawn, or orphaned per D-198 — the register releases the key and the staged draft survives.
- **Decision**: **(b), taken by the product owner 2026-08-06.** §3 `inst-mat-percurrency` now states normatively that a plan revision's materiality ranges over its whole candidate set — the assembled shape, published plus draft — **including rows the commit will not flip**, and states the cost in the same breath rather than leaving it to be discovered. The two row sets stay different **by decision**: the evaluator's input is the candidate set, the commit's output is `validated_draft_rows`.
  - **Why (b) over (a).** (a) — building the change set from `validated_draft_rows` — is the coherent reading of "evaluate what you publish", but it changes `inst-mat-newrow`'s input on **every** publish: a row newly authored and not yet publishable would leave the change set, so the fail-safe that makes a baseline-less row material would stop seeing it. That is a different rule's behaviour, and a larger move than the legibility it buys, for a divergence that fails safe.
  - **What (b) accepts, stated plainly.** A reviewer of a plan revision can be shown a verdict whose reason names a row not in the revision. It is over-material and never under-material, and the row it names is separately two-person through the supersession unit that staged it — so the accepted cost is legibility, not control. The window stays narrow while that unit is `submitted` (`refuse_held_key` refuses the plan-revision submit) and open once it is rejected, withdrawn, or orphaned per D-198.
  - **No code changes**, which is the whole difference between the two options: (b) is the tree's behaviour written down. What was already **paid** is the correction of three documents that asserted the pair was unreachable (`docs/DECISIONS.md` D-183, S5 §3, `domain::materiality_tests`).
- **Not flagged for veto.** The owner took it directly. No code moves and no rule changes meaning — the entry now records which of two row sets the evaluator ranges over, where before the set named only one.
- **What the second run reads.** Unchanged either way: the evaluator writes nothing, so a second publish of the same revision reaches the same arm over the same operands.
- **Propagated**: S5 §3 (`inst-mat-percurrency` — which row set the comparison ranges over).

#### D-201 [H] The supersession subsumes a window shorten D-62 makes always-material, and its own materiality rule does not say so

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) §3 `inst-mat-registered` (D-62's two window triggers); [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-su-commit` (the unit's materiality).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 by review): `inst-su-commit` says the unit's materiality is *"the standard per-currency price-delta evaluation"*, and one of the four writes it performs is the predecessor window's `effectiveTo` shorten — the act `inst-mat-registered` makes **always material**, whatever a threshold says, because `DELETE /price-windows/{id}` and the shortening `PATCH` "carry the identical hazard under plain `plan × write`". `compose_windows` guarantees the target always shortens. So one principal can, through this route and under a configured threshold, perform a window move that the window route requires two principals for.
  - **The hazard D-62 names does not arise here**, and that is the substantive defence: its Problem paragraph is about *removing coverage* — "cancelling an approved scheduled successor silently reverts a two-person-approved price change and leaves the key failing closed once the active window expires". A supersession hands coverage over in the same transaction (`inst-su-commit`: "no interim state exists in which the key is shortened without its scheduled successor"), and `compose_windows` refuses outright if any occupying window begins at or after the changeover — so it cannot cancel or truncate an approved scheduled successor at all.
  - **What is missing is that nobody wrote that down.** Two normative statements point opposite ways and the more specific one silently wins. A reader comparing the two surfaces sees one principal doing through route A what needs two through route B, with nothing in the set explaining the difference — and the code carries no argument either.
- **Decision**: **(a), taken by the product owner 2026-08-06.** `inst-su-commit` states that D-62's window triggers do **not** reach a shorten performed as half of a supersession, with the coverage-continuity argument as the reason and `compose_windows`' collision refusal as the mechanism that keeps it true; `inst-mat-registered`'s D-62 clause carries the same carve-out from the other side, so a reader arriving on the window plane is not left to derive it. The rejected **(b)** — making the unit's change set carry `Trigger::WindowShortening` — is what a literal reading of `inst-mat-registered` requires, and it renders `inst-su-commit`'s own "standard per-currency price-delta evaluation" sentence dead; (a) is the reading the set already implies.
  - **The carve-out is the composition's, not the caller's**, and both texts say so: it holds only for a shorten `compose_windows` produced as half of a changeover. A shorten reaching the window plane by any other route stays always-material, and the supersession unit is still two-person whenever its own per-currency delta trips the configured threshold.
  - **No code changes, and that is the point of the option chosen.** The tree already behaves this way; what was missing was the sentence. The mechanism the carve-out leans on — `compose_windows` refusing any occupying window that begins at or after the changeover — is pinned by the compose suite and was verified against it before the text was written.
- **Not flagged for veto.** The owner took it directly. It narrows nothing: the collision it resolves was between two normative statements, and the resolution states the more specific one explicitly instead of letting it win silently.
- **What the second run reads.** Unchanged: the shorten is applied once per committed act, and a replay is refused before it.
- **Propagated**: S7 `inst-su-commit` (the materiality sentence, the carve-out and the mechanism that keeps it true); S5 §3 `inst-mat-registered` (D-62's trigger, carrying the same carve-out from the window plane's side).

#### D-202 [M] Four residues of the supersession unit, recorded rather than closed

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-su-api` / `inst-su-commit`; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) §6.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 by the four-reviewer pass; each is real, none is worth a decision of its own, and a register entry is cheaper than four doc comments nobody greps):
  1. **The scheduled window's `reason_code` is not covered by the pin or the subject.** It arrives on the *committing* call and lands in a column the append-only trigger then freezes, so an operator can submit under one reason and commit under another — and §6 calls that column "the operator-supplied change reason". The shorten carries none at all, which is the separate design-set gap `SupersessionCommit` already records.
  2. **`PublishUnitKind::PlanContent` is what a supersession's change set declares**, because `ChangeSet::of_records` is the only constructor that fits. It makes `revises_plan_content()` true for an act that revises no plan shape — harmless today, since the set always carries exactly one moved row, and a misdeclaration the next rule keyed on `ChangeSet::unit()` would inherit. Minting a variant is a wire-adjacent change S5 §6 does not declare.
  3. **`region` is a free-form axis inside a cross-tenant registry idempotency key.** `unit_request_id` embeds the canonical scope key's rendering, and `Region::new` accepts any non-blank string, so the `request_id` this gear presents is unbounded in length and charset. `publish_request_id` is built from UUIDs and a `u64`; this is the first that is not.
  4. **The `price_unit` `subject_kind` now carries two `subject_ref` shapes.** The approval store writes `<plan>/supersession/<key>/<instant>`; `price_repo`'s row-authoring audit entries write a bare `price_id`. Both are `price_unit`, so a join on `(subject_kind, subject_ref)` between the two stores resolves nothing for this kind. The shapes are each right for their act; what is missing is a statement that the kind does not imply one shape.
- **Decision**: **recorded, not decided.** Each needs an owner elsewhere: (1) and (4) are §6 statements, (2) is an S5 §6 enumeration question, (3) is a bound on an axis Foundation owns. What is paid is the record.
- **Not flagged for veto.**
- **No propagation, and the field is omitted rather than filled with prose.** This entry changes no design-set text. D-197 already recorded that P1 resolves this field against document paths, so a sentence in it reads as a claim the checker cannot verify — and writing one here moved the live count from 2 to 3 within a minute, which is the same instrument catching the same mistake.

#### D-203 [M] `PriceCreated` has two producers, and that is what makes the name true

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) §17.5 (the authoring door's event) and [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-gc-commit` (the cutover's two).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 while starting the cutover): the event was declared when the gear was created and had **no producer anywhere in it**, so every consumer counting row creations has been counting zero. D-88's entry already carried it as owed and put it on the authoring plane. The cutover forced the question rather than inheriting it: **R-02 is CONFIRMED** (2026-07-10, extended by D-03) and requires the cutover commit to emit `PriceCreated` **×2**, copy and successor.
  - So a confirmed requirement demanded an event from a unit, while the standing engineering note said the producer belonged elsewhere, and neither had been built.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — the authoring door is the producer S3 names, and the cutover is a second one.** S3 settles the first half in as many words: *"a draft price row is authored on the canonical scope key … `PriceCreated` emits per row"*, and *"`PriceCreated` on row authoring"*. The cutover's copy and successor are born **published**, inside its commit, and never pass that door — so R-02 needs it to emit as well. **Two callers, one spelling** (`outbox_repo::NewOutboxEvent::price_created`), and the event then means what its name says: a price row came into existence.
  - **Rejected: minting it from the cutover alone.** It would have satisfied R-02 on the day and made the name a lie — the event would fire for cutover-created rows and nothing else, and a consumer counting creations would see a fraction it could not tell from the whole. This gear has paid three Criticals for one wire-level lie already (D-88's `charge_kind` placeholder); a second is not a saving.
  - **Rejected: emitting nothing and recording the gap.** It narrows a confirmed requirement, which is not a code group's to do.
- **The payload is shorter than `PriceUpdated`'s, and the missing member is the informative one**: no `pending_version_ref`, because authoring is the draft plane and a draft is addressable at **no** `CatalogVersion`. A supersession's event carries one because that act publishes. The dedup key is the row id: a row is created exactly once, a replayed call is refused upstream by the idempotency gate, and a re-authored key is a different row with a different id.
- **What it cost the suites, and the repair is the part worth keeping.** Nine cases reddened, all of them counting outbox contents over fixtures that author a row. The obvious repair — bump each expectation from 0 to 1 — would have left `a_stale_row_version_is_refused_and_writes_nothing` and `registry_absence_stops_the_publish_and_writes_nothing` **named for a guarantee they had stopped checking**. The three helpers that read the outbox now exclude the authoring event instead, so "the commit wrote nothing" keeps meaning that. Two `seq` assertions did move and those are honest: the seed's event now holds seq 0.
- **CORRECTED 2026-08-06, and the correction reverses this entry's conclusion** (`70a3f0ba6`, measured by `the_compose_announces_both_drafts_and_nothing_about_the_act`). The premise above — *"the cutover's copy and successor are born **published**, inside its commit, and never pass that door"* — is **false, and unavoidably so**. Both rows are staged as **drafts** at compose, because `inst-gc-compose` clause (a) makes them the reviewer's subject and the approval content pin covers a `PlanShape` that must contain them; a pin taken over a world without the rows under review is a unit **no approve can ever satisfy**, which is exactly the defect `infra::supersession`'s controlled arm already records. So both rows *do* pass the authoring door, both announce `PriceCreated` there, and a second emission from the commit would be a duplicate its own `PriceCreated/<price_id>` dedup key refuses.
  - **`PriceCreated` therefore has ONE producer, the authoring door**, and the cutover's commit announces `PriceWindowScheduled` ×2 and nothing else. The event still means what its name says — a price row came into existence — and it now means it through one spelling rather than two.
  - What survives of this entry is its **argument**: minting the event from the cutover alone would have made the name a lie, and refusing to emit at all would have narrowed a confirmed requirement. Both are still true; the third option — that the authoring door already covers the cutover's rows — was not considered because the entry believed the rows never reached it.
  - **Propagated**: S7 `inst-gc-commit` (the event list and the second-producer sentence).
- **Not flagged for veto.** Nothing is narrowed: an event the design set already declared acquires the producer the design set already named, plus the second one a confirmed requirement already demanded.
- **Propagated**: S3 §17.5 (the authoring door as the producer, and the payload's absent version ref); S7 `inst-gc-commit` (the cutover as the second producer of one event).

#### D-204 [M] Four shapes the cutover's compose fixed, recorded together

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) §5 (the code roster), `inst-gc-compose`, `inst-co-copy`, `inst-co-bounds`.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 while building the cutover's domain half; each is small, none is worth an entry of its own, and a register entry is cheaper than four doc comments nobody greps):
  1. **One floor, two codes.** §5 declares `SUPERSESSION_INSTANT_PASSED` as *"the same floor `inst-gc-compose` gives cutovers, applied to the everyday mechanism"* — so the bound is one rule with two names. `changeover_floor` is extracted and shared, returning the **sentence**; each unit wraps it in its own code, because an operator reading a refusal is told which act they were performing. A second copy of the bound is how two mechanisms come to disagree about one SLO.
  2. **The dormant key has a code here and none next door.** §5 declares `CUTOVER_GAP` (422) and declares nothing for `inst-su-compose`'s identical refusal, which therefore still renders as `LIFECYCLE_FORBIDDEN` with the ground only in its sentence. One fact, two spellings on the wire. A gear may mint an internal variant freely; a wire code is the set's to declare, so this is recorded rather than repaired.
  3. **The grandfathered copy carries the predecessor's usage line**, which is new since D-196 and could not have been asked before it. A copy that dropped `meter` and `dimensionKey` would be filed under the *meterless* line of the same market — a key the predecessor's subscribers never resolve to, and one that would collide with the copy of a **different** meter's cutover on the same plan.
  4. **`inst-co-bounds` holds at compose by construction**, because the copy's window is scheduled open-ended: an open interval covers every instant the D-04 margin could name, whatever the horizon is and whatever the cycle roster says — and the roster is not in hand at compose, while W6 leaves "the longest billing cycle sold on the key" undefined for usage and one-time keys. The rule's teeth are therefore on a later `effectiveTo` **adjustment**, not here, and a case pins the reading so a future computed end has to answer the bound explicitly rather than by omission.
- **Decision**: **recorded, and (1)–(4) are built** (`f51a9fed5`, `71dfa1000`, `696b85546`, `751dc16c2`). What stays open is (2)'s asymmetry, which belongs to §5.
- **Not flagged for veto.**
- **Propagated**: S7 §5 (the two cutover codes, now with producers); S7 `inst-co-copy` (the copy carries the predecessor's usage line).

#### D-205 [H] Widening the canonical scope key re-classified fields at six sites, and D-196 clause (3) reached two of them

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.1 (the ten axes); D-196 clause (3).
- **Problem** (found by the 2026-08-06 review of `1287dde6d..cf6af5c3d`, then by re-reading the neighbours it named): clause (3) attached `(meter, dimensionKey)` to `to_scope_key` and `scope_key_filter` and was recorded as paid. **Four further sites compared or rendered the key and were left at eight axes**, each found separately, by a different route, over two days:
  1. `price_repo::scope_key_columns` — the tuple `refuse_mispaired` decides "these two rows are on one key" with. A successor on a different meter of one market read as being on the predecessor's key. Failed closed behind D-82's unit guard (`0586ff4ee`).
  2. `domain::sellability::siblings` — six axes of ten, and its own doc said "every *other* axis". Two usage lines of one market in different eligibility classes read as siblings, so most-specific-wins **dropped one from the sellability gate** and `plan_market_verdict` answered over a market whose second line no window had been looked at for. This one is money: a purchase binds both lines.
  3. `content_pin::put_scope_key` — the approval pin. **Masked** for a price record, whose row carries the same two values, and **live** for `put_key_windows`, where a key is framed with no row beside it: two window plans on two meters of one market pinned identically, so an approve could be satisfied by a re-derivation over the other line's coverage.
  4. `api::rest::prices::ScopeKeyView` — the served rendering. Two rows on two meters answered with byte-identical `scope_key` objects, so the surface a reviewer approves from showed one key twice.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — all four repaired, and the generalisation is recorded because it is what the next axis will cost.** **Widening a key is not a schema change; it re-classifies fields, and every site that compares, renders, hashes or stores one has to be re-read.** Clause (3)'s own commit message named three sites and there were seven. Two structural repairs follow from it rather than being incidental:
  - The sibling comparison moved onto the key as `ScopeKey::is_sibling_of` and **destructures with no rest pattern**, so an eleventh axis is a compile error at the one comparison defined by what it *excludes*. A comparison stated at a distance has no way to learn the list grew, which is how this defect survived.
  - `CONTENT_PIN_DOMAIN_SEP` moves **`v3` → `v4`**. Framing two more fields changes every preimage containing any scope key, which is the re-freeze that counter exists for. `THRESHOLD_PIN_DOMAIN_SEP` deliberately stays `v1`: a `ThresholdVersion` carries no scope key, and moving it would invalidate every pending policy unit to record a re-freeze of content those units are not about.
- **The wire grows asymmetrically, and that is D-196's shape rather than an oversight.** `ScopeKeyView` carries ten members and `ScopeKeyRequest` keeps six: the usage line is authored on the **content**, so a request naming it on the key would give one fact two homes — which clause (3) answered with a refusal rather than a rewrite. `dimensionKey` renders `null` for the undimensioned line rather than the store's `''` sentinel.
- **What made the sweep incomplete is recorded because it is the transferable part**: `scope_key.rs`'s module doc still said D-196 was "not yet built" for a day after it was, and that paragraph is what a reader consults before deciding whether a neighbouring "eight axes" needs revisiting. **A module doc that describes the state of the work goes stale in the same commit that finishes the work.**
- **Not flagged for veto.** Nothing is narrowed: four sites are brought up to a decision already CONFIRMED.
- **Propagated**: S1 §4.1 (the ten axes are what every comparison site compares).

#### D-206 [H, latent] `price_basis` and `invoiceItemization` are always-material and not revision-scoped

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §6 (`pricing_bundle`; the "Revision discipline (D-92)" paragraph); D-92; D-104; `inst-ba-material`.
- **Problem** (found by the bundles strand while building `m20260802_000024`): D-92 puts the copy-on-new-revision discipline on *"the three composition tables"* — component, rev-share and rev-share group. `pricing_bundle` is not one of them, and it is where `price_basis` and `invoice_itemization` live. Both are D-104 **always-material** triggers, so a change routes through two people before it publishes — but the column is un-revisioned, so the published revision reads the new value from the instant it is authored, before any approver has seen it. That is verbatim the defect D-92 closed, one table above where D-92 looked, and it re-opens D-11's premise from the other side: the approval pin protects the composition, and here the content moves before the pin exists.
- **Latent, and checked rather than assumed**: the only writer of either column is `BundleRepo::create`, and no mounted route mutates them — `POST …/bundles` sets them once, `PATCH …/bundles/{bundleId}` replaces the composition only. Nothing today can provoke it. The severity stays `[H]` on **consequence**: a basis change turns a bundle from summing its parts into charging its own price, which is a price change to every buyer. The reader this entry is for is the first person to add a route or a bulk path that edits either field, who walks into it with no test and no guard, because the columns are physically mutable and carry no revision.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (b), split identity from content**, adopted as proposed. `pricing_bundle` keeps `PK bundle_id` and holds identity, and the two content columns move onto a revision-scoped carrier that takes the same copy-on-new-revision treatment and append-only trigger as the three children. **Not built** — it is a migration against a table the same wave created, and it is sequenced with the route that would make the hazard live rather than ahead of it.
  - **Rejected: widening `pricing_bundle`'s key to `(bundle_id, plan_revision)`.** It contradicts D-105's stated `PK bundle_id` and makes every child's `bundle_id` foreign key need the revision too.
  - **Rejected explicitly: accepting the in-place mutation and dropping the two fields from D-104's list.** What they gate is a price change to every buyer; they are on that list for that reason.
- **Propagated**: S8 §6 (`pricing_bundle`'s column list and the D-92 paragraph); `inst-ba-material`.

#### D-207 [M] The `plan_id` foreign key D-105 presupposes is not declarable on Postgres

- **Where**: D-105 (*"`pricing_bundle` gains the `plan_id` FK the revision keying presupposes"*); [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §6; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 (D-56's revision-row model).
- **Problem** (measured on Postgres while building `m20260802_000024`): `pricing_plan` is keyed `(plan_id, revision)`, and its only uniqueness on `plan_id` alone lives in two **partial** indexes — `uq_pricing_plan_current` and `uq_pricing_plan_open_draft`. Postgres refuses a partial index as a foreign key's referent, and `pricing_bundle` cannot carry a `revision` because it is the bundle's identity and belongs to no single revision. The constraint D-105 names cannot be written on either backend.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), state the reference as un-enforced at this table and enforce it one level down**, which is what the build does: the three composition tables carry a real foreign key onto `pricing_bundle`, and their append-only triggers resolve `plan_id` through it to read the owning revision's `lifecycle_state`. So an orphan **bundle** row is possible and an orphan **composition** is not, and `a_bundle_may_name_a_plan_that_does_not_exist` pins the absence deliberately rather than leaving it to be rediscovered.
  - **Rejected: a non-partial `UNIQUE (plan_id)` on `pricing_plan`.** It contradicts D-56 outright — one plan has many revision rows.
- **Propagated**: S8 §6 (`pricing_bundle`'s `plan_id` sentence).

#### D-208 [L] `component_plan_id` is a primary-key column and cannot be conditional

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §6 (*"`component_plan_id` (required for `sum_of_parts`, B1)"*); D-105 (PK `(bundle_id, plan_revision, component_plan_id)`).
- **Problem**: a primary-key column is `NOT NULL`, so the qualifier "for `sum_of_parts`" cannot hold — either the column is always required or it is not in the key, and the two statements are in one section.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), always required**, which the key forces and which costs nothing: `inst-bb-own` already requires an `own_price` bundle to carry a matching-currency component set, and a component row naming no plan is one the coverage walk cannot evaluate. Built that way.
  - **Rejected: dropping it from the key and discriminating on `included_sku_id`** — it re-introduces the ambiguity B1 rejects, bare SKU ids being ambiguous per `(currency, region)`.
- **Propagated**: S8 §6 (the component column list).

#### D-209 [M] The two bundle triggers' subject exists in this repository now

- **Where**: `pricing/src/domain/materiality/triggers.rs`; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered`; D-104.
- **Problem**: `Trigger::subject_exists_in_this_crate` answered `false` for `BundleComposition` and `RevenueShareChange`. That predicate's doc says *"a `false` here is a fact about this repository, not about the design set. The slice that lands the subject flips it."* Slice 8 landed it, and nothing goes red on its own — the roster test **transcribes** the answers, so the statement would simply have become false in silence, which is the failure mode the predicate exists to prevent.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — both flipped to `true`** (`a15f754c8`), on evidence rather than on the hand-back's word: `infra::bundle` declares both acts through `ChangeSet::of_act`, four tables and four entities carry the subject, and `module.rs` merges three mounted routes. The roster test reddened on the flip and was updated in the same edit, which is the obligation it exists to create.
  - **`GrandfatheringCutover` stays `false`**, and the contrast is the useful part: most of the cutover's *store* landed on `bss/pricing-impl` in the same stretch, but a registered trigger of this kind is an **act**, read back from a change set some surface declared, and no surface declares the cutover — its orchestrator and route are owed. The flip belongs to the commit that writes the declaration, not to the one that finishes the store.
- **No propagation.** The design set already declares both triggers; what changed is a fact about this repository, and no document cites this.

#### D-210 [L] An unresolvable residual absorber renders under `REVSHARE_UNBALANCED`

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §5 (problem responses) and `inst-rs-residual`; D-07.
- **Problem**: `inst-rs-residual` requires the absorber to be *"a party row within that `(bundle, vendor SKU)` group, or the platform sentinel"*. An absorber naming neither is a reachable authored state that the schema cannot check — the referent is a row in a table pointing back at this one, and the `platform` sentinel has no party row by construction — and §5 declares no code for it. A gear may mint an internal variant freely; a **wire code is the design set's to declare**, so the refusal has to render under an existing one.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), render it as `REVSHARE_UNBALANCED`**, whose D-07 narrowing is *"structural malformation"* and which fits exactly: an unresolvable absorber means no group member takes the residual, so the group cannot be made to sum to 10000 bp. Stated in §5 so the mapping is read rather than inferred.
- **Propagated**: S8 §5 (the `REVSHARE_UNBALANCED` line); `inst-rs-residual`.

#### D-211 [M] `inst-bc-coverage` delegates the currency axis to a checker no crate declares

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-bc-coverage` (*"the currency axis delegates to `CurrencyBindingChecker` case ii"*); §1.4 (the Slice 4 dependency).
- **Problem** (grepped, not assumed): `CurrencyBindingChecker` is Slice 4's named component and **nothing in this crate declares or implements it**. So the sentence describes a delegation with no target, and an implementer either writes a call to an absent type or quietly writes the currency check twice.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), state the ordering.** S8's coverage walk owns **both** axes until Slice 4 lands its checker, at which point the currency arm delegates and the region arm stays S8's. That is what the build does, and `domain::bundle_rules`' module doc names the arm that has to delegate.
  - **Rejected: blocking S8 behind S4.** The two halves are one walk; splitting them is what would need justifying.
- **Propagated**: S8 `inst-bc-coverage` (the ordering clause).

#### D-212 [M] D-119's reverse guard belongs to the **component's** publish pipeline

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-bc-taxbasis`; D-119 (*"a component re-publish whose basis change would mix a referencing bundle's market fails the same way, the referencing bundle enumerated"*); D-54 (the pattern it rides).
- **Problem**: the forward half — every row of one sold market shares one `tax_inclusive` — is a property of the composition and is enforced in `domain::bundle_rules::check_tax_basis`. The **reverse** half is a rule about *another plan's* publish, and it needs a read the composition walk does not have and must not have: the set of bundles referencing that component. A pure validator over a handed-in snapshot cannot express it, so as written the rule has no owner in the code.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), it is a rule of the component's publish pipeline**, registered in `domain::publish::rules::run_publish_rules` beside the other Foundation-owned rules, reading `pricing_bundle_component` by `component_plan_id` — the index `idx_pricing_bundle_component_plan` exists for exactly this shape of question, and S11's `inst-re-references` asks the same one for retirement. `inst-bc-taxbasis` says so, so its absence from a bundle-side validator is not read as a gap.
  - **Rejected: a periodic reconciliation.** It makes the rule a detection rather than a guard, and contradicts D-119's own argument that the bundle-side check is otherwise *"a point-in-time promise a later component publish silently breaks"*.
  - **It does not become a second assembly point.** `run_publish_rules` is the single one, and its module doc says why: a bundle-side call bolted into `infra::publish` is the *"whichever slice happens to load first"* outcome §4.2 keeps the base set out of. The facts the rule needs are **resolved by the caller and handed in** on `PublishRuleParams`, exactly as the tenant's default rounding policy and the size caps are.
- **Two derivations this decision settles, so the implementer inherits no open questions**:
  1. **Which markets.** Not the referencing bundle's declared set — D-216 leaves that on the publish request and a component's publish does not carry it. The markets that matter are exactly those the **publishing plan's own candidate rows** sell in: a basis conflict cannot arise in a market this plan contributes no row to, so the narrower set is complete for this guard.
  2. **Which revision of the referencing bundle.** Its **current published** one — the composition consumers actually resolve against. A draft revision's components are not yet anybody's truth, and guarding against them would fail a publish over a composition no consumer can see.
- **Built 2026-08-06** (`8e7fd6bb7`), and two things the entry did not anticipate came out of building it. **The resolver's answer was order-dependent**: it takes the first basis seen per market, and the component read was unsorted — so on one engine the sibling is read first and on another the publishing plan might be, in which case the guard compares the plan against *itself* and can never fire. The sibling read is ordered now. **And the first fixture could not reach the rule's own scenario**: it published the plan for the *first* time, so the plan had no published rows and excluding them was a no-op — D-119 says **re-publish**, and the case now gives the plan a market it already sells in and asserts that market by name.
- **Propagated**: S8 `inst-bc-taxbasis` (the reverse guard's owner).

#### D-213 [L] The missing-basis refusal is an authoring-edge code, not a publish-pipeline one

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §5 (the missing-basis problem response); `inst-bb-declared`. *(The code is named descriptively here and not as a bare token: a bare token in register prose reads to spec-check as a reference and would close that code's P3 `code-unreferenced` debt without any rule having gained one — which is what a first draft of this entry did.)*
- **Problem**: `pricing_bundle.price_basis` is `NOT NULL` under a two-value `CHECK` and the domain type is a closed enum, so a **stored** bundle always has a basis. The code is reachable only from a request that omits the field — which is the right code for the right caller — but §5 lists it beside nine publish-time refusals, so it reads as though the publish pipeline raises it, and a validator carrying that arm would carry a branch no state can reach.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — annotate it in §5 as an authoring-edge code.** Built that way: `check_basis_declared` is a separate entry point taking `Option<PriceBasis>`, and `validate` does not carry the arm.
- **Propagated**: S8 §5 (the missing-basis annotation).

#### D-214 [L] "This plan already carries a bundle" gets a code of its own

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §5 (problem responses); §6 (`pricing_bundle`, one per plan).
- **Problem**: a plan carries at most one bundle, enforced by `uq_pricing_bundle_plan`, and the refusal is one an operator can act on. §5 declares no code for it. Borrowing `DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY` would name a **price row's** canonical key and send the operator to look at the wrong object entirely.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), declare `BUNDLE_EXISTS_ON_PLAN`** on the conflict ladder, which is the gear naming a refusal it owns. Built with that spelling; a different one is a one-line change in `infra/error_mapping.rs`.
- **Propagated**: S8 §5 (the new code).

#### D-215 [L] The composition route addresses a resource, not the collection

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §5 API table (`POST/PATCH | /bss-pricing/v1/bundles`).
- **Problem**: a `PATCH` to the collection puts the subject in the body, and the composition route carries an `If-Match` precondition. A precondition addresses a **resource**; two concurrent composition edits on different bundles would be presenting entity tags against one URL.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), `PATCH /bss-pricing/v1/bundles/{bundleId}`**, which is what is built and what every other subject-addressing route in this gear does.
  - **Rejected: keeping the collection `PATCH` and dropping the precondition.** The composition rides the plan revision's tag, and dropping it reopens the lost update `fr-concurrent-edit` closes.
- **Propagated**: S8 §5 (the API table row).

#### D-216 [L] The bundle's sold-market set is carried on the publish request

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-bc-coverage`, `inst-bc-taxbasis` (both quantify over *"each `(currency, region)` the bundle sells in"*), `inst-bb-rowless` (a `sum_of_parts` bundle carries no price rows of its own).
- **Problem**: both rules quantify over the markets the bundle sells in, and for `sum_of_parts` there is nothing to read that set off — the bundle has no rows, and the components' rows are the thing being *checked against* it rather than the thing defining it. Taking the union of the components' markets would make the coverage rule vacuous: every market would be covered by construction and `CURRENCY_NOT_COVERED` unreachable.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), the publish request carries the market set**, which is what is built: it is the operator's declaration of where the bundle is offered, and the rule then has something to fail against. **(b), a sold-market child table, is named as the likely destination** once the set must be *frozen* into the read model rather than restated per publish.
  - **Rejected: deriving it from the registry's SKU availability.** That is a read across a gear boundary this slice has no contract for.
- **Propagated**: S8 §5 (the publish request shape); `inst-bc-coverage`.

#### D-217 [M] The stored materiality verdict does not say which trigger made an act material

- **Where**: `pricing/src/domain/materiality.rs` (`MaterialityReason::AlwaysMaterialTrigger`); [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered`; D-104.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 while building the cutover's orchestrator): the reason is a **unit** variant, so every registered act renders the same stored verdict — `{"material":true,"reason":"alwaysMaterialTrigger","tripped":null}` — whether it was a window cancellation, a window shortening, a bundle composition, a rev-share re-split or a grandfathering cutover. D-104's own entry requires the opposite in as many words: *"the stored verdict names which act it was: a rev-share re-split **is** vendor payout, and an operator reading the approval record should not have to infer that from a trigger called composition"*. The register is where an auditor reads years later, and the one field that would tell them what happened is the one the act's subject string already carries — so the verdict is the redundant half today and the *unavailable* half the moment a subject convention changes.
- **Options**: (a) give the variant its [`Trigger`] payload and render its token beside the reason; (b) leave it and rely on `subject_ref` plus `subject_kind`; (c) store the trigger in a separate column.
- **Recommendation**: (a). The trigger is already in hand at every producer — each one passes it to `ChangeSet::of_act` — so nothing has to be resolved, and the token exists (`Trigger::as_str`) for exactly this kind of diagnostic. (b) is what stands and it is what D-104 rejected; (c) adds a column for a fact the verdict is already the home of.
- **Decision**: owed. **Not built** — it moves a shared enum, a shared renderer and the shape of every stored verdict, which is more than a clause of the cutover should carry.
- `a_cutover_is_always_material_and_the_evaluator_is_not_asked` asserts the **absence**, so it reddens the day the payload arrives.

#### D-218 [M] A cutover replaced a row on a key and announced no `PriceUpdated` — **DECIDED and BUILT 2026-08-07**

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-gc-commit` (the event list), `inst-su-commit` (the supersession's), `inst-co-supersede`; D-127.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 while building the same clause): a cutover's successor lands on the predecessor's **own** key and flips it `published → superseded` — that is `inst-co-supersede`, and D-127 binds both producers to one unit guard for exactly that reason. A supersession announces `PriceUpdated` for that transition, whose payload names `supersedesPriceId` precisely so a consumer can stop resolving the row that was replaced. `inst-gc-commit` lists `PriceWindowScheduled` and (before D-203's correction) `PriceCreated`, and **not** `PriceUpdated`. So a consumer that reads `PriceUpdated` to learn a key's current row has changed learns nothing from a cutover, on the one act whose whole purpose is to change it.
- **Options**: (a) add `PriceUpdated` ×1 to `inst-gc-commit`, for the successor, with the predecessor as `supersedesPriceId` — the payload already fits, since the cutover sets that link (D-127); (b) leave it, on the argument that the grandfathering copy makes a cutover a different shape of change and consumers should read the window events; (c) mint a cutover-specific event.
- **Recommendation**: (a). The transition being announced is the *same* transition, produced by the second sanctioned producer of it, and D-127 already says the two acts are one guard's business. (b) makes a consumer's correctness depend on which act moved the row, which is the coupling D-100 removed on the truth side. (c) mints a name for a fact an existing name covers.
- **Decision (2026-08-07): option (a), and it is built.** `infra::cutover::cutover_in` enqueues one `PriceUpdated` for the successor with the predecessor as `supersedesPriceId`, **before** the two `PriceWindowScheduled` — the order `infra::supersession` emits the same pair in, which matters because this entry's whole argument is that the two acts announce one transition. `inst-gc-commit`'s event list says so.
- **One premise of this entry was imprecise and is corrected rather than quietly worked around.** Option (a) argued the payload "already fits, since the cutover sets that link (D-127)". **The cutover sets no such column**: `src/infra/cutover.rs` contains no `supersedes_price_id` at all, because a cutover's successor arrives as a **client-authored row ref** `(Uuid, RowVersion)` — which is exactly what D-127's own Problem paragraph describes. D-127's *decision* text ("both set `supersedes_price_id` on the successor") therefore reads as an implementation fact where it is a statement about the guard's referent. It changes nothing here, and the reason is worth writing down: the commit transaction holds `context.predecessor.price_id` and `successor.price_id` directly, so it is the **only** place that has both sides of the link without a lookup — the payload is constructible whether or not the column is set. Whether the column itself is owed on the cutover path is a separate question this entry does not answer.
- **The census caught the change before the assertion did.** `sqlite_cutover_unit::an_approved_cutover_announces_two_window_schedules_and_no_second_price_created` transcribes the act's whole event list, so adding an event reddened there first and the line was extended deliberately. A second case pins the payload's two ids, the key and the changeover, because the name census would stay green if the event named the wrong row. Probed: removing the emission reddens exactly those two cases and nothing else.

#### D-219 [H] §6's line primary key and D-92's stable line identity cannot both hold

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §6, the `pricing_price_overlay_line` heading — *"(PK `line_id`; FK `(price_overlay_id, overlay_revision)` — D-42, ≥ 1 per overlay revision; **copy-on-new-revision** with stable line identity where unchanged, D-92 …)"*; D-92.
- **Problem**: the two clauses in that one parenthesis contradict each other. A copy-on-new-revision writes a **second row** for one logical line under the successor revision; under `PK line_id` that row needs a **new** id; the line's identity is then not stable across revisions — which is the half a consumer diffing two revisions actually uses, and the half D-92 is about. The defect propagates one table down: §6 keys `pricing_price_overlay_line_amount` `UNIQUE (line_id, currency)` while saying it *"rides the same revision through its line"*, and under that pair a value set is **shared** by every revision of its line, so editing a draft's money would mutate the published revision's.
- **Options**: (a) widen the line's key to `(line_id, overlay_revision)` and the amount table's to `(line_id, overlay_revision, currency)`, with the pair as its foreign key; (b) keep `PK line_id` and mint a new id per revision, withdrawing D-92's stable-identity clause; (c) keep `PK line_id` and store lines once per **overlay**, abandoning copy-on-new-revision.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a)**, built as `m20260802_000033` and `m20260802_000034`. It preserves the **decision** (D-92) and amends the **spelling** (§6's column list), which is the right way round: a primary key is a spelling of a decision, not a decision. (c) reintroduces the exact defect D-92 exists to close — a draft edit mutating published truth.
- `sqlite_overlay_store::one_line_id_survives_the_copy_onto_a_new_revision` is the case that cannot exist under §6's literal key.
- **Propagated**: S9 §6 (both column lists).

#### D-220 [H] D-138's authoring warning had no computable condition until §F.1 decided the stack's sort direction — **CLOSED and BUILT 2026-08-07**

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §3 step 2 (`inst-plv-adjustment`) and §5's warning list — *"authoring **warns** when a `fixed` line is not the lowest-precedence layer that can match its target"*; D-138; §F.1 (the overlay stack's sort direction, open).
- **Problem**: the warning presumes the stack is applied from the low end. Under the opposite reading a `fixed` layer at a *higher* precedence is applied first and discards nothing, while a `fixed` at the **lowest** precedence discards everything above it. The condition is not merely uncertain — it is exactly **inverted**, and a warning that fires on the wrong half of the cases is worse than none, because an operator who sees none concludes their stack is safe. D-138 itself observes the direction is *"strictly load-bearing"* for `fixed` lines; what it did not observe is that its **own** warning inherits that dependency.
- **Options**: (a) decide the sort direction in §F.1 and keep D-138's wording; (b) withdraw the warning until the direction closes; (c) reword it to fire on any `fixed` layer that is not the *only* matching layer — true under both readings, at the cost of firing more often.
- **Recommendation**: (a), since everything downstream of the direction is blocked on it anyway; (c) is the honest interim. What is built is D-138's **literal** condition (`domain::overlay_rules::check_replacement`), which is correct under only one of the two readings — flagged rather than silently picking a direction.
- **Decision**: **owner's, not the controller's.** Recorded, not resolved — **and resolved 2026-08-07 by [D-249](#d-249-h-the-overlay-stack-applies-ascending--lowest-precedence-first-most-specific-last), which takes this entry's option (a)**: the direction is ascending, so D-138's wording is right as written and `domain::overlay_rules::check_replacement` is correct rather than correct-under-one-reading.
- **Clause 1 is built (2026-08-07).** `OverlayWorld::lower_precedence_matchers` is renamed `layers_beneath`, because the old name became false: *beneath* is the **total** order read ascending, so it is a strictly lower precedence **or** an equal precedence with a lower class. `overlay_facts` collects both, and a `fixed` line discarding an equal-precedence lower-class layer is warned where it was silently not.
- **Clause 2 stands.** The world is still collected at **plan** granularity, so the over-warning half — a lower layer on one SKU warning against a `fixed` on another, a cohort-less layer against a cohort-targeted line — is unchanged. Under-warning was the serious half and is closed; over-warning is visible to the operator and remains.
- **A second clause of the same warning, and it survives whichever way the direction goes.** The predicate is coarser than the stack it warns about, in two ways that pull opposite:
  1. **It cannot see the class-order half of the total order.** §3 step 3a orders `precedence → class order → overlay id`, so at *equal* precedence the class order decides which layer is beneath; precedence is unique only **within** a class, so an equal-precedence cross-class pair is reachable and trips no duplicate refusal. The world collects only overlays at a **strictly lower** precedence, so a `fixed` line discarding a lower-*class* layer at the same precedence is silently **not** warned.
  2. **It fires on layers that provably cannot match the same row.** The world is collected at **plan** granularity, so a lower layer on one SKU warns against a `fixed` line on another; a cohort-less lower layer warns against a cohort-targeted `fixed` line although D-78 makes the two disjoint by eligibility; and lower layers of **any** scope class and value contribute.
  Under-warning is the more serious half. Rebuilding the predicate before the direction is decided risks building it twice, and its inputs are already assembled — `overlay_repo::overlay_facts` reads every published overlay's class, precedence, scope and line keys in one pass.
- **Propagated**: S9 §3 step 2, §5 (the warning), §F.1.

#### D-221 [M] The overlay's audit token, and the approval mirror that D-158 obliges to hold it

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) (every mutating surface); [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) §6 (`pricing_approval.subject_kind`, `pricing_audit_log`); D-14, D-135, D-158, D-50.
- **Problem**: the overlay plane wrote **no audit record at all** — a plain regression against D-14 on an always-material subject. `AuditSubjectKind` had four members and none was an overlay, and an overlay may not borrow `plan_revision`: an overlay **is not a plan and has no plan**, so that token would put two aggregates on one chain and make *"who changed this plan"* answer about an object the plan does not contain (D-135 keys a chain on the audited subject's aggregate).
- **Options**: (a) add the member, its audit writers and the approval mirror's token together; (b) give the overlay plane its own subject-kind enum; (c) leave overlay mutations unaudited.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), built.** `OverlayRepo`'s four mutations append inside their own transactions, on a chain of the overlay's own. (b) is two vocabularies for one column, which is the defect D-158/D-175 removed from the audit vocabulary; (c) is the regression itself.
- **The token is `overlay`, and the design set had already declared it.** The overlay register's O-3 reports that *"S5 §6's `pricing_approval.subject_kind` enumeration also does not name an overlay … so the same token is owed on the approval plane and that **needs a D-number**"*. **That premise is false and was measured before this entry was built on**: S5 §6's enumeration reads `plan_revision | price_unit | window | **overlay** | membership | bundle | retirement | policy | historical_import | bulk_batch`, and the audit half of §6 takes it *"verbatim"* under a paragraph forbidding exactly the alternative — *"Not a parallel vocabulary … two spellings would let the approval record and the audit record of one decision disagree about what the decision was about."* So **no token is owed and no D-number was needed for one**; what this gear owed was to *implement* the declared spelling. It was first built as `price_overlay` — a second spelling of a declared token, and the resource type of the same name in S5's authz table is what made that plausible — and corrected the same day. Nothing in the design set changes.
- **What the decision does cost on the approval plane.** D-158 makes the two stores one enumeration **extended together**, so a member added for the audit plane obliges the mirror's CHECK to admit it — `m20260802_000035`.
