The token therefore becomes storable **ahead of its writer**, which is narrower than the gear's usual "no token without a writer" and is exactly what D-158 asks: the kind is *storable* and not yet *stored*. D-50 makes every overlay mutation an approval subject, so the writer is owed; it is [D-225](#d-225-m-the-overlay-submits-approval-and-publish-units-are-not-wired)'s. Both approval-plane resolution arms refuse a record carrying the token and say why, so *storable* is never read as *resolvable*.
- **One test's premise moved with it**, and it had named the day: `approval_repo_tests::a_subject_kind_outside_d158s_enumeration_is_a_corrupt_row` used `overlay` as *"the next member of S5 §6's enumeration with no writer here"*, having used `window` before that for the same role. It uses `membership` now.
- **The chain id is not the overlay id.** A plan chain is the plan id verbatim, and plan ids and overlay ids are both `UUIDv7` from one generator into two tables — so a raw overlay id would be disjoint from every plan chain by **improbability** only, a standard this gear's own `policy_chain` refuses because an interleaved segment verifies perfectly while telling an auditor something false.
- **Nothing is owed to the governance slice**, which is this entry's own finding: the token it would have added is already declared there, verbatim, and the correction was this gear's to make.
- **Propagated**: S9 §6.

#### D-222 [M] Three refusals this slice must make have no wire code in §5

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §5's problem-response list, against three rules the same document states: `inst-plv-scope`'s taxonomy failure (§2 says only *"Unknown `brand`/`customerGroup` value → taxonomy failure (422)"* and names no code); the **zero-line** overlay (D-42 requires ≥ 1 line and §9 asserts a zero-line overlay fails, while §5's nine codes cover no empty line set); and D-31's **retired target**, which names a read-model flag and an alarm and no code because the design set never expected it to reach a caller — it does, the moment an author retargets an overlay onto a retired plan.
- **Options**: (a) §5 declares `SCOPE_VALUE_UNKNOWN`, `OVERLAY_HAS_NO_LINE` and `TARGET_RETIRED`; (b) fold each into an existing code; (c) leave them minted by the gear.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a)**, and (c) until §5 carries them, which is what is built (`domain::overlay_rules`, all three documented as minted). (b) loses the discriminator in all three: reporting a zero-line overlay under the nearest candidate would send the operator to check a target that does not exist. This is [D-214](#d-214-l-this-plan-already-carries-a-bundle-gets-a-code-of-its-own)'s posture verbatim — a gear may mint a `DomainError` variant freely; a **wire code** is the design set's to declare.
- **Propagated**: S9 §5 (the three codes), §2 (`inst-plv-scope`'s response).

#### D-223 [M] `customerGroup` is refused while its taxonomy does not exist, not recorded as unenforced

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §3 step 1 (*"`customerGroup` → `GroupTaxonomy`"*), §6's `pricing_customer_group_taxonomy`; D-211; §F.1.
- **Problem**: that table belongs to the membership half, which sits on §F.1's open owner fork (`inst-mm-immediate` and `inst-ms-pinned` state opposite outcomes) and which the overlay strand does not build. So one of the six scope classes has no value universe.
- **Options**: (a) refuse the class while its taxonomy is absent; (b) record the arm as unenforced and let such overlays publish unvalidated; (c) build the customer-group taxonomy alone, ahead of the membership half.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), built.** `overlay_repo::taxonomy_declares` answers `false` for that class unconditionally and the refusal names the missing universe. It is the fail-closed reading and it is reversible in one line when the membership half lands. (b) is precisely the shape D-211 was written against — answering "declared" for a universe that does not exist. (c) is viable and cheap but presumes a value vocabulary the open fork may still move.
- **Propagated**: S9 §3 step 1.

#### D-224 [M] `pricing_price_overlay` carries a concurrency column

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §6's `pricing_price_overlay` column list, against §5's authoring routes and `fr-concurrent-edit`; D-170.
- **Problem**: §6 names no `row_version`. Without one the `PATCH` has no entity tag to answer `If-Match` with, and two authors editing one draft revision both satisfy their precondition — the lost update `fr-concurrent-edit` exists to refuse. A bundle's composition borrows the **plan revision's** tag because a bundle rides a plan; an overlay has no plan, so it can borrow nothing.
- **Options**: (a) add `row_version`, matching `pricing_plan`'s and D-170's `"<revision>-<version>"` tag shape; (b) tag the revision by a content hash; (c) leave the `PATCH` without a precondition.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a)**, built as part of `m20260802_000032`. (b) is heavier and inconsistent with every other authoring plane in the gear; (c) is rejected outright.
- **Propagated**: S9 §6 (the column list).

#### D-225 [M] The overlay submit's approval and publish units are not wired

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `inst-pl-commit` — the submit is *"always material (D-50)"*, opens a Slice 5 approval unit, and *"the approved overlay is then a **publish unit through the Foundation engine** (D-06)"*; `inst-as-reject`.
- **Problem**: what the route does today is resolve the world, run the whole validator set, answer the §5 status split and return **202** with an evaluated always-material verdict. What it does **not** do is open the approval unit or request a `CatalogVersion`. So the status is honest about the act and the act is incomplete: nothing publishes, and nothing holds the overlay while an approver looks at it. `inst-as-reject` has nothing to reject in consequence — same root, not a second gap.
- **Options**: (a) wire both, together with the approval-plane token [D-221](#d-221-m-the-overlays-audit-token-and-the-approval-mirror-that-d-158-obliges-to-hold-it) has now made storable; (b) a follow-on strand owns the approval + publish seam for overlays.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — (a), and the approval half is built.** The submit opens the Slice 5 unit, evaluates the always-material verdict through `materiality::evaluate` over the declared act rather than asserting it, and **names the unit on the wire** — a submitter cannot chase a review they cannot name. `re_derive` resolves the pinned revision so the unit can be decided, and the reject path is asserted rather than assumed (below). **The publish half is still owed**: an approved overlay does not reach the Foundation engine, so nothing publishes yet — see [D-234](#d-234-m-an-overlay-publish-unit-is-not-a-planpublishunit-and-cannot-be-one).
- **Three sub-decisions the build had to make, each recorded where it is made**:
  1. **The unit holds no keys.** `inst-co-single-pending` binds units *"whose change set touches the key"*, and the key it names is a **canonical scope key** — the held-key register's vocabulary is scope keys and its refusal says *"scope key {key}"* in as many words. An overlay writes no price row, so a held key minted for it would put a string in that register which the register's own diagnostic would misname. The guard is the **subject ref**: one pending unit per overlay *revision*, the revision and not the overlay, so a pin taken over revision 3 cannot authorize a decision about revision 4. **Left open deliberately**: two *different* overlays pending on one `(scope class, precedence)` slot are both approvable and the second publish is refused by the partial index — D-148's read-then-index arrangement, and the posture Slice 9 already built for the unapproved path. A unit-level refusal would need a register keyed on something other than a scope key, which is a decision the design set has not made.
  2. **The pin is the overlay's own**, under a third domain separator beside the approval and threshold ones. The window and supersession units pin the *plan shape* (D-99 makes windows plan facts); an overlay has no plan and its lines may target several or none, so there is no plan shape to pin. `row_version` is **excluded**: a pin answers *"is this the content the reviewer saw"*, and a concurrency token can move for reasons the content did not — a pin failing for a reason no reviewer can see. That is `PlanShape`'s own argument for excluding `evaluated_at`.
  3. **An overlay reaches a region exactly when it is scoped to one.** `regions()` decides which grants may decide the unit; a brand- or partner-scoped overlay has no region axis and answers the empty set, which every grant covers — the reading the policy unit already takes. A `region`-scoped overlay is the case where that would be wrong, and it contributes its own region.
- **`inst-as-reject` costs this slice nothing, and that is asserted rather than assumed.** A rejected overlay unit has no compensating write to make: the submit writes to `pricing_approval` and to nothing else — no lifecycle flip, no staged copy, no line touched — so the pre-submit state *is* the current state. That is a property of this slice's design and not a general truth: the plan plane has a draft to return, and Slice 7 parks a cutover's whole payload in the approval record because there is nowhere else to hold it.
- **Propagated**: S9 `inst-pl-commit`, `inst-as-reject`.

#### D-226 [L] `Trigger::owning_slice` named design documents that do not exist — four arms, not three

- **Where**: `pricing/src/domain/materiality/triggers.rs`, `owning_slice`; [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md); [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md).
- **Problem**: the field's whole stated purpose is *"a path rather than a slice number, **so a reader greps once**"*, and it greped to nothing for four of eighteen triggers. Three Slice-9 triggers returned `design/09-overlays-groups.md`, where the document is `09-price-overlays.md` and the groups half the old spelling named was never a separate file; and — not in the overlay register, found by measuring every arm rather than the reported ones — the GA-gate trigger returned `design/04-market-tax.md`, where the document is `04-currency-tax.md`.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — corrected, and the guard strengthened.** The roster test asserted `starts_with("design/") && contains(".md")` under a doc comment claiming it asserted *"the path is a real one and not a placeholder"*. That claim was the defect rather than the wording: a shape check cannot tell a document from a plausible name for one, and both wrong paths were perfectly well formed and sat green. `every_trigger_names_a_design_document_that_opens` now opens the file.
- **No design-set propagation**, and the field is spelled out rather than claimed as `none`: the value is a pointer *into* the design set, so what was wrong was the pointer and not any document. The register's resolver reads propagation claims, and a claim of nothing is not one.

#### D-227 [L] The overlay authoring `PATCH` addresses a resource, not the collection

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §5's API table (`POST/PATCH` both on `/bss-pricing/v1/price-overlays`, subject in the body).
- **Problem**: a precondition addresses a **resource**, and a collection that answers `If-Match` is a collection pretending to be one. Two concurrent edits of different overlays would be presenting entity tags against one URL.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — `PATCH /bss-pricing/v1/price-overlays/{overlayId}`**, which is what is mounted. This is [D-215](#d-215-l-the-composition-route-addresses-a-resource-not-the-collection) one slice over and the two are **one question decided once**: the same spelling error in two §5 tables, with the same remedy and the same reason.
- **Propagated**: S9 §5 (the API table row).

#### D-228 [L] `inst-plv-disclosure`'s exclusion half has no subject in this crate

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §3 step 7 — a `restricted` overlay *"is excluded from every consumer-facing enumeration and from the base-price preview"*, while *"Operator/service reads … are unaffected"*.
- **Problem**: this gear mounts **no consumer-facing enumeration**. The overlay list is the admin and Tariffs read, which step 7 explicitly exempts. So the catalog's whole half of the rule is: carry the flag, default it `restricted`, publish it — which is built and tested. The **exclusion** half belongs to Presentation and the Tariffs member-scoped preview (F-34).
- **Decision**: **no change to the design set.** Recorded so a reader who greps `inst-plv-disclosure` and finds one flag and no filter reads it as **placement** rather than omission. What makes it reachable is named: the first consumer-facing surface in this gear — a storefront enumeration, or a base-price preview returning overlay-adjusted prices — is where the rule acquires a subject.
- **Propagated**: S9 §3 step 7 (the placement, so the missing filter reads as latent rather than absent).

#### D-229 [L] One cutover instant, two renderings, across the price and overlay planes

- **Where**: `pricing_price.cohort` against `pricing_price_overlay_line.cohort`; [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `inst-plv-eligibility`.
- **Problem**: the price row's cohort axis is stored as **epoch milliseconds in text** (with the literal `none` for the two non-grandfathered classes); the overlay line's is a `timestamptz`, because §6 types it one. `inst-plv-eligibility` compares them, so it is a comparison **across two renderings** of one instant — and the naive implementation, comparing as text, would silently match **nothing** rather than fail.
- **Options**: leave it as one documented conversion, or converge the two columns on one rendering.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — leave it.** The two renderings are each right for their own column: the price row's cohort is a **key axis** compared for equality across a gear boundary, the overlay line's is an authored instant. One conversion in one place (`overlay_repo::published_generation`, with the reason) is cheaper than a migration. Recorded for the reader who adds the **second** comparison site, which is where the cost changes.
- **Propagated**: S9 §6 (the overlay line's `cohort` column, where the second comparison site would be added).

#### D-230 [L] `inst-plv-class-tiebreak`'s equal-precedence warning was not built; its class order was — **BUILT 2026-08-07**

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §3 step 3a — *"authoring additionally **warns** on an equal-precedence cross-class pair with overlapping targets so the operator sees the tie before relying on the break"*.
- **Problem**: the id's **normative half** — the class-specificity order `customerGroup > partner > orgTier > brand > region > global` — **is** built, because the read model has to carry it: `ScopeClass`'s variants are declared least-specific-first so the derived ordering *is* that order, pinned against a re-ordering. The **warning** half is not, and the id was outside the overlay strand's census.
- **Built 2026-08-07 together with D-220's first clause**, as this entry required: `EQUAL_PRECEDENCE_CROSS_CLASS_TIE` warns once per tying overlay, naming it, its class and the plans both reach, and says which side the class order puts beneath. Nothing is refused — the tie is legal and the break deterministic; the warning exists so the operator sees it first.
- **Decision**: recorded so the built half is not mistaken for the whole id. Whoever owns `inst-plv-class-tiebreak` builds the warning; the world it needs is already assembled — `overlay_repo::overlay_facts` reads every published overlay's class, precedence and line targets in one pass, which is exactly the warning's input. It is the same predicate [D-220](#d-220-h-d-138s-authoring-warning-has-no-computable-condition-until-f1-decides-the-stacks-sort-direction)'s first clause needs, so the two should be built together.
- **Propagated**: S9 §3 step 3a.

#### D-231 [M] An overlay had no `abandoned` tombstone, so a discarded revision number was re-minted — **DECIDED 2026-08-07, design landed, migration deferred behind the strand fence; BUILT 2026-08-08 on `m20260802_000045`**

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §6's `pricing_price_overlay` `lifecycle_state` — *"`draft | published | superseded`"*; D-145; D-170.
- **Problem**: the overlay's three states carry **no `abandoned` tombstone**, unlike `pricing_plan`, whose D-145 discard flips a draft to `abandoned` precisely so *"the number it consumed is never attached to a different shape"*. An overlay's discarded draft leaves by DELETE, so the number it consumed is gone from the table and the next open re-mints it. A client still holding the discarded draft's entity tag finds a **different** revision under the same overlay identity, and a stale `If-Match` would match the fresh row's compare-and-swap and land an edit against content that no longer exists.
- **Found by trying to fix it**, which is why the entry is this specific. `open_revision` was changed from "published + 1" to a high-water `max(revision) + 1` on a review finding, and the test written to prove the fix **failed**: `max` cannot see a deleted row either. The change is kept for a different and smaller reason — a `superseded` predecessor can outrank the published row, so the old form read the wrong row even where the two agreed — and the case asserts the **re-mint** rather than a fix.
- **Options**: (a) give the overlay an `abandoned` state, as `pricing_plan` has; (b) keep a separate high-water column on the overlay's identity; (c) accept the re-mint and make the entity tag carry something the number does not.
- **Recommendation**: (a) — it is the discipline the plan plane already has, for the reason D-145 states in the same words this entry needs, and it makes a high-water read a true one. (b) duplicates state a tombstone represents naturally.
- **Decision (2026-08-07): option (a).** The overlay gets `abandoned`, and §6 now declares it: a discarded draft revision takes a terminal `draft → abandoned` flip and MUST NOT leave by `DELETE`. This is an engineering call rather than a product one — it mirrors a discipline the plan plane already carries for the reason D-145 states in the same words, and (b) duplicates as a column the state a tombstone represents naturally.
- **The design half is landed; the code half is deliberately deferred, and the reason is a fence rather than a difficulty.** Closing it needs a migration — `abandoned` added to the overlay's state `CHECK`, on both engines, and the discard path changed from `DELETE` to a flip — and two slice strands are building against this branch under an agreement that **the main session mints no migration number while they run** (they own `m20260802_000043`–`000049` and `000050`–`000059`). Minting one here would be the collision the ranges exist to prevent. Owed as one commit after they merge, and it is small: the state, the flip, the guard that refuses any exit from `abandoned`, and the case that a re-opened revision number is now genuinely new.
- **What is already true and does not wait for it**: `open_revision`'s high-water `max(revision) + 1` is kept and is the right form for the tombstone world; under `DELETE` it was simply not a true high-water read, which is what the failed test measured.
- **Built 2026-08-08 on `m20260802_000045`**, and three things this entry predicted turned out differently enough to be worth the lines.
  1. **There was no discard path to flip.** The entry says "the discard path changed from `DELETE` to a flip", and `OverlayRepo` had no such path at all — no `abandon_draft`, no delete of a revision, nothing. The only discard in the tree was a raw `DELETE` in a **test helper**, which is what made the re-mint reachable. So the build *added* the sanctioned operation (`OverlayRepo::abandon_draft`, `AuditAction::Abandon`, lines dropped before the flip because `abandoned` is not `draft` and the child triggers refuse a delete under a non-draft parent) rather than redirecting one. Recorded because "flip instead of delete" reads as a one-line change to a path that existed.
  2. **What closes the hazard is removing `DELETE`, not adding the state.** `abandoned` alone would have left the old exit open and the re-mint reachable by the path it already took, so the trigger refuses `DELETE` in **every** state on both engines. That digest moving is the fix; the `CHECK` widening is the enabling half.
  3. **The `SQLite` rebuild moved ten triggers across three tables, not four across one.** `ALTER TABLE … RENAME TO` re-parses the whole schema, so the three `pricing_price_overlay_line` and three `pricing_price_overlay_line_amount` triggers — each sub-selecting this table's `lifecycle_state` — fail the rename after the `DROP TABLE`, and are dropped before the rebuild and restated verbatim after it. This is `m20260802_000019`'s lesson exactly (*"a rebuild's census cannot stop at the table being rebuilt"*), met a second time; `PRAGMA legacy_alter_table` was rejected for that entry's reason. The entry predicted one migration and was right, and predicted its size and was not.
- **One guard became unobservable, and it is named rather than lost**: `pricing_price_overlay_line`'s composite foreign key was proved on its own by a case that deleted a **draft** parent — the one state no trigger stood in front of. With `DELETE` refused everywhere the key is shadowed on every path, so no test can assert it acting. It is kept (it still declares the relationship, and a later narrowing of the trigger would find it behind), and the case now asserts the trigger's refusal with the loss stated in its own doc.
- **Test movement**: `sqlite_overlay_repo` 27 → 30 (the re-mint case inverted to `an_abandoned_revision_number_is_never_re_minted`, plus the freed open-draft slot, terminality driven past the repository, and the draft `DELETE` refusal); `sqlite_overlay_store` 36 → 38 (deletability inverted to every state, the `draft → abandoned` flip and its terminality, and the partial open-draft index that lets the tombstone keep its number without keeping the slot). Two trigger-body digests re-pinned in `sqlite_migrations`, both of them mine, with every other digest in the census unchanged.
- **Propagated**: S9 §6 (`lifecycle_state`); S1 §2 (`DraftStateMachine`, which said the flip was the *plan's*) and its deletability rule in the same entry, which said only price rows were undeletable and left the overlay as the one place the no-re-mint rule did not hold. `pricing_price_overlay` has no §3.7 row in Foundation, so there is no third target — checked rather than assumed.

#### D-232 [M] D-104's two bundle triggers are declared by functions nothing calls

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/bundle.rs` (`composition_change_set`, `rev_share_change_set`); `pricing/src/api/rest/bundles.rs` (`publish_bundle`); [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-ba-material`; D-104; [D-209](#d-209-m-the-two-bundle-triggers-subject-exists-in-this-repository-now).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 while measuring whether the overlay trigger's flip was earned): both functions build the change set that declares a bundle act, and **neither has a caller anywhere in the crate**. `publish_bundle` evaluates no materiality verdict at all. A registered trigger of this kind is an **act**, and the act half is read back off a change set some surface declared — so a trigger nobody constructs can never be answered by the evaluator, however many tables its subject has. D-104's rule is therefore **not enforced** on the bundle publish route: the entry exists because *"with a threshold configured the evaluator saw nothing to trip on and a component swap reached consumers with no approver, while a $1 price-row change above threshold took two people"*, and that is the state the route is in.
- **It is partly masked, and the masking is worth stating so the severity is not over-read.** A recomposition that moves **no price row** is caught as D-115's pure-shape revision through the *plan* publish path. One that **moves rows** falls through to the threshold rules — which is D-104's own scenario.
- **This corrects [D-209](#d-209-m-the-two-bundle-triggers-subject-exists-in-this-repository-now)'s premise rather than its decision.** That entry flipped both triggers' `subject_exists_in_this_crate` on the ground that the subject is here — four tables, four entities, the revision lifecycle, *"`infra::bundle`'s two `ChangeSet::of_act` declarations"* and three mounted routes. The subject **is** here and the predicate is about the subject, so the flip stands; what does not stand is reading those two functions as declarations. A `pub fn` that builds a change set is not a surface declaring an act.
- **Options**: (a) `publish_bundle` evaluates the verdict and acts on it, which means deciding what a **material** bundle publish does — the same approval seam [D-225](#d-225-m-the-overlay-submits-approval-and-publish-units-are-not-wired) leaves open one slice over; (b) delete the two constructors as dead code and record D-104 as unenforced; (c) leave both.
- **Recommendation**: (a). (b) is honest about the code and abandons the rule; (c) is the state that let this go unnoticed, because two functions that exist read as coverage to anyone who greps for the trigger.
- **Decision**: owed. **Not built** — wiring it is a slice's worth of work on the bundle approval seam, not a clause of the overlay merge that found it. `triggers_tests::only_the_triggers_with_a_subject_in_this_crate_answer_true` says so at the roster rather than only here.
- **Propagated**: S8 `inst-ba-material`.

#### D-233 [M] The overlay submit declares its act, and that is what earns the trigger's flip

- **Where**: `pricing/src/api/rest/overlays.rs` (`overlay_submit_materiality`); `pricing/src/domain/materiality/triggers.rs`; [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `inst-pl-commit`; D-50.
- **Problem**: Slice 9's hand-back owed the merge `Trigger::PriceOverlayMutation`'s flip to `subject_exists_in_this_crate`, on [D-209](#d-209-m-the-two-bundle-triggers-subject-exists-in-this-repository-now)'s ground. Measured first: the submit route wrote its materiality token as a **literal**, and the whole crate contained no construction of the act's change set — so the flip would have claimed a trigger the evaluator could never answer. That is the mistake the trigger registry's own module doc records for the grandfathering cutover, whose store landed three commits before its declaration and which stayed `false` throughout.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-06 — the declaration lands first and the flip follows it.** `overlay_submit_materiality` evaluates over the declared act and renders the verdict's **own** reason token, which is `api::rest::threshold_policy::policy_diff_materiality`'s shape for its stated reason: two units compared by a reader should be two answers from one evaluator, not one answer and one literal. It passes no policy and no baseline because the evaluator examines the act half above every threshold, so D-50 holds by construction.
- **The literal and the evaluator agreed on the string, and nothing but coincidence kept them agreeing.** Renaming the materiality vocabulary's token now reddens the overlay route's case; with the literal in place the same rename left every case green.
- **Propagated**: S9 `inst-pl-commit` (the materiality the 202 carries).

#### D-234 [M] An overlay publish unit is not a `PlanPublishUnit`, and cannot be one

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `inst-pl-commit` (*"the approved overlay is then a **publish unit through the Foundation engine** (D-06)"*); D-06; D-112.
- **Problem** (measured 2026-08-06 while building [D-225](#d-225-m-the-overlay-submits-approval-and-publish-units-are-not-wired)'s approval half): the engine's unit type is **plan-shaped** — a plan id, one of its revisions, and a kind discriminator — and both its constructors name a plan fact. An overlay has no plan, so an overlay publish unit cannot be a value of that type, and the second half of `inst-pl-commit` is therefore a **new pipeline** rather than a third constructor. That is why the approval half landed alone.
- **What is already in place, so the gap is narrower than it looks**: the read model's `subject_kind` CHECK already admits `price_overlay` **and** `overlay_index`, so the store is ready for both subjects D-112 requires an overlay publish to re-project. What is missing is the requester: nothing asks the registry for a `CatalogVersion` on an overlay's behalf, and nothing warms those two subjects.
- **Options**: (a) widen the unit type with an overlay variant and let the engine dispatch on it; (b) give the overlay plane its own publish path that requests a `CatalogVersion` and projects its two subjects, leaving the plan engine untouched; (c) ride the overlay's publish on the targeted plans' units.
- **Recommendation**: (b). (a) puts a non-plan subject inside a type whose every field is a plan fact, which is the shape that made this measurable in the first place. (c) is wrong on the design set's own terms — an overlay's lines may target several plans or none, so there is no unit to ride, and D-112 added the tenant `overlay_index` subject precisely because per-plan resolution gave overlay *enumeration* no access path.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-07 — (b), and it is built, end to end.** Six groups: the store's multi-subject key, the payload vocabulary, the projector's two overlay arms, the commit, and the route's second act. `POST /bss-pricing/v1/price-overlays/{overlayId}/submit` now opens the unit when no approved one matches this revision **and this content**, and commits when one does — so **an approved overlay publishes**, and D-225's second half is closed with it. *(This line said "the commit half is not built" for the length of one docs wave, which was true when written and false four commits later; it is corrected rather than left, because a register entry describing a pipeline that exists as one that does not is the same defect as one describing the reverse.)*
- **Four things the build had to decide, each measured rather than assumed**:
  1. **The pipeline mints no route and no authz action.** S9 §5's API table already spells the submit route as *"Submit the draft — always-material Slice 5 approval unit (D-50), **then the D-06 publish unit**"*, so the publish arm is `POST /plans/{planId}/publish`'s two-act shape one plane over rather than a new path; and the S5 authz catalog gives `price_overlay` exactly `write` and `read`, which D-50 states as a decision — *"`price_overlay × write` still authors, but the commit routes through the material approval workflow before its D-06 publish unit fires"*. The two-person property is the approval unit's, not a second action's. Minting `price_overlay × publish` would be inventing surface, the same discipline as inventing a wire code.
  2. **One act, one registry request, one handle, N subject rows.** `pricing_catalog_version_ref` was keyed `(tenant_id, pending_ref)` and this unit records two subjects, three when a revision moves the scope value (D-133) — so `m20260802_000036` widens the key. **Two handles is not the way out**: the registry batches (D-47), so two handles of one act may resolve to two versions, and a pin landing between them resolves the overlay document as live while the shard at or below it does not list it. `m20260802_000004`'s own doc had named this as owed; what that paragraph got wrong is *which* thing could not hold two subjects — it blamed the `subject_revision`/`subject_lifecycle_state` pair, which is per row, where the obstacle was the key.
  3. **The finalize is per subject, not per handle.** The projector finalizes a ref inside the transaction that projects *that* subject; handle-wide, a sibling whose own projection failed would be committed, unwarm, and therefore absent from the pending scan — so no later pass would ever offer its version again, and the version would stand incomplete with the frontier stuck behind it.
  4. **The overlay arm pins no lifecycle state on the ref.** D-128 pins a plan's because sellability predicate (4) reads it at the pin and the row's own state keeps moving; an overlay revision's state rides inside the immutable document it freezes, so a second pinning would be two places for one fact to disagree.
- **Two things the build did not do, and both were owed rather than absent by choice** (2026-08-08: **(2) is discharged**, see below; **(1) alone is still owed**):
  1. **§4.2's second validation run.** The commit enforces the **content** pin inside its transaction, which is the half that keeps a reviewer's decision honest; §4.2 also re-validates *state* against a world that moved, and the overlay rule set's world comes from `overlay_repo::world_for`, which takes a provider rather than a runner. So a target plan retired or a scope value withdrawn between the submit and the commit is not caught at commit time. The submit's own run catches it at submit time, which makes this a **narrowed window** — D-47's batching delay plus the reviewer's thinking time — rather than an absent check. **Built 2026-08-08, and the stated blocker was narrower than this entry recorded**: `world_for` is provider-bound, but all four fact readers underneath it (`declares_selector`, `plan_facts`, `price_facts`, `overlay_facts`) have taken `&impl DBRunner` since they were written, so the world was always assemblable inside a transaction and only the entry point said otherwise. Split out as `overlay_repo::world_on` and run on the **transaction** — not a fresh connection, which could read a world a concurrent publish invalidates before the flip lands — as step **1b**, after the content pin and **before** the registry request, so a refusal leaves no pending handle to trip `commit_overdue`. The refusals are the submit's own two tiers: `PRECEDENCE_DUPLICATE`/`OVERLAY_INTERVAL_OVERLAP` lifted out as 409s, everything else the aggregate 422; warnings do not block, since `EQUAL_PRECEDENCE_CROSS_CLASS_TIE` and `FIXED_LINE_DISCARDS_STACK` are things an operator is told rather than things a commit refuses.
     - **It found two fixtures on its first run**, which is the check earning its place before it ever saw production: `sqlite_read_model`'s two overlay seeds scope themselves to `partner/acme` and **never declared that value in the taxonomy**, so both drove a commit that published an overlay the submit route refuses outright with `SCOPE_VALUE_UNKNOWN` (D-120). They passed only because the commit ran no world check. The seeds now declare the value — a fixture fix, not an accommodation, and the same class as the 2026-08-07 probe that found a fixture unable to reach its claimed state.
     - **And a probe found the new case's own assertion vacuous**, which is the second time this discipline has paid on this branch. The case asserts a refused commit "writes nothing"; a probe moving the run to *after* the registry request reddened **nothing**, because a refused commit rolls its database writes back either way — the revision stays a draft and no ref row survives, whatever the ordering. What the ordering actually protects is outside the transaction: a `request_version` already issued is not rolled back, and a pending handle nobody commits is exactly what trips `commit_overdue`. The case now asserts the registry was never asked (`issued_count() == 0`), and the same probe reddens it.
     - A stale claim corrected with it: `api::rest::overlays` already said *"§4.2 runs the same set again inside the publish commit"*, which was false for the length of one wave.
  2. **No wire event.** The frozen `CatalogEvent` set names none for an overlay publish, and a wire event is the design set's to declare (D-218's posture), so none was minted. **Closed 2026-08-07 by [D-248](#d-248-m-an-overlay-publish-moves-two-projected-subjects-and-announces-neither), and this residue is discharged**: the name arrived as a decision extending `fr-event-contract` to fourteen — which is what the deferral was waiting for, not a gap in this pipeline — and `PriceOverlayPublished` is emitted at the commit's step 6, aggregated on the **overlay id** (an overlay may target no plan at all), dedup key `PriceOverlayPublished:<overlay_id>:<revision>`. It cost `m20260802_000060`, the set having turned out to be frozen in the schema as well. Only residue (1) is still owed.
- **Where the route lives, and why it moved.** `AuthoringState`'s criterion is *which of the two states may request a `CatalogVersion`*, and the publish arm requests one — so the submit route moved to the **governance** router rather than the criterion being bent. That is where the plan plane's identical two-act route has always been. The path is unchanged, so no §5 row and no authz catalog row moves.
- **Propagated**: S9 `inst-pl-commit`.

#### D-235 [M] The `overlay_index` shard is the set live **at V**, and it is derived from the ref table

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §7 (the D-112 paragraph, as D-133 narrowed it); `pricing/src/infra/read_model.rs` (`project_overlay_index_subject`); `pricing/src/infra/storage/repo/catalog_version_ref_repo.rs` (`overlay_revisions_at_or_below`); D-112; D-133; D-47.
- **Problem** (measured 2026-08-07 while building [D-234](#d-234-m-an-overlay-publish-unit-is-not-a-planpublishunit-and-cannot-be-one)'s projection half): §7 states the *requirement* — enumeration yields *"every scope-matching overlay **live at V**"*, followed by *"one per-subject document read per matching overlay"* — and never states the **mechanism**. The obvious implementation reads the overlay table, which answers a different question: what is published **now**. The projector arrives up to D-47's five-minute batching maximum after the commit, so a second overlay publishing inside that window lands in the *earlier* version's frozen shard; a consumer pinned at `V` then enumerates an overlay whose document has no delta at or below `V`, and the document read the same sentence prescribes finds nothing. It is §4.4's "current revision is the wrong source" defect a third time (after D-128 and the plan subject's own), and the loudest of the three, because enumeration and resolution are two reads one consumer makes in one breath. Permanent, on an INSERT-only row.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-07 — derive the shard from `pricing_catalog_version_ref`, not from the overlay table, and not from a new column.** shard(class, value, `V`) is every overlay holding a `price_overlay` ref at `catalog_version ≤ V`, taken at its **greatest** such ref, carrying the scope, precedence and interval of the revision *that* ref pinned, kept only if that revision's scope still falls in this shard. "Greatest such ref" is Foundation §4.4's own greatest-completed-≤-pin rule, applied to choose which revision the **index** speaks of rather than which delta a reader resolves — and it delivers D-133's *"two shards when a revision moves the scope value"* with no special case, since an overlay appears under whichever shard its greatest-≤-`V` revision names.
- **A column was not an option and the reason is size, not taste.** The shard payload is O(live overlays), which is exactly what D-133 sharded and horizon-bounded rather than widened; the plan plane's answer to the same question was a column because a plan subject pins one revision.
- **One clause is load-bearing and is not obvious: the version's own refs count, though they are not finalized yet.** The two subjects of one act are projected in **two transactions**, and the pending scan orders by `requested_at` then `pending_ref` — which ties on both for one act, because the commit writes both refs at one instant. So the order is genuinely arbitrary, and a shard derived from committed refs alone would omit **its own sibling** whenever it was projected first. Ordering the subjects does not fix it: a document whose projection failed would leave the shard warm, wrong and never re-derived, because only the document is re-driven.
- **One premise is written down rather than proved**, and it was a probe that established which: the `≤ V` bound is exercised by **nothing**. What keeps a later version's overlays out today is the `IS NOT NULL` filter beside it — a version is projected while every later version's refs are still pending, since the sweep walks versions ascending — and the reverse order is unreachable, because D-114's prefix closure means the frontier would already have passed the earlier version and `refuse_projection_below_frontier` refuses that loudly. The bound stays because it states the rule; whoever changes the sweep's ordering, or adds a re-drive reaching a version below a committed sibling, makes it the only guard.
- **D-133's horizon `H` is absent**, and it is the same absence D-121's is rather than a second one: `H` has no producer in this tree at all. The set it would bound is the tenant's **live** overlays rather than an accumulation, because a revision leaves the index only by being superseded by its own successor — which replaces it in the set rather than adding to it.
- **Propagated**: S9 §7 (the D-112/D-133 paragraph, where the mechanism belongs beside the requirement).

#### D-236 [M] No roster pinned any table's primary key, so a key change passed every store census — **DECIDED and BUILT 2026-08-07**

- **Where**: `pricing/tests/sqlite_migrations.rs` (`EXPECTED_TABLES`, `EXPECTED_TRIGGERS`, `EXPECTED_INDEXES`, `EXPECTED_CHECKS`, `EXPECTED_TRIGGER_BODIES`); `pricing/tests/postgres_migrations.rs`'s four; `m20260802_000036`.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-06 while building [D-234](#d-234-m-an-overlay-publish-unit-is-not-a-planpublishunit-and-cannot-be-one)'s store half): this gear's store-obligation surfaces are described as six and as having been right every time — and **a primary key is not among the things any roster watches**. `m20260802_000036` changes the physical identity of a truth-linkage table on the seven-year horizon, and every roster in both migration suites passed unchanged. The reverse is equally silent: a later rebuild that restated a table and dropped a key column would be caught by nothing, and the first symptom would be a duplicate row in a table whose whole contract is that it has none.
- **It is narrower than "untested", and the correction matters** — the first version of this finding said *no* obligation surface fired at all, and that was false. `postgres_schema_stores::one_pending_ref_is_one_row_per_tenant` pins this table's key **behaviourally**, by driving a duplicate insert and expecting `pricing_catalog_version_ref_pkey`, and it failed exactly as it should. What is missing is the **roster**: the enumeration a reader consults to ask "what are this chain's keys", and the gate that fires when the answer moves. And the pin that caught it is `#[ignore]`d behind Docker, so the fast tier stayed green through the whole change. That is this gear's standing "one premise duplicated across tiers breaks in instalments" hazard arriving from its sharpest direction — the premise lived on **one** tier only, so the Docker-gated suite held the entire gate and a run without Docker would have reported a clean change.
- **Options**: (a) a sixth roster over every table's key, mirrored on both backends the way the CHECK-name roster is; (b) a per-table pin written whenever a key is touched; (c) leave it.
- **Recommendation**: (a). The existing rosters earn their place by being **complete** — a trigger absent from the roster fails the suite — and a key roster is the same shape of statement over the same DDL. (b) is what this build did as a stopgap (`the_ref_tables_key_carries_the_subject_as_well_as_the_handle`, on the fast tier so the next reader needs no Docker) and it has the defect every per-item pin has: it protects the table somebody remembered.
- **Decision (2026-08-07): option (a), and it is built.** `EXPECTED_PRIMARY_KEYS` is a sixth roster on both engines: `sqlite_migrations.rs` reads `PRAGMA table_info`'s `pk` **ordinal** (a position within the key, not a boolean) and `postgres_migrations.rs` reads `unnest(conkey) WITH ORDINALITY` — both order the columns by the key's own order, so a composite key *rearranged* into a different key reads as a change rather than as the same set. 31 tables, and the two engines agree column for column and in the same order, which is itself a first measurement: nothing had compared them.
- **Where it is asserted**: the `SQLite` half runs on the **fast tier**, at both census sites — the first `up` and the `down`-then-`up` re-run. The second site is the direction this entry calls equally silent, and it had no coverage at all: no `sqlite_rebuild!` expansion was ever checked for a key.
- **The probe took two attempts, and the first one proved less than it appeared to.** Narrowing `pricing_catalog_version_ref`'s key reddened the new census *and* the stopgap pin `the_ref_tables_key_carries_the_subject_as_well_as_the_handle` — that table is precisely the one the stopgap remembered, so the roster demonstrated nothing new on it. The second probe dropped `subject_ref` from `pricing_read_model`'s key, a table no per-table pin covers: **only the two new census sites reddened.** Tables, triggers, indexes, `CHECK`s, trigger bodies and the `SeaORM` entity round-trip all stayed green, which is this entry's claim demonstrated rather than asserted.
- **The roster was seeded from the live schema and then hand-checked**, per the standing rule that a pin taken from the code's own output pins the bug. Four keys are named by a decision and were read back against the migration that declares them: `pricing_catalog_version_ref` (`m20260802_000036` — the change this entry is about), `pricing_read_model` (`m20260802_000003`), `pricing_price_overlay_line` (`m20260802_000033`, D-42) and `pricing_plan` (`m20260802_000001`, D-145). The note beside the roster records which four and why.
- **The stopgap stays.** `the_ref_tables_key_carries_the_subject_as_well_as_the_handle` is behavioural where the roster is structural, and the first probe is the argument for keeping both: it is the one that fires on a duplicate row rather than on a DDL string.
- **No design-set propagation**, and it is spelled out rather than claimed as `none`: the rosters are a test surface this gear built for itself and no §3.7 clause states them, so what is wrong is the gate rather than any document.

#### D-237 [M] `PlanPublishDegraded` is plan-only by what the event **is**, not because other subjects cannot occur

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/jobs/readmodel_warm.rs::mark_degraded` (the `subject_kind != Plan` refusal and the comment justifying it); `pricing/src/infra/read_model.rs`'s projector dispatch; `pricing/src/domain/events.rs` (the 13 frozen names); `design/09-price-overlays.md` §7.
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-07): the refusal was justified with *"the other three kinds have no store in this gear, so no publish unit here can have produced one, and the projector refuses them by name"*. **Two thirds of that is now false.** The projector dispatches `SubjectKind::PriceOverlay` and `SubjectKind::OverlayIndex`; only `GroupMembership` is refused by name. `OverlayPublishService::commit` writes one `pricing_catalog_version_ref` row per subject, and — measured, because the finding turns on it — `catalog_version_ref_repo::list_pending_for_tenant` filters on tenant and `catalog_version IS NULL` and **carries no subject-kind predicate**. So overlay rows genuinely reach `mark_degraded` and are refused there, silently: `report.degraded_emitted` never counts them.
- **The consequence the finding predicts does not follow, and measuring that is the decision.** The same stuck row raises `pricing.readmodel.pin_eligibility_overdue` — a **Critical** — by two mechanisms that are both subject-agnostic *by construction* rather than by intent: a version is incomplete exactly while it holds a pending ref (that query again), so the overlay row holds the tenant's frontier; and the alarm's `waiting` predicate reads `tenant_id` and `requested_at` and nothing else. The module's own doc already states the invariant this rests on — *"either failing state also holds the frontier and is reported by `pin_eligibility_overdue`"*.
- **Options**: (a) plan-only is correct and the comment is merely stale — repair the comment, no behaviour change; (b) degradation is subject-generic — widen `PlanPublishDegradedPayload` off `plan_id`, which changes a **frozen** event's contract for Tariffs / Rating / Subscriptions / Billing; (c) a new frozen event name, with the outbox `CHECK` migration and a reconciliation against §7's *"No new frozen event names"*.
- **Decision: (a)**, and the reason is the event's addressee rather than the cost. `PlanPublishDegraded` names a `plan_id` and what it adds over `commit_overdue` is **which of two waits is failing**, not which subject; the subject-generic hazard already has a subject-generic Critical. And there is nothing an overlay consumer could act on in a per-overlay copy: the frontier does not advance past an incomplete version, so no pin ever resolves one whose overlay is unwarmed — the overlay has not taken effect, which is the safe reading. (b) widens a frozen payload for four consuming gears to carry a distinction none of them can act on. (c) buys the same non-actionable distinction and contradicts §7 outright.
- **Built**: the comment now states that reason, and `sqlite_read_model::an_overlay_subjects_stuck_warm_raises_the_frontier_alarm_and_no_degraded_event` pins **both** halves — the silence *and* the alarm that makes it acceptable, because the silence alone would pass equally against a pass that alarmed on nothing.
- **No design-set propagation.** §4.4 and §7 both stand as written; what was wrong was a doc comment, and the class it belongs to is the one this program keeps finding — a paragraph explaining why a design is right, written at the moment of least evidence and read as verification ever after.

#### D-238 [M] Two Critical alarms the design set names by string sit off the alarm plane the gear now has — **DECIDED and BUILT 2026-08-07**

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/jobs/readmodel_warm.rs` (`ALARM_COMMIT_OVERDUE`, `ALARM_PIN_ELIGIBILITY_OVERDUE`); `pricing/src/domain/ports/metrics.rs` (`PricingAlarm::ALL`).
- **Problem** (found 2026-08-07 while repairing [D-237](#d-237-m-planpublishdegraded-is-plan-only-by-what-the-event-is-not-because-other-subjects-cannot-occur)'s comment, and it is the **same defect class one file over**): this module justified emitting `pricing.catalogversion.commit_overdue` (§3.6) and `pricing.readmodel.pin_eligibility_overdue` (§4.4) as bare `tracing::error!` with *"this gear has no metrics or alarm facility at all"*. Slice 4 falsified it — `domain/ports/metrics.rs` + `infra/metrics.rs` now carry the ledger's port-and-adapter pair and a `PricingAlarm` roster. `PricingAlarm::ALL` holds Slice 4's two and neither of these, so the gear's **only two Critical** alarms are the two not on its alarm plane.
- **Options**: (a) add both to the roster and raise them through the port beside the `tracing::error!`; (b) add them and drop the log; (c) leave them as strings and record that the roster is deliberately per-slice.
- **Recommendation**: (a). The roster's own doc says a slice adds its alarms **when it wires them**, which is why they were not there — but the wiring is now one call, and a Critical that an operator can only find by grepping logs is the state the plane was opened to end. Keeping the log alongside costs nothing and is what the e2e boot without a registry reads.
- **Decision (2026-08-07): option (a), and it is built** — `b0516ea83`. `PricingAlarm` gains `CatalogVersionCommitOverdue` and `ReadModelPinEligibilityOverdue`, both `AlarmSeverity::Critical`, both in `ALL`, both raised through the port beside the retained `tracing::error!`.
- **Correction, 2026-08-07**: this entry read *"owed. **Not built**"* for the rest of the day **after the commit that built it**, and its title said "owed, not decided". Both were false from `b0516ea83` onward. The same defect was found in the review wave the same day (`1a81dc519`: two sentences still claiming D-211's delegation was owed, written before the delegation and outliving it), and the lesson taken there applies to its author too — **a decision that is built and an entry that still says "owed" is worse than an entry that never claimed anything**, because a reader who greps for owed work is told to do it twice. The build commit must carry the register flip, not a later sweep. Caught by grepping the register for owed markers when asked whether any tasks remained, which is a poor substitute for the entry being right when it was written.

#### D-239 [M] One refusal, two declared codes — split by **surface**, not by class (`T-1`)

- **Where**: `design/04-currency-tax.md` §5 (`BRAND_UNKNOWN` / `PARTNER_UNKNOWN` / `ORG_TIER_UNKNOWN`, since D-120) versus `design/09-price-overlays.md` §5 (`SCOPE_VALUE_UNKNOWN`, D-222, three days later) — one rule, one rejection, two discriminators, and the older list is three codes for **four** classes.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): option (3).** `REGION_UNKNOWN` stays on the **price-row authoring** path — there `region` is a scope-key axis and the remedy is to fix the row, which §2 declares independently of any overlay. `SCOPE_VALUE_UNKNOWN` is the sole code on the **overlay scope** path, where the remedy is identical for all four classes (declare the value in the named universe) and the class rides the violation's `subject`/message. **This is what the crate already raises**, so the decision is a documentation act.
- **Propagation — landed in `e620f2ace`, the commit that built this decision**: `BRAND_UNKNOWN` / `PARTNER_UNKNOWN` / `ORG_TIER_UNKNOWN` are struck from `04-currency-tax.md` §5 and `inst-tx-brand`, which now names `SCOPE_VALUE_UNKNOWN` for every taxonomy-backed class; D-222's justification stays as provenance but is annotated: it read that §2 *"names no discriminator"*, true of Slice 9's own §2 and false of the set.
- **Correction, measured 2026-08-07 by running spec-check after the strike**: this entry first said *"spec-check's `code-unreferenced` debt for the three is correct"*. **It was correct for one of the three.** Only `BRAND_UNKNOWN` was ever a pinned member — `PARTNER_UNKNOWN` and `ORG_TIER_UNKNOWN` were named in `inst-tx-brand`'s own prose as well as declared in §5, so P3 saw them referenced and they never entered the debt. The strike therefore paid the pin down by **one**, 59 → 58, not by three. The general lesson, and the reason this is recorded rather than quietly edited: **a claim about what a tool measures is a claim to be measured, not inferred from the document.** The standing caution against paying a pin with a bare mention (`REGENERATE.md` entry 15) has a mirror — a mention can also *hide* a code from the debt it belongs in, which is what happened to these two for a week.

#### D-240 [M] `pricing_policy_object.tax_display_mode` is retired (`T-2`)

- **Where**: `m20260802_000006_create_pricing_policy_object.rs` — `tax_display_mode text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'tax_exclusive'` under `chk_pricing_policy_object_tax_display`.
- **Problem**: it carries a display **basis** default under the name §6 uses for an enforcement **mode**; nothing in `src/` reads it (no accessor, no domain type, no rule — only the two migrations that create it and three fixtures); and `01-foundation.md` §3.7 never declares it. Slice 4 added the column §6 actually asks for beside it (`tax_display_policy_mode`, `fail_closed | warn`, `m20260802_000038`).
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): retire it — drop the column.** An implementation invention no document requests, sitting on the name a document does request, next to the real switch. Leaving it is a second thing a reader must learn is dead.
- **Cost, stated rather than discovered**: a **destructive** change to a Foundation-owned table, and on `SQLite` a create-copy-drop-rename rebuild that takes every trigger, index and CHECK on `pricing_policy_object` with it. `m20260802_000019` and `m20260802_000035` are the worked examples, and `sqlite_migrations` pins trigger **bodies**, so a rebuild that lost one reddens there.
- **Built** (2026-08-07, `m20260802_000041`, both engines). The premise this entry was written on held **and the hazard flagged before building was real**: `pricing_policy_object` had already been rebuilt once, at `m20260802_000018`, so the current `SQLite` text is *that* rebuild plus `m20260802_000038`'s appended column — **not** `m20260802_000006`'s. A restatement written from the creating migration would have silently dropped the C4 switch. Postgres drops the column and its CHECK in place. Cost measured lower than feared: the table carries **no trigger**, so no digest of the roster's 67 moved.
- **The inventory in the Problem above is wrong and is corrected here rather than quietly**: not *"the two migrations that create it and three test fixtures"* but **six** touch points across four files, plus the `SeaORM` entity field and the CHECK name in **both** roster censuses — `entity/policy_object.rs`, `sqlite_policy_object.rs`, `sqlite_approval_threshold.rs` (a *witness* `SELECT`, not a fixture), and `postgres_schema_stores.rs` ×3. What the decision actually rests on — *nothing in `src/` **reads** it* — held under measurement; it was the count that was short, and the full set was found by census **before** editing rather than one at a time as the compiler complained.
- **Found while building, and it is the reason the retirement was not a pure deletion**: the *surviving* C4 switch `tax_display_policy_mode` — the one §6 declares — had **no behavioural coverage on either engine**, only its name in the two roster censuses, while the column being retired carried a dedicated refusal case for its token set. Deleting that case with its column would have left the living switch unproven everywhere. The case **moved one column over**, and a second case pins that the retired column is no longer a column at all.

#### D-241 [L] The `orgTier` path segment becomes `org_tier` (`T-4`)

- **Where**: `design/04-currency-tax.md` §5's `GET/PUT /bss-pricing/v1/config/taxonomies/{region|brand|partner|orgTier}` against `ScopeClass::as_str()`, which renders the same class `org_tier` on the wire and in `pricing_price_overlay.scope_class`.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): option (2) — one spelling everywhere.** The strand recommended keeping §5 literal on the grounds that a path segment is not a JSON field and no rule of the set requires them to agree; the owner's call is that an OpenAPI-generated client carrying **both** spellings for one class is the cost that decides it, and the gear is not deployed, so nothing breaks.
- **Propagation — all four landed in `e620f2ace`**, verified one at a time on 2026-08-07 rather than as a group: (i) the route's path segment is `{class}` and `TaxonomyScopeClass::path_segment` **returns `scope_class().as_str()`**, so the token cannot drift from the class it names — the rule is structural, not a table of four strings; (ii) the OpenAPI operations are built on `/bss-pricing/v1/config/taxonomies/{class}` for both `GET` and `PUT`; (iii) `rest_taxonomies.rs` builds every URL as `TAXONOMY.replace("{class}", class)` from the crate constant — **stronger than the "pinned paths" this line asked for in one direction and weaker in the other**: the test can no longer disagree with the route, and equally can no longer catch the route changing, which is the honest trade and is recorded rather than glossed; (iv) §5's route row spells `{region|brand|partner|org_tier}` and cites D-241 by name for why the camelCase `orgTier` it used to carry is refused rather than aliased. Option (3) — accepting both — stays refused for the register's reason: two spellings that both work is the state in which neither is canonical.

#### D-242 [L] The taxonomy `value` CHECK is tightened to reject whitespace (`T-6`)

- **Where**: `m20260802_000028`…`000031` on both engines — `CHECK (length(value) > 0)` — against `ScopeValue::new`, which **trims** before deciding.
- **Problem, measured rather than reasoned**: `'   '` has `length > 0`, so the store admits it and the domain refuses it; the insert landed on both engines. `TaxonomyRepo::list` maps a value `ScopeValue` refuses to `RepoError::CorruptRow`, so **one** whitespace row makes `GET /config/taxonomies/{class}` fail for **every** value in that class, and the only remedy is direct SQL — the `PUT` cannot round-trip a list it cannot read.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): option (1)** — `length(btrim(value)) > 0`, four tables, both engines. It is the only one of the three that stops the bad row existing rather than coping with it, and it makes the store agree with the domain type, which is the invariant that was intended. Option (2) stays refused: a read that silently dropped values would make the next `PUT` retire them.
- **Cost**: a `CHECK` change is a `SQLite` create-copy-drop-rename **per table** — four rebuilds, each under the roster's four censuses.
- **⚠ The predicate above is the Postgres spelling only, and this entry was not executable as written.** Postgres has `btrim(text)`; **`SQLite` has no `btrim` at all** and spells the same operation `trim(X)`. A chain built literally from *"`length(btrim(value)) > 0`, four tables, both engines"* dies at `CREATE TABLE` with `no such function`. Each arm takes its own engine's name — `btrim` on Postgres, `trim` on `SQLite` — and `m20260802_000042`'s own *Backend differences* section says so. Recorded here rather than silently fixed in the code, because this is the **decision text** being wrong, not an implementation slip.
- **Built** (2026-08-07, `m20260802_000042`, four tables, both engines). Postgres replaces each constraint in place; `SQLite` rebuilds each of the four. **Every constraint name is deliberately kept**, so neither engine's CHECK census moved and none of the roster's 67 trigger digests moved either — the four taxonomy tables carry no trigger. Measured per table rather than inferred: unlike `pricing_policy_object`, each of the four is named in **exactly one** migration, its own, so each rebuild is written from its creator.
- **The stated purpose stands, and the hole actually closed is a different one** — worth keeping straight because the constraint's own doc argues the first: `length(value) > 0` was written to keep the classless-scope sentinel unforgeable, and `'   '` never threatened that (`pricing_price_overlay` renders the classless scope as the empty string exactly, so `inst-plv-scope` was never at risk). What the whitespace row cost is one level up — `TaxonomyRepo::list` maps a value `ScopeValue` refuses to `RepoError::CorruptRow`, so a single such row fails `GET /config/taxonomies/{class}` for the **whole class**.

#### D-243 [M] The retire guard's TOCTOU resolves on the publish-side rule, and the ordering is stated (`T-9`)

- **Where**: `taxonomy_repo::apply_replace` — `list_on`, then N × `references_to`, then `write_set`, all plain `SELECT`s with no row lock inside a bare `BEGIN` (READ COMMITTED on Postgres).
- **Problem**: nothing serialises the guard's count against a transaction concurrently creating a reference. T1 retires `eu` seeing zero published rows while T2 publishes a row in `eu`; both commit, and a published row sits on a retired region. **The entry was written while this was unreachable** — `RegionsDeclared` was registered nowhere, so a publish did not consult the taxonomy at all. `476668ad8` registered it, so the hole is now real.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): option (3)** — accept the race and let the publish-side rule carry it. `inst-tx-region` refuses a row whose region is not `active`, so the row T2 published would be refused on its own merits at its next publish, and the race resolves to *whichever committed first wins; the loser is refused next time*. Option (1) locks a set that is large at C1's 20-currency floor and makes a config write block publishes; option (2) buys a retry, not a refusal.
- **Owed**: the ordering must be **stated** in the design set rather than left as an emergent property, and pinned by a case — the whole reason this entry exists is so the outcome is chosen rather than discovered.

#### D-244 [L] The preview quotes the terminal phase's `all_subscriptions` recurring row (`T-12`)

- **Where**: `design/04-currency-tax.md` §2 — *"Returns the catalog **base list price** for a `(region, currency)`"* — against a market that legitimately holds many rows, since `phase`, `chargeKind`, `meter` and `dimensionKey` are all scope-key axes.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): option (1)** — §2 names the row: the **terminal phase's `all_subscriptions` recurring** row. The preview's audience is prospective purchasers, so the honest answer is the row they would actually be charged first, and the set already has the vocabulary to say it. Option (2) turns a price preview into a price list and moves the question to every consumer; option (3) is what is built and the register calls it a placeholder.
- **Propagation — both landed**, §2's sentence in `ed0b9abd7` and the selection in the same commit, with the doc paragraph corrected again in `60e967749` after the review wave: §2 now names "the **terminal phase's `all_subscriptions` recurring** row", terminality structural (`convertsToPhaseId` null) and never `kind`; the selection is `base_amount_row` + `terminal_phase_id`. Determinism was not lost by the change; it stopped being arbitrary.
- **Two corrections to this line itself, 2026-08-07.** It said the selection lives in **`infra::preview`**; there is no `infra/preview.rs` — the surface is `api/rest/preview.rs`, and a propagation target named by the wrong path is a target nobody can check. And the naming predicate's `amountMinor` conjunct, which a review commit attacked as a live defect, is **unreachable**: `inst-mk-chargekind` (D-18) refuses `graduated` on a `recurring` row and `AMOUNT_PLACEMENT_INVALID` refuses `flat`/`per_unit` without an amount, so every publishable terminal recurring row carries one. The conjunct was dropped anyway as a no-op simplification; `60e967749` carries the refutation.

#### D-245 [M] The region taxonomy's `tax_category` is guarded against removal while a published row depends on it (`T-16`, and half of `T-13`)

- **Where**: `domain/tax_display.rs` — `TaxBasisComplete` ranges over every candidate row; `MarketBasisUniform` beside it excludes `ExistingGrandfathered` before grouping (measured: `:253` versus `:336`).
- **Problem**: an `existing_grandfathered` row published with `tax_category_ref = NULL` takes the region default; the tenant later drops that default — an unguarded column update, since the retire guard counts referencing **rows** and not **markers** — and every subsequent publish of that plan fails `TAX_BASIS_INCOMPLETE` on a row that is immutable, MUST NOT be superseded and cannot be `PATCH`ed.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): option (2)** — guard the marker, not the rule. Extend the retire guard's shape one column over: a region's `tax_category` may not be removed while a published row resolves through it. Option (1), mirroring D-132's carve-out onto the completeness arm, was declined because the two exclusions are not the same fact — D-132 excludes a generation whose *basis* is frozen and harmless, whereas a missing *category* is a descriptor element genuinely absent from something still published.
- **This also closes half of `T-13`**: the value can no longer vanish from under a frozen category. What it does not close is the value **changing**, which `ea8ebc29b` already handled by freezing the resolved category at the publish commit.

#### D-246 [M] `pricing_tax_not_sellable_ga` becomes an observable gauge with a callback over the read model (`T-17`, bullet 1)

- **Where**: `domain/ports/metrics.rs` / `infra/metrics.rs` — the instrument is declared and the adapter builds it; nothing writes it.
- **Problem**: the declared quantity is **catalog-wide** ("how many markets are gated now") and every path in this gear is per-plan. `8c5e10075` removed the per-plan write as a `[C]`: an un-dimensioned series written per pre-check is last-writer-wins across every plan and tenant, so a tax-exclusive plan checked after one gating five markets writes `0` and clears §7's alarm while those five stay gated.
- **Decision (owner, 2026-08-07): an observable gauge with a callback over the read model.** It is the only shape in which a catalog-wide quantity is honest — the exporter asks for the value at collection time and no publish claims to know other plans' state. A periodic job in the warm sweep was declined: it puts a catalog-wide scan on the warm path, whose whole contract is bounded per-tenant work (D-163).
- **That decision's mechanism was measured before it was built, and it does not exist as stated** (2026-08-07). `opentelemetry` 0.31 types the callback `pub type Callback<T> = Box<dyn Fn(&dyn AsyncInstrument<T>) + Send + Sync>` — **synchronous**, with the crate's own doc requiring it to *"complete in a finite amount of time"*. A read-model query is `async`, so the callback cannot perform it, and a blocking read there would put database latency on the exporter's collection path and stall every instrument behind it. Measured a second time from the other side: **no gear in this workspace uses an observable instrument at all**, so the *"seven gears already carry this pattern"* argument that justified opening the metrics plane (`T-17`) does not extend here — this would be the first, and there is no house shape to follow.
- **Refined (owner, same round): a cached value with a dedicated refresher.** The callback reads an `AtomicI64`, which satisfies the finite-time contract by construction; a small async task refreshes it on its own cadence. **This is not the periodic job that was declined, and the distinction is which path pays**: what was declined is a catalog-wide scan inside the *warm sweep*, whose contract is bounded per-tenant work (D-163). A dedicated refresher touches neither the warm path nor the publish path. The cost is a second background task and a value up to one interval stale — irrelevant for a backlog the PRD risk table sizes in months.
- **Not built.** The refresher's cadence is a value the §14 NFR set should ratify beside the gear's other bounds, rather than a constant chosen here.
- **Unchanged by this**: §7's Info alarm stays on the **counter**, which is well-defined from one plan. **Still owed and not decided here** — `PreviewFailClosed::NoPublishedVersion` conflating "never published" with "published, not yet warmed" (splitting it needs a fact the frontier read does not return), and `pricing.tax.readiness_divergent`, declared and unraised because its reconciliation is the future Tax Engine's contract.

## F.1 Open product forks (2026-07-29) — not decided by the review

These carry commercial flavour and are deliberately left for the product owner. Each is a real
gap, verified against the documents; none is blocked on more analysis.

| Fork | Question | Consequence of leaving it open |
|------|----------|-------------------------------|
| **Overlay stack floor** (D-67 residue) | When a legal stack drives the resolved price below zero, does Tariffs **clamp at 0** (+ operator alarm) or does pricing **reject the stack at publish**? | The catalog forbids negative price rows but currently publishes stacks that compute negative charges — a credit, in a gear whose credit machinery is explicitly deferred |
| ~~**`fixed` arithmetic**~~ | — | **CLOSED by D-138 (2026-08-01, CONFIRMED the same day)**: `markup` **adds**, `discount` **subtracts**, **`fixed` replaces** the running line amount — replacement forced by the rest of the model, since `markup`/`discount` already express additive absolute money via `magnitude_kind = amount` (D-08), so an additive `fixed` would duplicate `markup` with no distinct meaning. **This row stood open for two days and five waves after its own question was answered** (noticed 2026-08-03 while auditing the standing list at the phase boundary); the half of it that is genuinely still open was riding as a rider and now has its own row below |
| ~~**Overlay stack sort direction**~~ | **CLOSED by D-249 (2026-08-07): ascending** — lowest `precedence` first, most specific last, so `precedence` reads as *how late I speak* and composes with the class order the set already declares least-specific-first. The catalog's side is settled; **Tariffs' adoption is owed** and this row stays struck-through rather than deleted until it lands, on the D-48 pattern. It unblocked D-220 (option (a): D-138's warning wording is right as written, because ascending is the reading it presumed) and with it D-230 |
| ~~**Overlay × grandfathered rows**~~ | — | **CLOSED by D-78 (2026-07-30)**, on the recommended fail-safe: an optional `cohort` **filter** on the line key; unset ⇒ grandfathered rows are exempt, a value ⇒ the line reaches only that generation. Joint with Rating (adopted verbatim); the Rating-side adoption is owed |
| **"Immediate" membership re-resolution** | Delete the mode (all membership changes renewal-aligned; out-of-cycle = a plan change) or build it (a `reResolveFrom` instant Tariffs honours by re-composing affected snapshots)? | Today it is governed but inert: an approved immediate removal from a VIP group changes nothing until renewal — up to twelve months of leakage on a change everyone believes took effect. `inst-ms-pinned` states the opposite outcome 16 lines later |
| **Seat-scaled entitlement** (D-66(4) residue) | Does the launch product need per-seat quotas? | Subscriptions had already built toward a marker pricing does not publish; deferred for now, but if a launch SKU needs it, it is a third grant-set entry shape + a §17.6 contract row, not a Subscriptions-side inference |
| ~~**`pricingSnapshotRef`'s evaluation-policy segment**~~ (D-161, added 2026-08-03 — **joint with Rating**) | — | **CLOSED by D-162 (2026-08-03)**, on the recommended option **(a) a vocabulary generation**: `ep-<n>`, a declared gear constant naming which evaluation-policy field set the frozen row content is read under, opening at `ep-1` over a nine-field roster the design set had never written down. (b) lost because a digest moves on cosmetics as readily as on semantics; (c) lost because it needs Rating to countersign away the only field that could say a replay is reading old rows under new semantics. The bump is a **replayed append-only log** rather than a rule someone remembers, and the gear's build reads it — a constant nobody bumps asserts on posted money a stability that is not there. D-161 clause (1), no placeholder ever, is unchanged. **Rating's adoption is owed** (equality comparison, fail-closed on an unrecognised generation, and the "two segments" count corrected in `design/01` §4.3 / `design/11` §4) |
| ~~**`crossBoundaryWarningText`'s source**~~ (D-168, added 2026-08-03) | — | **CLOSED by D-169 (2026-08-03)**, on the recommended option **(c) not a catalog field at all**: the read model publishes `crossBoundaryChangePolicy = cancel_plus_new` alone and the surface that renders the warning owns its wording — which this set had already named, PRD **AC #66** requiring the preview/migration UI to warn that in-place credit is forfeited and take an explicit confirmation, so the field was a *second* home for a sentence the PRD had placed on the surface that shows it. (a) lost on the **store** rather than the sentence: whatever S6 declares, publish freezes into an INSERT-only delta row on the ≥ 7-year horizon, so one language is permanent for every version already stamped and this set has no localization story anywhere. (b) lost because re-authorability is going forward only — every already-stamped version stays frozen against what the tenant had at the time — and it puts customer-facing copy on the carrier D-152's own confirmation marks **provisional**. D-168 clause (1) is **discharged, not overridden**: with one field there is no half to stamp, so the §9 AC it recorded as unsatisfiable is satisfiable. **Subscriptions' countersign is owed** (the wording is theirs — SUB-P1's classification seam and the AC #66 preview that displays it) |
| **Dedup-store retention** (D-142 residue, added 2026-08-02) | Nothing bounds `pricing_idempotency_dedup`. Does the gear (a) reclaim nothing, letting the rows ride the ≥ 7-year truth-history horizon like every other store; (b) reap rows older than TTL × K, with K configurable; or (c) reclaim exactly at the TTL? | The table grows one row per mutation forever and holds response bodies, so it becomes the gear's largest by row count on storage sized for catalog truth. D-142 decides the TTL's *semantics* (claim-time expiry, no reaper, compare-and-swap takeover) precisely so this can be answered separately: (b) is bounded and safe — a row vanishing between the read and the swap simply lets the INSERT path win — but needs a value for K; (c) is semantically safe too (nothing is owed past the TTL) yet makes "expired" observable two ways, a missing row and an old row, where D-142 deliberately has one. Recommended: **(b)**, with K ratified in the §14 NFR set beside the 24h value that was ratified there |

## F.2 Carried forward — findings not closed this round

Verified or plausible, but needing design work rather than a text fix. Listed so the next pass
starts here instead of rediscovering them.

- ~~**[H] Legacy snapshot synthesis cannot reach its own backdated rows.**~~ — **CLOSED by D-76 (2026-07-30)**: disjoint `pricing_historical_price` store (uniqueness scoped to it, so a live row on the same key is not a conflict) + the two-tier `inst-sy-select` rule (live/superseded window-covered row → reference row by its own interval → fail closed).
- **[M] Overlay `tax_basis` vs the target row's `taxInclusive`.** `TAX_BASIS_UNDECLARED` fires only on silence; a declared `exclusive` overlay against a `tax_inclusive = true` row is unreconciled, and nothing says which basis the adjusted amount carries downstream (D-48 mirrors the row's `tax_category_ref`, not the overlay's basis). Proposed: `TAX_BASIS_CONFLICT` at publish; `delegated_tariffs` the only legal value across a mixed-basis scope; the adjusted amount always carries the base row's basis.
- **[M] `rounding_policy_ref` is an unvalidated free-form id.** Only *presence* is checked (row ref, else tenant default, else fail) for the field that decides the last minor unit of every charge — no vocabulary, no registry, no referential check, no fixture variant, while `discountRef` (which affects nothing at MVP) does get `DISCOUNT_REF_UNRESOLVED`. Proposed: a gear-build constant set + `ROUNDING_POLICY_UNKNOWN` + one fixture per id.
- ~~**[M] Two granularities claim the band unit on non-`sum` rows.**~~ — **CLOSED by D-77 (2026-07-30)**: `billingGranularity` MUST pair with `aggregationGranularity` on non-`sum` rows (`hour` ⇒ `per_hour`, `day` ⇒ `per_day`), else `LEVEL_GRANULARITY_MISMATCH`; the two rules then name one unit by construction and the circular reference is removed.
- ~~**[L] AC #26 is treated as an exhaustive error enumeration but is not maintained.**~~ — **CLOSED by D-75 (2026-07-30)**: the list is now declared illustrative, with §17.4 + the slice-registered pipeline rules normative and the validator governing on disagreement.
