That is the same defect class as a rule with no operand, one level up: the test has an operand, and the operand is the wrong set.
- **The structural remedy, on both engines**, and on Postgres it is sliced to the arm: a census that reads the column list off the **table** (`pragma_table_info` / `information_schema.columns`) and the predicate out of the **guard's own text** (`sqlite_master.sql` / `pg_proc.prosrc`), so a column added to `pricing_plan` and forgotten in the trigger reddens in the suite instead of in production. Text censuses rather than behavioural ones deliberately: a per-column `UPDATE` needs a value that both differs from the seed and satisfies every pairing `CHECK`, which is exactly why the sibling cases hand-pick their lists — and hand-picking is the step that gets skipped when a column is added. The behavioural cases for the four columns are kept beside them, because a trigger could name a column in a comment.
- **The Postgres census matches inside the frozen arm only, and the first version did not** (corrected on review). `bss.pricing_plan_append_only()` carries three arms plus comments, so a function-wide `contains` passes a column named in the `DELETE` ban, in the flip whitelist or in prose while it is unguarded. The sibling table already exhibits the shape: `grandfather_until` sits in `pricing_price_append_only()`'s **monotonicity** arm and not in its frozen-column arm, so a function-wide match would call it frozen when it is not. The `SQLite` twin never had the weakness — it reads one trigger — and the engine that ships must not carry the weaker census of the two.
- **The vehicle is a sanctioned flip, and the first version of this entry got that wrong** (corrected on review, 2026-08-08). A content-**only** `UPDATE` on a non-draft row is already refused — `trg_pricing_plan_flip_whitelist` and Postgres's arm 4 both refuse *every* non-draft `UPDATE` that is not `published → superseded | retired`. What was unrefused is content **riding** one of those flips: `SET lifecycle_state = 'retired', entitlement_grants = …` moved the grant set. The entry originally reported a bare `UPDATE` as the measured reproduction; it reproduces as a **refusal**. The defect is real, the tests used the right vehicle throughout, and the *prose* described a statement nobody ran — **the third time this session that a claim in prose outran what the code supports**, after an invented release actor and a probe result read wider than the probe. The narrower vector is also the realistic one: supersession and retirement are writes the gear performs.
- **Every later-column wave that owed a freeze on `pricing_price` paid it; the two on `pricing_plan` did not.** The accounting, corrected on review: five column waves on `pricing_price` (`000037`, `000039`, `000050`, `000054`, `000056`) paid by four restatements, `m20260802_000040` covering the first two at once. `m20260802_000021` is **not** an example of the discipline and the entry first cited it as one — it restates the window function to add an act-sequence arm, and `mutation_seq` is *designed to move*, so freezing it would be a defect. Checked across every table carrying a frozen-column trigger; `pricing_plan` is the only one with the gap, and the count came from a `grep -c` of `CREATE TRIGGER` rather than from reading what the migration does, which is the same "a filtered count is not a census" this program has written down twice.
- **Probed**: dropping any one of the four from either engine's restated arm reddens both that engine's behavioural case and its text census, and only that engine's.

#### D-264 [M] The clone's provenance, and what a new column on `pricing_plan` costs now

- **Where**: `m20260802_000061_add_pricing_plan_cloned_from`, `m20260802_000062_guard_pricing_plan_cloned_from`; `pricing/src/domain/plan.rs` (`PlanRevision`, `PlanShapePatch`'s sibling `NewPlanDraft`); `pricing/src/infra/publish.rs`; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §6, `inst-cl-copy`, `inst-cl-draft`; D-19, D-259, D-263.
- **Decision (2026-08-08): `cloned_from` is provenance, and it is frozen.** One nullable column naming the plan a clone was copied from. `NULL` means "authored, not cloned" and is not a sentinel: the clone is an **ordinary draft** (`inst-cl-draft`) taking the full pipeline and an approval on its first publish, so no rule reads this and the content pin does not frame it.
- **It carries forward across revisions**, because lineage is the *plan's* and not one revision's — `open_revision` copies it, and a second revision of a cloned plan is still cloned.
- **It is not caller-settable.** `POST /plans` sets it `None` unconditionally: that surface creates an authored plan by definition, and a caller able to name a source could forge provenance for a plan it never copied. The clone path is the only writer.
- **It is not a foreign key, and the reason is the parent's key shape rather than an omission.** `pricing_plan`'s key is `(plan_id, revision)`, so `plan_id` alone is not unique and nothing exists for a bare id to reference. Both alternatives are worse: `(cloned_from, cloned_from_revision)` would pin lineage to one *revision* of the source and go stale the moment it re-publishes, and a `UNIQUE (plan_id)` cannot exist on a table holding one row per revision. Written down because D-261 shipped two unkeyed id columns whose module doc listed three deliberate omissions and was silent on them — an absence a reader cannot tell from an oversight is one they will re-litigate.
- **D-259's destructure did its job on the first field added since it landed.** Adding `cloned_from` to `PlanRevision` produced `error[E0027]: pattern does not mention field` in `assemble_from`, which is exactly the compile error D-259 exists to raise — so "is this authored content or provenance?" was answered at the assembly boundary rather than defaulted silently. The compiler then named all six remaining sites; none was found by grep.
- **D-263's census did its job on the first column added since it landed.** The guard restatement `m20260802_000062` was not written from memory: the frozen-whitelist census reads the column list off the table, so the column with no guard reddens two suites. That is the whole point of moving a rule out of a maintainer's head and into a test, demonstrated one migration later.
- **The writer is the next group, and this is stated rather than implied**: nothing sets `cloned_from` to anything but `None` today, so the column is `NULL` everywhere until the cloner lands. That is the same posture the slice's three tables took before their repositories — a writer with no caller is dead code, and a column is cheaper to land with its guard than to bolt a guard onto later, which is precisely what D-263 had to repair.
- **Three of `inst-cl-*`'s clauses have no operand in this gear, measured before any of it was built**, and the cloner must **name** them rather than write them: `pricing_plan_grant` is Slice 10's *credit* table (D-52) — a different object from Slice 6's entitlement column, unbuilt, and inside the `inst-ac-*` chunk D-177 blocks — so D-130's "a `compiled_allowance` grant is recompiled rather than copied" has nothing to range over; **contract locks** live in the Contracts registry, which D-251 records as absent, and no per-plan or per-row lock storage exists here to copy or refuse to copy; and **`inst-cl-discount`**'s "copies only if it still resolves to a registered instrument" rests on `inst-dr-referential`, which `m20260802_000056` already records as **not buildable** — there is no instrument registry, so the ref copies unconditionally and a drop-if-unresolved arm could never fire.

#### D-265 [M] The clone path, and a collapse the reset produces that §3 names only for grandfathered rows

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs`; `pricing/tests/sqlite_clone.rs`; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §3 `algo-clone`, `inst-cl-copy`, `inst-cl-resets`, `inst-cl-discount`, `inst-cl-draft`, `inst-cl-windows`; D-19, D-41, D-52, D-130, D-146, D-251, D-264.
- **Decision (2026-08-08): the clone copies configuration and leaves lifecycle state behind**, which is one rule rather than a list to memorize: configuration is what an author wrote, and windows, grandfathered generations and superseded history are all *where the plan got to*. `inst-cl-resets` says so in the same breath for all three.
- **The phase remap has exactly three sites, and the third is C-7's.** The chain's `converts_to_phase_id`, the `phase` axis of every copied price row, and **the keys of the D-41 `entitlement_grants.perPhase` map** — the map being keyed *by* `phase_id`, an unremapped clone publishes a grant set naming phases that exist only in the source and fails `GRANT_SET_PHASE_UNKNOWN` on its first publish, quoting ids the operator cannot see. `AddonRule` and the descriptor set carry no phase, checked rather than assumed, so those are copies and not remaps.
- **A dangling reference is carried, not repaired.** A `converts_to_phase_id` or a `perPhase` key the remap does not know is copied as it stands: `PhaseGraph` and `GrantSetPhasesKnown` are what judge those, and a cloner that silently mended a broken source chain would hide the fault instead of copying the plan.
- **Three of §3's clauses have no operand in this gear and are named rather than written** (measured before building): `pricing_plan_grant` is *Slice 10's* credit table (D-52), unbuilt and inside the `inst-ac-*` chunk D-177 blocks, so **D-130's recompile-rather-than-copy rule has nothing to range over**; **contract locks** live in the Contracts registry D-251 records as absent, and nothing here is a lock to skip; and **`inst-cl-discount`**'s drop-if-unresolved rests on `inst-dr-referential`, which `m20260802_000056` already records as not buildable — there is no instrument registry, so the ref copies unconditionally. Writing any of the three would be a rule that can never fire.
- **§5's clone-source-not-found code is not minted.** The design set declares it and this crate does not, so the refusal renders through the canonical not-found family naming the plan — D-146's posture exactly: a gear mints its own error *variants* freely, and a **wire code** is the design set's to declare. It is named here in words rather than as a token, because a bare code token in register prose **pays the P3 `code-unreferenced` debt the raising rule never earned** — and this entry did exactly that on its first draft, moving the pinned baseline from 54 to 53 for a code no line of Rust references.
- **Found by building, and left as a finding rather than settled: the reset collapses `new_subscriptions_only` onto `all_subscriptions`.** `inst-cl-resets` sends every copied row's `priceEligibility` to `all_subscriptions`, and it excludes `existing_grandfathered` rows *because* copying them under that reset "would collapse two rows onto one canonical scope key (guaranteed duplicate-scope publish failure)". **The identical collapse happens for the third class and the clause does not mention it**: a source plan holding an `all_subscriptions` row and a `new_subscriptions_only` row on one `(currency, region, phase, chargeKind)` — two distinct keys the published-plane index admits — resets both onto one key, and the clone fails `DUPLICATE_SCOPE_KEY`. Measured: seeding exactly that pair reddened four of five cases with a real driver refusal. **Not settled here**, because the three available answers are all edges the design set does not draw — exclude the class as lifecycle state, keep its eligibility, or fail the clone with a remediation — and this session has three times had to remove a rule invented to fill such a gap. It wants a decision of D-231's kind, beside `awaiting_approval`'s missing rejection exit.
- **A probe that reddened nothing, and it was a finding — the third time this session.** Removing the eligibility reset entirely left the whole suite green, because every seeded row already carried `all_subscriptions`: the assertion was true of a value nothing had to move. The suite now seeds a `new_subscriptions_only` row **in its own market**, which gives the reset an operand without triggering the collapse above, and the probe bites.

#### D-266 [H] The clone dropped the plan-change contract, and D-259's remedy was not applied to the second place that rebuilds a revision

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs`; `pricing/tests/sqlite_clone.rs`; `plan_repo::open_revision`; D-259, D-113, D-23, D-265.
- **Problem** (found by review of the commit that landed D-265, one commit later — the same lens, the same interval, the third time): `clone_plan` built `NewPlanDraft` field by field, and **`NewPlanDraft` has no field for `change_contract`**. So the clone silently lost `allowedChangeTargets`, `comparabilityRank` and D-113's `usageCounterOnPlanChange`, and **nothing refused it**: with no edges K4 asks for no rank, so the clone published clean and wrong — out of self-service change entirely, with the counter flag flipped to its opposite default. `plan_repo::open_revision` copies all three and carries a comment saying exactly why an edge list that resets itself is a silent drop; this path did not read it.
- **This is D-259's defect class re-introduced one commit after D-259 was praised for catching it.** D-259's remedy was to destructure `PlanRevision` so a forgotten field is `error[E0027]`; the cloner reinstated the field-by-field literal and lost a field on its first draft. **Fixed structurally**: `clone_plan` now destructures the source revision, ignoring five fields by name, so the next field added to `PlanRevision` is a compile error here too. The lesson is not "remember the contract" — it is that **a remedy applied at one site is not applied at the class**, and the second site is where the defect went.
- **The bundle composition is not copied, and the clone now says so rather than handing back a plan that silently is not a bundle.** §3's copy set predates Slice 8 and names none of `pricing_bundle` / `bundle_component` / `bundle_revshare*`, while `plan_repo::open_revision` copies them as the plan's child tables — **the two paths that reproduce a plan disagree**, and this is the fourth table D-265's "which clauses have an operand" census should have found and did not: unlike `pricing_plan_grant`, contract locks and the discount resolver, these tables are built and populated. Copying them and refusing to clone a bundle are **both** edges the design set does not draw, so this path does neither and reports instead. It wants a decision.
- **Owed, and stated rather than hidden — the clone is not atomic.** Six-plus writes, each its own transaction, where every sibling orchestrator in this gear wraps its unit (`infra::publish`, `infra::cutover`, `infra::bundle`, and `plan_repo::create_draft` itself). A failure partway — D-265's `new_subscriptions_only` collapse is the reachable one — leaves an **orphan draft consuming the target plan id**, and a retry on that id then fails on `uq_pricing_plan_open_draft`. The fix needs runner-taking variants of four `PlanShapeRepo` writers, which do not exist; that is a group of its own and not this one's to invent.
- **Two resets are structurally unreachable and their assertions were vacuous**: `Cohort::None` and `grandfather_until = None` are both already true of every row that reaches them, because grandfathered rows are excluded first — `check_cohort_eligibility` and `check_grandfather_horizon` each admit their value only on an `existing_grandfathered` key. Kept, because a later change admitting those rows would need them, but no longer claimed as behaviour a test proves.
- **Two further coverage gaps the review named and this wave closed**: the suite seeded no descriptor set and no composite meter, so `composites_copied` had never been non-zero and the `composite_id` re-mint — a DoD-named behaviour — had never run. Both are seeded and asserted now, and all four new assertions are probed.
- **Owed and not closed**: `supersedes_price_id`'s reset has a real production operand and none in the suite (no seeded row carries one); the copy set's authored **bands** are verified by reading rather than by execution; `available_from` copies forward and makes the clone unpublishable against `AvailableFromNotBackdated` for a second systematic reason the receipt does not name; excluding the source's **draft** rows is a rule §3 does not state and this path invented; and `PlanCloner` is constructed nowhere but the suite — §8's `POST …/plans/{planId}/clone` is unbuilt.

#### D-267 [M] `awaiting_approval` gains its rejection exit, and D-137 holds without a second clause

- **Where**: `m20260802_000063_add_bulk_operation_rejected_state`; `pricing/src/infra/storage/entity/bulk_operation.rs`; `pricing/tests/sqlite_bulk_operation_store.rs`; `pricing/tests/postgres_schema_bulk_operation.rs`; `pricing/tests/sqlite_migrations.rs`; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §4 (`inst-bs-reject`); D-37, D-137, D-231, D-260, D-261, D-262, O4.
- **Propagated**: (2026-08-09) S12 §4 — the state list (the new state is terminal, reachable **only** from `awaiting_approval`, and unreachable for an import by D-137's existing rule rather than by a new one) and transition 6, `inst-bs-reject`.
- **Problem**: D-260 found this on the day it built the machine, recorded it and deliberately left it pending a decision. §4 drew exactly one exit from `awaiting_approval` — `→ committing`, on approval — so a **refused** batch approval had nowhere to put the run. `DELETE` is refused by the table's own trigger, `uq_pricing_bulk_operation_client_key` is unique per tenant and stops the operator resubmitting the same work under the same key, and no other edge leaves the state. A rejected repricing run sat in `awaiting_approval` permanently, holding its client key against every retry.
- **Decision (2026-08-09, the product owner's): a seventh state, `rejected`, and one edge into it.** The two cheaper answers were both refused for D-231's reason: exempting the run from the `DELETE` ban, or freeing its client key, would each erase the record that a decision was taken — and the record *is* the audit. A refusal that leaves no trace is not a refusal anyone can review. The word is Slice 5's own: `pricing_approval` has carried `rejected` in its state vocabulary since `m20260802_000015`, so the run's state now reads the same as the approval's rather than inventing a synonym.
- **It is terminal, and it costs no new arm to make it so.** No disjunct names it on the left of an edge, and an edge list that is a whitelist refuses every unnamed move already — `m20260802_000045`'s shape, where `abandoned` became terminal without a clause mentioning it. A run that left this state would re-enter `committing` and apply exactly the rows the approver declined.
- **A rejected run is over, so it carries a `completed_at`.** The terminal set in `chk_pricing_bulk_operation_completed_at` gains it beside the other three, which is what keeps the instant and the state from disagreeing about whether the run ended. That half of the migration is a separate rule from the edge and gets its own case on both engines: the move with a null instant is refused **by constraint name**.
- **D-137 holds without a clause saying so, and that was verified rather than assumed.** An import can never be rejected, because the state is reachable only from `awaiting_approval` and `chk_pricing_bulk_operation_import_never_awaits` already forbids an import that state. A second constraint restating it would be **a rule that cannot fail**, which is indistinguishable from a rule that holds — the defect class this program has now met repeatedly. So both engines carry the proof behaviourally instead: an import at `validating` moving to the new state is refused by the **edge list** and not by the import `CHECK` (which is never reached, the import never getting to the state it is about), and an import born there by the born-validating arm. Both were run and both refuse.
- **`SQLite` is a rebuild and Postgres is five statements** — `m20260802_000045`'s asymmetry, unchanged. Postgres drops and re-adds each of the two **named** constraints and replaces the function whole, because `CREATE OR REPLACE` takes a body rather than a patch. `SQLite` cannot alter a `CHECK` at all, so the table is rebuilt, and **`DROP TABLE` takes the indexes and the triggers with it**: both indexes and all four of this table's triggers are recreated after the rename.
- **Three more triggers had to move, on two other tables**, and this is `m20260802_000045`'s recorded lesson applied before it could bite rather than after. `ALTER TABLE … RENAME TO` re-parses the whole schema, so a trigger left dangling by the `DROP TABLE` two statements earlier fails the **rename** instead of waiting to fail at run time: `pricing_repricing_journal`'s parent-reading arm and `pricing_bulk_row_lock`'s two each sub-select this table. All three are dropped before the rebuild and re-created verbatim after it; the other five triggers on those two tables name only their own tables and stay put, checked per trigger body rather than assumed from the table. Both child tables' **foreign keys** need no re-issue and that is a property of the engine rather than an omission: `SQLite` records a key in the **child's** DDL, so the rename restores the name it points at.
- **The restatement was produced neither by hand nor by a free-form script** (`m20260802_000051`'s rule, `m20260802_000058`'s procedure). Every block was read out of `m20260802_000047`'s own `UP` text — and the three sibling triggers out of `m20260802_000048`/`m20260802_000049` — and the generator asserted, before writing anything: exactly one arm per engine; each edge list **+1 line** with every original line surviving; each of the four vocabularies replacing **exactly one** named line and keeping the rest; the new state appearing exactly once per block that gains it and **not at all** in the three triggers copied unchanged; nine columns in the copy, read off the table rather than retyped; and the seven `SQLite` triggers naming this table across the three source migrations exactly accounted for.
- **Four of those assertions fired on the first run, and each was a real defect a reviewer's eye would have had to catch.** Two index extractions and one trigger extraction were matching the **Postgres** statement of the same name as well as the mirror's — the two engines' objects share their names, so a pattern anchored on the name alone silently spans both — and the state `CHECK`'s trailing comma had been stripped before the substitution went looking for the line that carries it. A hand-written restatement produces exactly these, and produces them silently.
- **The roster arithmetic, measured rather than reasoned about**: trigger and function **names** do not change, so `EXPECTED_TRIGGERS`, `EXPECTED_INDEXES`, `EXPECTED_CHECKS`, `EXPECTED_TABLES`, `EXPECTED_PRIMARY_KEYS` and all four Postgres rosters stand unmoved — the two widened constraints keep their names and the rebuild restores every object. Both censuses were **run** rather than argued from. Exactly **one** trigger-body digest moved, `trg_pricing_bulk_operation_transitions`, and its stability elsewhere is the evidence that matters: the rebuild re-types six other trigger bodies, three of them on tables this migration does not otherwise touch, and a typo in any of them would have surfaced in the same census. Re-pinned from the census's own failure output, with the previous digest beside it, as that file's convention requires.
- **Nothing is stranded behind a rejected run, and that is a consequence of where the lock is taken rather than of a new rule.** `inst-bs-commit` starts the bulk lock on entry to `committing` and `trg_pricing_bulk_row_lock_only_while_committing` enforces it, so a run refused at approval never held one — unlike D-261's aborted run, which leaves `pending` journal rows behind by design.
- **Probed: six times, one guard at a time and one engine at a time, with *both* tiers run on every probe.** D-262 recorded that **a green tier the probe never touched is not evidence about that tier**, so each probe ran the `SQLite` pair (`sqlite_bulk_operation_store` + `sqlite_migrations`, 22 cases) *and* the Postgres suite (8 cases), and every one of the six restored the file byte-identically, verified with `diff` because `cmp` is aliased to an ssh command here. The three guards are the edge, the state vocabulary and the terminal set, and each was reverted to its pre-D-267 form on one engine:
  1. **The edge.** `SQLite`: 5 cases red (`every_sanctioned_edge_is_walkable`, `a_rejected_run_carries_the_instant_it_ended`, `a_rejected_run_never_moves_again`, and **both** trigger-body censuses), 17 green, Postgres **run** at 8/8. Postgres: 3 cases red (the two walkable ones plus `rejected_is_terminal_and_reachable_only_from_a_pending_approval`), 5 green, `SQLite` **run** at 22/22. This is D-260's demonstration reproduced in both directions — a lost disjunct on the shipping engine leaves the mirror entirely green.
  2. **The state vocabulary.** Three cases red on the engine disabled, none on the other, both tiers run. The refusal names the constraint on both engines, and **no census moves** — the `CHECK` rosters are name-only and both constraints keep their names, so the widened vocabularies are held by behaviour and by nothing else. That is also what tells this probe apart from the edge probe, whose `SQLite` half is the only one of the six that moves a trigger body.
  3. **The terminal set**, and it is the sharpest of the three because it reddens the case's *own* assertion rather than its setup: with the state out of `chk_pricing_bulk_operation_completed_at`, the move carrying a **null** instant becomes legal and `a_rejected_run_carries_the_instant_it_ended` fails on `this statement must be rejected` — on both engines, each in its own probe, with the other tier run and green.
- **Three things found and deliberately not fixed**, each recorded rather than built:
  1. **Nothing drives the new state.** §5's API surface has no approve-or-reject endpoint for a bulk run — the batch approval is Slice 5's object — and no repository or orchestrator in this crate writes the run's state at all yet. The store admits the edge and no caller walks it, which is D-264's posture stated plainly rather than left to be discovered: the state is cheaper to land with its guard than to bolt a guard on afterwards, and the writer is a later group's.
  2. **The journal's writes are not bound to a `committing` run the way the lock's are.** `pricing_bulk_row_lock` refuses an insert unless its run is `committing`; `pricing_repricing_journal` has no such arm — its `INSERT` guards are the born-`pending` state and the `kind = repricing` restriction only. So the claim "a rejected run has no journal rows" is true of the runner and not of the schema. Not filled here: which states may journal is a rule the design set does not draw, and this session has three times had to remove a rule invented to fill such a gap.
  3. **`m20260802_000047`'s module doc still draws the six-state machine it built**, and was left alone deliberately — `m20260802_000032` stands in exactly that relation to `m20260802_000045`, each migration documenting what it did rather than being rewritten by its successors. The seventh state is documented where it is added.
#### D-268 [M] `new_subscriptions_only` is lifecycle state too, and the reset it made unnecessary

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs` (`CloneNotice`, `PlanCloner::copy_rows`, `reset_key`); `pricing/tests/sqlite_clone.rs`; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §3 `inst-cl-resets`; D-265, D-266.
- **Decision (2026-08-09): a `new_subscriptions_only` price row is not cloned**, and the rows left behind are counted on the clone receipt under their own notice, exactly as a grandfathered generation's are. This closes the gap D-265 measured and deliberately declined to fill.
- **The reason is the one `inst-cl-resets` already gives, applied to the class it did not name.** The clause excludes `existing_grandfathered` rows *because* copying them under its own reset "would collapse two rows onto one canonical scope key (guaranteed duplicate-scope publish failure)". The collapse is identical for this class: a source holding an `all_subscriptions` row and a `new_subscriptions_only` row on one `(currency, region, phase, chargeKind)` holds two distinct keys the published plane admits, and the reset sends both onto one. The clause was silent on it, so the clone of a perfectly ordinary cutover was refused by the draft plane's own unique index.
- **And the class is lifecycle state on the same reading that excludes the other two.** It is made by a **cutover** rather than authored — nobody writes "new subscriptions only" as the shape of a plan, they write it by closing a price to the subscribers already on it — and on a clone it is meaningless twice over, because every subscription on a brand-new plan is new. Configuration is what an author wrote; this is where the source plan got to.
- **The reset it protects now has no operand, and that is recorded rather than left to be rediscovered.** `PriceEligibility` has exactly three members, two of which are excluded before a row reaches `reset_key`, so **every** key the reset sees already reads `all_subscriptions`. It joins the cohort and `grandfatherUntil` (D-266) as a structural fence: kept, because a later change admitting either class would need it, and no longer claimed as behaviour a test proves. The suite says so where the assertions stand, and §3 says so beside the rule.
- **The suite's operand moved rather than vanished.** D-265 seeded a `new_subscriptions_only` row *in its own market* to give the reset something to move; D-268 took that operand away and the same row now serves the exclusion. It stays in its own market so that the exclusion is observable as an **absence** — nothing else in the seed occupies that market, so a clone holding a row there copied the class it was told to leave — and the collapse D-265 measured gets a case of its own, seeding both classes on **one** market and asserting the clone runs. Without the exclusion that case does not merely copy an extra row: it fails on the store's refusal, which is what makes the rule load-bearing rather than tidy.
- **Owed and not closed**: §8's Clone DoD named `existing_grandfathered` alone in its never-copied list when this landed, and §8 was not this group's to edit — **paid the same day by `06392d1e5`**, a later commit in the same merge range, which is why an entry recording a debt has to be re-read when the range closes rather than when it is written; the receipt still carries no counter for the bundle it now copies (D-269); and none of the three exclusions is reachable through a route, `PlanCloner` being constructed nowhere but the suite — D-266's finding, unchanged.

#### D-269 [M] A clone of a bundle is a bundle

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs` (`PlanCloner::copy_bundle`); `pricing/tests/sqlite_clone.rs`; `bundle_repo` (`find_by_plan`, `load_composition`, `create`, `replace_composition`); [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §3 `inst-cl-copy`; D-07, D-92, D-105, D-106, D-266.
- **Decision (2026-08-09): the bundle composition is copied**, and §3's copy set is extended to name `pricing_bundle`, `pricing_bundle_component`, `pricing_bundle_revshare_group` and `pricing_bundle_revshare`. D-266's notice — the one that reported the composition as left behind — is deleted rather than kept beside the copy.
- **The disagreement this settles is between two paths, not between a path and a document.** `plan_repo::open_revision` already copies the composition as one of the plan's child tables — *a bundle rides its plan's revisions* — while §3's copy set predates Slice 8 and named none of the four tables. So the clone handed back a plan holding a bundle's price rows and none of its composition: not a degraded bundle, a plan that silently is not one.
- **The identity is re-minted and the component references are not**, and the two halves have different reasons. `pricing_bundle.plan_id` is unique per plan, so the identity *cannot* be shared even in principle; that is `composite_id`'s reason (D-106) reaching one slice further. `bundle_component.component_plan_id`, by contrast, names **other** plans — plans the clone did not copy and has no business repointing. The phase remap has nothing to do with them, and a copier that ran them through it would have been a rule silently doing nothing, since the remap is keyed by phase id.
- **No new repository function was needed, and that was measured before designing rather than assumed.** `bundle_repo::copy_composition` is a within-one-plan, across-revisions copy and does not serve a cross-plan one — but `find_by_plan`, `load_composition`, `create` and `replace_composition` compose into exactly the cross-plan copy, with the authored content travelling through the domain's own `CompositionDraft` and `effective_share_bp` minted by the publish that normalizes it (D-07) rather than carried. `bundle_repo.rs` is therefore **untouched**: the cheapest cross-plan copier is the one already exported.
- **The order inside `clone_plan` is forced twice over.** The bundle row precedes the composition because the child tables' foreign key points at it; and the whole copy precedes the grant-set patch because `replace_composition` is a compare-and-swap on the *same* plan-revision tag and returns the version the patch then has to hold. Threading that version is the reason this is one method rather than an inline branch — that, and `clone_plan` sitting at the cognitive-complexity cap.
- **Owed and not closed**: §8's Clone DoD's copy set names none of the bundle tables either, and §8 was not this group's to edit; the receipt gains **no** bundle counter, so a copied composition is the one copied child set invisible on the response — a field the design set does not draw, recorded here instead of invented; and D-266's non-atomicity finding now spans two further writes, so a clone that fails between the bundle row and its composition leaves an orphan draft that is a bundle with nothing in it.
#### D-270 [M] The price history is a read over the Foundation's own rows, and the two promises it cannot keep

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/history.rs`; `price_repo::list_history_page` + `HistoryPosition`; `pricing/tests/sqlite_history.rs`; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §3 `algo-history-export` (`inst-he-read`, `inst-he-export`, `inst-he-nostore`), §5, §8 `dod-history-export`; D-12, D-125.
- **Measured before building, because the premises were checkable.** `inst-he-*` had **one** mention in the whole crate — a doc line in `domain/audit.rs` naming the Auditor-only sibling — so none of this existed. `created_by` **is** on `pricing_price` and **is** already projected: `PriceRecord` has carried it, and `created_at_utc` beside it, since the Foundation. The actor therefore needed no column, no migration and no new read; what it needed was a surface to be read *by*.
- **Decision (2026-08-09): `inst-he-nostore` is honoured literally — no table, no migration, no second copy of anything.** The read walks `pricing_price` itself. `inst-he-read`'s "chronological immutable records" and the Foundation's append-only rows are the same set of rows, and a history store beside them would be a second answer to what a price used to say.
- **D-125 has two order branches and `list_for_plan_page` implements the other one.** The decision gives catalog lists *deterministic key order* and history *commit/append order*; the existing plan-price page walks `price_id ASC`, which is the first branch, and is additionally scoped to one plan and filtered to a state set. A history read inherits none of that: it is tenant-wide, spans every lifecycle state (a `superseded` predecessor is the row an operator is most often asking about), and orders on `(created_at_utc, price_id)`. Both columns, because neither alone is a total order — the instant ties for every row set one request wrote, and a walk resuming on the instant alone skips the rest of a tied group while `>=` re-serves the cursor's own row on every page. Both mistakes are provable only at page size 1, which is how the suite walks.
- **The cursor is a second token and that is not a second encoding of the same thing.** `api::rest::cursor`'s token names a `Uuid`; this walk's position is an instant *and* a row, which no `Uuid` spells. The token is 28 bytes — `i64` seconds, `u32` sub-second nanoseconds, the id — because a single count of milliseconds or microseconds **truncates**, and a truncated cursor sorts before the row it names, so the next page opens by repeating it. The *limit* rule's three branches — default, cap and the refusal of a zero page — are **restated here**, not delegated: a first draft did delegate them to the REST cursor type and `DE0202` correctly refused it (see below). Only the two **values** D-125 fixes cross the boundary, so the number the export SLO is stated per still has one spelling.
- **Commit order is approximated, and the residue is stated rather than papered over.** `created_at_utc` is the **authoring** instant the request carried, not the instant the transaction committed. A row authored at `T1` whose transaction commits after one authored at `T2 > T1` becomes visible behind a walk that has already passed `T1`, and is never returned — a real gap in D-125's "never skips a row at or before the cursor". Closing it needs a monotonic sequence assigned inside the writing transaction, the shape `pricing_price_window.mutation_seq` already has, and therefore a migration this group was not to write. **Owed.**
- **The index the walk wants does not exist either.** `pricing_price` is indexed on the plan and on the supersession link; `(tenant_id, created_at_utc, price_id)` is not there, so over a >= 7-year history each page sorts the tenant's whole row set. Also a migration, also **owed**, and named here because a read whose cost nobody wrote down is one that is discovered in production.
- **The SLO is not enforced and no guard pretends to enforce it.** `inst-he-export`'s p95 <= 5s per 100-record chunk is a percentile against a populated store under load; nothing in this module decides it, and a check asserting it against an in-memory fixture would be asserting about the test machine. What follows from it **is** enforced, because it is a shape rather than a latency: an interactive read naming no page size gets the 100 C-6 preserved, and one naming more than the cap is clamped to 1,000 rather than refused.
- **§5's "filters" are undrawn and none were invented.** The API table promises filters, §1.7 says "under Auditor/Finance filters", and no clause anywhere names an axis. So the read takes none: it is tenant-scoped and nothing else. This is the third group running to find such a gap, and the register's own record is that filling one costs more to remove than to leave open.
- **"Effective dates" is a set, not a pair, and the entry says so.** A row's effective dating is its `pricing_price_window` intervals, and a row may hold several — a shortened predecessor beside its successor, a cancelled schedule beside its replacement. §3 does not say which of them one "effective date" would be, so the entry carries them all in `effective_from` order, each labelled with its state; cancelled and expired are included for `window_repo::list_for_plan`'s reason, this being the store's read and not the read-model projection. Picking one would have been an invented rule.
- **The actor case is built so a wrong source would be visible.** D-12's 2026-07-28 amendment forbids `pricing_audit_log` here, and the ordinary authoring path stamps the same principal in both places — so a fixture in which every row had an audit record would be equally green either way. Three of the suite's four rows are seeded through the entity and have **no** audit record at all, and the case asserts the tenant holds exactly one; a read that consulted that table would lose them.
- **A probe that reddened nothing, and what it turned out to mean.** Deleting the explicit `tenant_id` predicate from the history walk left the whole suite green; so did widening the scope gate while keeping the predicate. Removing **both** reddens six cases. The tenant isolation is therefore genuine but doubly held, and the case binds to the conjunction rather than to either half — the crate's established idiom (`load_page` and `currency_binding` carry the same pair), recorded so the next reader does not mistake either line for the one that is load-bearing. The other twelve probes each reddened their own case and nothing beyond what shares its fixture premise.
- **No route, and no error code.** §5's two history paths are unmounted: `plan × read` is the route's gate to apply (D-12) and authz wiring is not this group. Nothing here mints a wire code — the only refusals are an undecodable cursor and a zero page, both of which are malformed requests under the Foundation validation envelope, which is the reading `api::rest::cursor` already argues for every list surface.
- **The layer rule refused the tidier version of the limit clause, and it was right.** The first draft delegated the page-size half to `api::rest::cursor::PageRequest::parse`, which reads well and is what `DE0202` exists to stop: a request struct is the transport's shape, and a store-facing reader that took one would make the transport's vocabulary load-bearing beneath it. Caught by dylint and not by clippy or any test. What crosses the boundary now is the pair of **numbers** — a constant is not a DTO — so the three branches are restated while the values D-125 fixed stay declared once. Recorded because "the clean refactor the linter forbids" is a shape the next reader will re-attempt.

#### D-271 [M] The review of three parallel strands, and the one defect a merge cannot see

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/history.rs`, `pricing/tests/sqlite_clone.rs`, `pricing/tests/sqlite_bulk_operation_store.rs`, [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §7, §8; D-266, D-267, D-268, D-269, D-270.
- **Problem**: three strands built in parallel off one base, each gated and each reviewed by itself. A review over the **merged range** then found eight things, and the two that matter most are of a kind no single strand could have seen.
- **A rule was labelled "not evidence" and it was a real rule.** The clone suite grouped four assertions as structural fences on the argument that every copied row already carries all four values — true of the eligibility, the cohort and the grandfather tombstone (D-266, D-268), and **false of `supersedes_price_id`**, which a published successor genuinely carries: both `price_repo::insert_successor_draft_on` and `infra::supersession` set it, on exactly the rows the clone copies. D-266 had already recorded that reset as having a live operand and no case. **Filing a live rule among the fences is how it gets deleted by someone tidying**, so the grouping is corrected and the rule now has the case it was owed — probed, and removing the reset reddens it.
- **A debt was paid by a later commit in the same range and two entries went on recording it as owed.** D-268 and D-269 said §8's Clone DoD was behind §3; `06392d1e5`, four commits later, brought it level. Neither entry was re-read. **An entry that records a debt has to be re-read when the merge range closes, not when it is written** — a strand cannot know what a sibling or the controller will land after it, which is exactly the class of staleness parallel work produces and a merge cannot detect.
- **D-267 widened a set that a clause nobody edited ranges over.** §7's stalled-run alarm fires on "a run reaching a **terminal state** with **all** rows `failed`" — and a `rejected` run holds **no** journal rows, so "all rows failed" is *vacuously* true over an empty set and every refusal would raise a Warn for a decision a human took deliberately. The alarm is unbuilt, so this was spec debt rather than a live misfire; §7 now excludes the state and says why. **A new member of an enumerated set changes every clause that quantifies over the set**, and propagating to the section that *defines* the set is not propagating to the clauses that *use* it.
- **A type did not hold its own invariant.** `HistoryPageRequest::parse` refuses a zero page because a zero limit makes the walk report itself exhausted at row 0 — the probe row is the only row fetched, it is popped, and the caller gets an empty page with no cursor. The fields were `pub`, so a struct literal skipped the refusal, and the suite took that door on every read. Fields are private now and the resume path goes through a second checked constructor rather than around it: **one unchecked door gives the invariant back**.
- **Three smaller corrections**: a doc comment still counting three terminal states after a fourth landed; D-270 contradicting itself about whether the page-size branches are delegated or restated (the code agrees with the later sentence, and `DE0202` is why); and this register's own repeated claim of "four Postgres rosters" — there are **five** declared, read by four census tests.
- **What the review found clean is worth as much as what it found**, and is recorded so it is not re-checked: the `SQLite` table rebuild in `m20260802_000063` was verified **mechanically** — every `CREATE` block hashed against `m20260802_000047`'s original, the rebuilt table byte-identical apart from the two intended additions, both indexes and all seven objects restored, the three sibling triggers on two other tables byte-identical, and the re-pinned digest recomputed independently and matched. Tenant scoping in the history walk is genuinely doubly held; no Auditor-only source reaches the `plan × read` surface; and cross-strand interference is nil beyond the two entries above.
- **One residual gap, recorded rather than closed**: the `CHECK` and index rosters are name-only on both engines, and `pricing_bulk_operation` is absent from the `SQLite` suite's `assert_readable!` list — so a rebuild that dropped a `NOT NULL`, a `DEFAULT`, or recreated an index over the wrong columns would pass every test in that file. It did not, and that is known by the byte-diff above rather than by any gate. `pricing_policy_object`'s rebuild got a per-table DDL-shape test; this one is owed the same.

#### D-272 [M] Mounting the clone route is blocked by its own atomicity, and that reorders what is left

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs`; `pricing/src/api/rest/plans.rs` (the mutating pattern); `pricing/src/infra/storage/repo/plan_shape_repo.rs`; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §5; D-266.
- **Found by starting the work rather than by planning it (2026-08-08).** Nothing in Slice 12 is reachable — §5 declares **eight** routes and **none** is mounted, and `PlanCloner` and `HistoryExporter` are each constructed at exactly one site, their own suite. Mounting the clone was to be the next group; it stopped on its first measurement.
- **The blocker**: §5 gives the clone **client-key idempotency**, and this gear's idempotency gate runs the mutation **inside its own transaction** — `idempotent::guarded` hands the closure a `DbTx`, and `plans.rs` states the reason in its own words: the gate is the first step of the mutation and shares its transaction, so a retry answers the first caller rather than acting twice. **`clone_plan` is not transactional** (D-266), so it cannot be the body of that closure. The two available shapes are both wrong: mount without the gate and drop a guarantee §5 specifies, or wrap a non-atomic sequence in a gate that then promises something it does not deliver.
- **Decision: the atomicity debt is paid first, and the route follows it.** Not a preference — the route cannot be written correctly before it. This reorders the remaining Slice 12 work by measurement rather than by taste, which is the point of measuring a group's premises before writing it rather than after.
- **What it costs, measured rather than estimated**: each of `PlanShapeRepo`'s four writers (`replace_phases`, `replace_addon_rules`, `set_descriptor_set`, `replace_composites`) opens `in_transaction` itself and holds its body in a closure, so each owes a runner-taking free function the way `plan_repo::create_draft_on` and `price_repo::create_draft_on` already have one. Then `clone_plan` composes them inside one transaction. D-266 called this "a group of its own" and the estimate holds.
- **Not built here, and the state is left clean**: an `AuthoringState` field and its construction were wired speculatively while measuring, and **reverted** — a field no route reads is the same shape as a column no code writes, which this register has spent two days removing.
- **The history route is not blocked by this.** It is a read: no gate, no transaction, no correlation edge (the read-only `frontier` router carries none either). It can be mounted independently of the clone's.

#### D-273 [M] Slice 12 becomes reachable, and a probe that reddened nothing showed the suite could not see its own gate

- **Where**: `pricing/src/api/rest/history.rs`; `pricing/src/module.rs`; `pricing/tests/rest_history.rs`, `pricing/tests/module_test.rs`; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) §3 `inst-he-read`, §5; D-12, D-125, D-270, D-272.
- **Decision (2026-08-08): the history read is mounted, and it is the first Slice 12 surface an operator can reach.** §5 declares eight routes and none was mounted; both built features were constructed at exactly one site each, their own suite. This one goes first because it needs nothing that does not exist: it is a read, so no transaction, no idempotency claim, no audit record — and therefore **no correlation edge**, the same reason `frontier` carries none. Its sibling the clone is blocked on its own atomicity (D-272).
- **Its own `ApiState` rather than a field on the authoring one.** The criterion is `frontier`'s: a read that opens no transaction and writes nothing has no use for the authoring state's reach, and sharing one would give it that reach for free.
- **The route census caught the route before the route caught anything.** `module_test`'s registered-path set is asserted **exactly**, so mounting without declaring reddened immediately — and the path is a `const` the router and the census share, because a census spelling its paths as literals is one a rename walks away from.
- **A probe reddened nothing, and it was a broken probe that exposed a real gap** — the two are not the same and telling them apart is the rule. Intending to skip the authz gate, the mutation left the refusal in place and replaced only the **compiled scope** with one built from the subject's tenant; every case stayed green. That proved nothing about the gate, but it proved something about the suite: the fake constrains to the very tenant the context carries, so the PDP's compiled scope and the subject's tenant were **identical in every case**, and the suite was silent about which of them does the filtering. `require_constraints = true` and the compiled scope *are* the isolation. A case now allows the caller while constraining to a **different** tenant and asserts the caller's own rows are not served; re-probed with the gate genuinely removed, it reddens together with the refusal case.
- **What only a router test sees here**, and why the engine's own suite is not enough: that the gate runs **before** the query, so a refusal is `403` and not an empty page — a consumer reading "no history" stops, and must stop for the reason it was given; and that D-125's two refusals survive the boundary as `400` rather than surfacing as an internal fault.
- **Owed and unchanged**: the export surface of §5 is not mounted (this is the interactive read), and D-270's two migration-shaped debts stand — commit order is approximated by the authoring instant, and the covering index does not exist.

#### D-274 [M] The five runner-taking writers D-272 named, extracted — and what the clone still owes after them

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/storage/repo/plan_shape_repo.rs` (four), `pricing/src/infra/storage/repo/plan_repo.rs` (one); D-266, D-272.
- **Decision (2026-08-08): `replace_phases`, `replace_addon_rules`, `set_descriptor_set`, `replace_composites` and `update_draft` each gain an `_on(runner, …)` form**, and the method becomes a delegation that opens the transaction and calls it. The shape is `plan_repo::create_draft_on`'s and `price_repo::create_draft_on`'s, which already existed — this closes the gap D-272 measured, where a caller that already owns a transaction had no way to join it and had to open a second.
- **Produced by script with assertions before writing, not by hand**, `m20260802_000051`'s rule applied to Rust rather than to SQL: the generator parsed each method's signature and closure body out of the source, asserted it found exactly one of each, and asserted every extracted name appears exactly once in both the free function and the delegation. All four parsed identically on the first run, which is itself the evidence that the four were one shape.
- **One difference from the original, and it is deliberate**: `update_draft`'s pre-swap refusal opened a **fresh connection**, because the method has not entered its transaction at that point. The `_on` form is already on the caller's runner and reads the refusal there — which is also the more honest read, being the state the mutation would actually have seen.
- **Behaviour is unchanged and that is measured, not asserted**: the phase, add-on-rule and descriptor-set suites (14/16/13), `sqlite_publish_commit` (23) and the 38-case plan-repository suite are green with no edits, and every existing caller now exercises the extracted body through the delegation. A refactor whose only evidence is that it compiles is a refactor nobody has tested. **Corrected 2026-08-09 (D-276): this bullet first called those four "the four shape suites", and the fourth is not one** — `replace_composites` has no dedicated suite at all, and its cover is `sqlite_publish_commit` plus `sqlite_plan_repo` and `sqlite_clone`. A count that names the wrong suites is a count nobody can re-measure.
- **What the clone still owes, named rather than left to be discovered**: the composition copy (D-269) goes through `BundleRepo`'s methods, and that repository's own helpers are **private** — so making the clone one transaction needs runner-taking forms there too, in a third repository. Until then `clone_plan` remains the sequence D-266 recorded, and the clone route stays blocked on D-272's finding. This entry is the prerequisite, not the fix.

#### D-275 [H] The clone became one act — and what the probe found on the way

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs`, `pricing/src/infra/storage/repo/bundle_repo.rs`, `pricing/tests/sqlite_clone.rs`, `pricing/tests/postgres_clone_atomicity.rs`; D-266, D-269, D-272, D-274.
- **Decision (2026-08-08): `clone_plan_on(runner, …)` performs the whole clone on the caller's transaction**, and `PlanCloner::clone_plan` becomes a delegation that opens one. `copy_bundle` and `copy_rows` follow it out of the impl block as free functions; `BundleRepo` gains `create_on`, `replace_composition_on`, `find_by_plan_on` and `load_composition_on`, so the composition copy joins the same transaction. **`PlanCloner` now holds only the database** — the four repositories it used to hold were the reason it could not be atomic, a repository method being a transaction of its own.
- **Runner-taking rather than transaction-opening, and that is the route's requirement, not a preference**: `idempotent::guarded` runs its mutation inside a transaction it opens itself. A cloner that could only open its own would meet that one head-on the day the route is built — which is D-272's finding, and building the fix in the transaction-opening shape would have paid it twice.
- **The probe found something real, and it was not what this entry first said it was — corrected 2026-08-09 by review, see D-276.** The new case clones inside a caller's transaction that then fails and asserts nothing survives; armed against `PlanCloner::clone_plan`, it failed on the survival assertion, the draft plan outliving the caller's rollback. What was armed was the **new delegation**, not the pre-D-275 entry point, and the difference matters: the pre-D-275 entry point's first statement was `DBProvider::conn()`, which **is** guarded and answers `ConnRequestedInsideTx`, so inside a caller's transaction it would have errored having written nothing. What the probe actually demonstrated is that **a nested `in_transaction` is not refused and commits independently of the transaction enclosing it** — a live trap in the wrapper this entry had just created, which is why D-276 removes the wrapper rather than documenting it.
- **The failure is the caller's, deliberately, and the reason is a finding of its own.** I looked first for a source that makes the copy refuse halfway, and there is none. `reset_key` changes **four** scope-key axes — plan, phase, eligibility, cohort — of which the two that could collapse two source rows onto one target key are operand-free: `Cohort::Generation` only ever accompanies `existing_grandfathered`, which D-268 excludes (and `ScopeKey::new` enforces the pairing as a biconditional, so a stored violation fails on load before any write), and eligibility has no surviving non-default class. The phase remap is injective, and `plan_id` is the target by construction. **`price_overlay` is not on that list and this entry first put it there**: `PriceOverlay` has one variant and `ScopeKey::new` hard-codes it, so it is not an axis any reset can touch. D-266's claim that the eligibility and cohort resets are fences rather than behaviour is correct, and now checked against the types rather than restated.
- **Postgres has its own suite** (`postgres_clone_atomicity.rs`, two cases). **The reason given here was wrong and is retracted (D-276):** "SQLite carries on after a failed statement, Postgres aborts the transaction" is true of the engines and irrelevant to these cases, in which no statement fails at all — `in_transaction`'s `Err ⇒ rollback` is engine-independent. The real reason is what the writers touch on the way: eleven tables, eight of them under append-only triggers and `CHECK`s this gear spells twice. Its second case commits the same clone, so that "nothing survived" cannot be satisfied by a clone that never wrote anything.
- **Behaviour is otherwise unchanged and measured**: the nine existing copy-set cases pass untouched.

#### D-276 [H] The wrapper that opened its own transaction, removed — and four claims of mine that outran the code

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs`, `pricing/src/infra/storage/repo/{plan_repo,plan_shape_repo,price_repo,bundle_repo}.rs`, `pricing/tests/sqlite_clone.rs`, `pricing/tests/postgres_clone_atomicity.rs`, `docs/design/12-operator-efficiency.md`; corrects D-274 and D-275. Found by two review passes over the green tree, neither of which any gate could have replaced.
- **Decision (2026-08-09): `PlanCloner` is deleted; `clone_plan_on` is the only door.** The wrapper held a `DBProvider` and opened its own transaction, and **`Db::in_transaction` does not consult the task-local `IN_TX`** — so calling it from inside another transaction silently nests, and the inner one commits whatever the outer rolls back. It had no production caller: `PlanCloner` was referenced nowhere in `src/` outside its own file, and the route that would have used it is exactly the caller that owns a transaction already. A convenience door with one user, opening onto the defect the module exists to prevent, is not documented — it is removed. The nine copy-set cases now compose the clone the way the route will, through a `clone_it` helper that owns the transaction.
- **What made the wrapper safe before was an accident, and the composition consumed it.** The pre-D-275 entry point began with `DBProvider::conn()`, which **is** guarded (`DbError::ConnRequestedInsideTx`). Removing the connection removed the guard with it, and nothing replaced it — the misuse became silent at exactly the moment the type stopped needing a connection.
- **Four claims of mine were wrong as written, and are corrected in place rather than left standing**: (1) D-275's probe narrative said the *old* cloner would have committed silently — it would have errored at the connection; what the probe armed was the new delegation, and what it demonstrated is the nesting trap above. (2) D-275 listed "eligibility, cohort and overlay" as the three axes the reset changes; `reset_key` changes four (plan, phase, eligibility, cohort) and **overlay is not one** — `PriceOverlay` has a single variant. (3) D-274 called four suites "the four shape suites" when the fourth is `sqlite_publish_commit`. (4) `copy_bundle_on`'s doc promised a `StaleRowVersion` race the composition had made unreachable, and `copy_bundle_on`'s summary said `BundleRepo` refuses nowhere when `replace_composition` refuses.
- **The Postgres suite was agreeing with itself.** Its seed was a plan and one price row, which reached **three of the clone's nine write functions** and skipped every guarded branch — while its own module doc justified its existence by "a clone assembled from several writers". It now seeds phases, an add-on rule, a descriptor set, a composite meter, and a bundle with two components and a rev-share group, and **both** cases assert every class: present after the commit, absent after the rollback. Neither reading can be satisfied by a clone that never touched the class. **This sentence was false when first written and is corrected here**: the enriched seed landed a round before the assertions caught up with it, so the add-on rule and the composition were authored and never read back — two writers could have been deleted with the suite green. A seed is not coverage; the read-back is.
- **And its stated reason was the wrong one.** "SQLite carries on after a failed statement; Postgres aborts the transaction" is true of the engines and irrelevant here: no statement fails, and `in_transaction`'s `Err ⇒ rollback` is engine-independent. The engine-specific surface is what the writers touch on the way — six tables under append-only triggers and `CHECK` constraints this gear spells **twice**, once in PL/pgSQL and once as `RAISE(ABORT, …)`, which are only ever as equal as the suites holding them to it. That is the reason, and it is now the one the suite gives.
- **A second undocumented difference in D-274, now named**: `PlanRepo::update_draft`'s value refusals used to run *before* `in_transaction` and now run inside it, because the method became a delegation. A patch refused on its value alone costs a `BEGIN` and a `ROLLBACK` where it cost neither. Error identity is preserved; the trade is accepted because the alternative is two implementations of the checks, which is what the extraction exists to remove. `PriceRepo::create_draft` makes the opposite trade and says so — the difference is that its check is a whole rendering pass and these are three comparisons.
- **`replace_addon_rules` had no operand in either clone suite**, so the writer whose faithfulness D-274 called measured was measured by nothing: the SQLite seed authored phases, a descriptor set and composites but never an add-on rule, and the copier's `if !rules.is_empty()` branch therefore never ran. Both suites now seed one and assert it arrives whole — probed by disabling the copy, which reddens the assertion. This is the same shape as the Postgres seed above and the same lesson twice: **a writer with no fixture is covered by the count of tests, not by any of them.**
- **The six multi-row runner-taking writers now state that the runner must be a transaction** — the four in `plan_shape_repo`, `plan_repo::update_draft_on` and `bundle_repo::replace_composition_on`, which `plan_repo::create_draft_on` had already established as the house rule and the new siblings had not repeated. Each writes child rows *and* a D-135 audit record; on a bare connection those are autocommit statements and "a rolled-back edit leaves no record" fails silently. Every current caller supplies one — this is a latent hole closed by documentation because the type system does not distinguish the two runners.

#### D-277 [M] The clone route — a plans route, a create, and a digest over the source

- **Where**: `pricing/src/api/rest/plans.rs`, `pricing/tests/rest_plans.rs`, `pricing/tests/module_test.rs`, `pricing/tests/rest_authz.rs`; §5's `POST /bss-pricing/v1/plans/{planId}/clone`, D-272, D-275.
- **Decision (2026-08-09): the clone lands in `plans.rs`, not in a module of its own.** The path says so — the source is a plan and the surface belongs to the plan family, exactly as `/abandon` does — and a separate module would owe a router, a mount, a merge into `rest_support` and two censuses to say the same thing. The cost of the choice is that `plans.rs` grows; the cost of the alternative is four registrations whose only content is "this is a plans route".
- **The route needs no new state.** `AuthoringState` already holds `db` and the idempotency gate, and `clone_plan_on` is a free function taking a runner, so it needs no repository handle. That is D-275's dividend, and it is the concrete form of the argument for building the runner-taking shape first: `idempotent::guarded` hands its mutation a `&DbTx<'_>`, and the clone drops into it unchanged.
- **The gate addresses the source, because the target does not exist yet.** `plan × write` with `resource_id = Some(source)` and `owner_tenant_id = Some(caller's tenant)`: there is nothing to authorize against a plan that has not been created, and the authority to make a plan out of this one is authority over this one. The membership assertion refuses a source outside the compiled scope rather than trusting the PDP filtered it — `create_plan`'s posture, and a clone is a write.
- **The idempotency digest is taken over the source plan id, and this is not decoration.** The request has no body. Hashing the empty body would make **every clone in a tenant hash identically**, so a client reusing a key against a different source would be handed the first source's clone instead of `IDEMPOTENCY_PAYLOAD_MISMATCH` — a replay of an act nobody performed. Probed: with the source removed from the digest the mismatch case answers `201` instead of `409`.
- **It is a create, not a version-holder, and the roster had to be told.** `module_test`'s precondition guard classified it by its path — a route naming an existing plan looks like one that would hold that plan's tag — and refused it for declaring no `If-Match`. It holds none: it writes nothing to the source, and there is no version of the target to assert. So it joins the three creates whose precondition is the gate's key. **The guard found a registration I had not: five sites were expected and there were six.** This is the second time a route census has caught a classification the author got wrong, which is the argument for a census that reads the registration rather than a list somebody maintains.
- **No `ETag` on the response.** The clone answers a *receipt*, not a revision, and a tag on a body that is not the resource is a precondition token pointing at nothing. A caller wanting the draft's tag reads `Location`, which is the read it would make before editing anyway.
- **The receipt is the recorded body**, so a replay answers the first caller's plan id **and its notices** verbatim — which is the only way a second caller learns that the class it is missing was left behind on purpose rather than lost by the retry.

#### D-278 [H] A scope has two jobs, and the clone is the route where they diverge — REMEDY REVERSED BY D-279

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs`, `pricing/src/api/rest/plans.rs`, `pricing/src/infra/error_mapping.rs`, `pricing/src/domain/error.rs`, `pricing/tests/{sqlite_clone,postgres_clone_atomicity,rest_plans,rest_preview}.rs`, `pricing/tests/rest_support/mod.rs`; corrects D-277, **and its own central remedy is reverted by D-279**. Found by review over the green tree.
- **~~Decision (2026-08-09): `clone_plan_on` takes two scopes~~ — REVERSED BY D-279, read that first.** The reasoning below is kept because its *diagnosis* is sound and D-279 builds on it; its remedy is not. What follows is left as written except for this marker: **`clone_plan_on` takes two scopes** — `source_scope` for every read and `target_scope` for every write — and the route compiles both. **A compiled `AccessScope` is the authorization answer *and* the SecureORM row filter.** `pricing_plan` binds `RESOURCE_ID` to `plan_id` and `validate_insert_scope` fails closed on an INSERT the constraints do not admit, so a PDP answering the clone's gate with the id-shaped constraint this gear's PEP advertises (`authz::SUPPORTED_PROPERTIES`) compiles a scope naming the **source** — and handing it to the writers denies the target's INSERT for a principal the PDP had just authorized.
- **This is the gear's first route that gates on resource A and writes resource B.** Every other mutating route on the plan path gates on the plan it writes, so the two jobs coincided and nobody had to notice; `create_plan` passes `resource_id = None` and so never invites an id-shaped answer. The target scope is `create_plan`'s exactly, for its reason: the row being written carries an id no constraint could have named, the surface having just minted it.
- **No suite could have seen it**, which is why it is recorded rather than quietly fixed: every PDP double in `rest_support` returns an `OWNER_TENANT_ID`-only constraint. The evidence is now two domain cases built on `AccessScope::for_resources` — an id-shaped **source** scope still clones, and **one** id-shaped scope in both roles refuses and leaves nothing behind. The second is kept as a case rather than run once as a probe, because it is the whole evidence that the two parameters are load-bearing.
- **`CLONE_SOURCE_NOT_FOUND` is minted**, paying the debt D-277 should have. §5 declares the code and the route shipped a bare canonical 404; `clone.rs` had argued the code should not be minted ahead of the route that returns it, and that reason expired the moment the route landed. It rides `context.resource_name` because the canonical not-found family has no `reason` slot — `PRICE_ROW_ABSENT`'s rendering and its `T-11` note — and the reading now lives once in `rest_support::not_found_code` rather than twice.
- **The receipt's counts and notices are now asserted on the wire** — **overstated, corrected by D-281**: of `notice_view`'s three wire codes only `no_coverage_scheduled` is exercised, and `composites_copied` is structurally zero at the HTTP layer. They were not, and every clone case seeded a bare plan, so all three counts were structurally zero and `notice_view`'s three wire codes were **never executed by any test** — they could have been typoed or swapped with the suite green. The same defect as the Postgres seed a wave earlier, in a different place: **coverage is what reads a value back, not what makes a path run.**
- **Also**: the `no ETag` decision now has an assertion (a decision no test reads is one a later group undoes by being helpful); a foreign tenant's plan is proved indistinguishable from an absent one on the one route that takes a plan id and creates something from it; **a retired plan is clonable** — `load_current` admits `Published | Retired`, `error_mapping` already says a retired plan has "a clone route forward", and now the route says so too; and the description's three paragraphs were collapsing into one, because a `\`-continued blank line in a Rust string literal is not a blank line.

#### D-279 [H] D-278's diagnosis was right and its remedy was wrong — a loud denial made silent

- **Where**: `pricing/src/infra/clone.rs`, `pricing/src/api/rest/plans.rs`, `pricing/tests/{sqlite_clone,postgres_clone_atomicity}.rs`; **reverts D-278's scope split**. Found by the fifth review over the green tree, one commit after the fourth introduced it.
- **What D-278 got right**: a compiled `AccessScope` is the authorization answer **and** the SecureORM row filter, and the clone is this gear's first route that gates on resource A and writes resource B.
- **What it got wrong**: it gave the clone a *source* scope for the reads, on the assumption that a scope naming a plan filters that plan's rows. It does not. **The child tables bind `RESOURCE_ID` to their own id, not to `plan_id`** — `plan_phase` to `phase_id`, `pricing_price` to `price_id`, `composite_meter` to `composite_id`, `pricing_bundle` to `bundle_id`. Only `pricing_plan`, `pricing_plan_addon_rule` and `pricing_plan_descriptor_set` bind `plan_id`, which is exactly why `load_current`, `load_addon_rule_set` and `load_descriptor` kept working and masked the other four.
- **The consequence was worse than the defect.** Under precisely the PDP answer D-278 was written for — one constraint AND-ing the tenant with `RESOURCE_ID IN (source)` — the source reads returned **empty** and the target writes succeeded under the resource-less scope. So the route stopped failing and started producing a committed plan with no phases, no price rows, no composites and no bundle, carrying a grant set whose `perPhase` keys still named the *source's* phases: **verbatim the C-7 dangling-key defect the module doc claims is fixed.** A 403 became silent data loss.
- **And the evidence certified it.** D-278's case `a_source_scope_naming_only_the_source_still_clones` asserted the receipt's `cloned_from` and that a draft row existed — both true of an empty clone. Adding `assert_eq!(receipt.phases_copied, 2)` makes it fail against the same fixture that another case in the same file proves copies two phases. **A test that asserts a subset that survives the bug is not evidence**; this is the session's third instance of the same shape, after the Postgres seed and the receipt counts.
- **Decision (2026-08-09): one scope, gated on the source.** Under an id-shaped answer the clone is refused at its first write, which is the correct answer: **this gear cannot express "that plan's subtree" as a `RESOURCE_ID` constraint on any route**, and `patch_plan` and its siblings have the same exposure. Failing closed beats writing half a plan. The two cases are now a refusal and its companion — the same fixture under a scope the gear can honour, copying everything — because "the clone was refused" and "there was nothing to clone" are otherwise the same green.
- **The wider property is named rather than fixed here**: an id-shaped `plan × write` constraint is unusable as a subtree filter anywhere in this gear. That is a schema-and-PEP question, not a clone question, and it wants an owner decision rather than a route-local workaround.
- **Also corrected**: the OpenAPI description's paragraphs still collapsed after D-278 claimed to fix them — a `\`-continued blank line elides both newlines, so removing it changed nothing; they now carry real `\n\n`. `CLONE_SOURCE_NOT_FOUND`'s doc credited D-277 with paying a debt D-278 paid. `clone_plan_on`'s `# Errors` still said the code was "not a declared code in this crate" one commit after minting it.
- **Owed, named**: of `notice_view`'s three wire codes only `no_coverage_scheduled` is exercised on the wire; the other two need a cutover-made fixture the REST harness has no seeder for. `composites_copied` is asserted at the domain layer and is structurally zero at the HTTP layer, `attach_shape` attaching no composites.

#### D-280 [M] The bulk import's Phase 1 begins with the rule that needs no store — and with the key it is judged on

- **Where**: `pricing/src/domain/import.rs` (new), `pricing/src/domain/import_tests.rs` (new), `pricing/src/domain/scope_key.rs`; §3 `algo-bulk-import` `inst-bk-phase1`, §4 `inst-bi-validate`, D-118, D-148.
- **Decision (2026-08-09): Phase 1 splits by what a rule needs, and the batch-only half lands first.** Some of Phase 1 needs the store — whether the row addresses a **published** key (`IMPORT_TARGETS_PUBLISHED`, D-118), whether its key holds a pending unit (D-35), whether its `If-Match` is current. The in-batch duplicate needs only the batch. The split is not tidiness: the batch-only rules run before the caller has opened anything, and their behaviour is statable without building a world.
- **The duplicate is judged on `ScopeKey::draft_row_identity`, not on the key.** Every axis **except** `meter` and `dimension_key` — which is the column list of `uq_pricing_price_scope_key_draft`. **Two rows differing only in their usage line are one draft row**, and an equality over the whole `ScopeKey` calls them distinct: exactly the case D-148 moved into Phase 1, because commit is per-row and cannot report a collision as a batch fault. Probed — swap the identity for whole-key equality and that one case reddens while the other six stay green.
- **The identity is a type, built by a no-rest destructure, and lives in `scope_key.rs`.** `is_sibling_of`'s discipline applied to a second partial key: a comparison spelled "every axis except two" cannot be maintained at a distance, so an eleventh axis must be a compile error rather than an input that silently stops being compared. A type rather than a predicate because the check is over a **set** — a pairwise predicate makes the batch quadratic for no reason.
- **Both sides of a collision are named**, each row told which others it collides with. A collision has two rows in it and neither is more at fault; reporting only the second would also make the report depend on arrival order, which is not a property of the batch.
- **Every rule answers for every row.** `inst-bk-phase1`'s all-or-nothing posture only pays for itself if the report is complete — an operator fixing a batch one refusal at a time is doing exactly what the rule exists to spare them.
- **Owed next, named**: the store-dependent half of Phase 1; the D-177 arm refusing `includedAllowance` / `tierQualificationWindow` per row, which **inherits** `PRIMITIVE_RULES_UNBUILT` rather than minting a second code (the design says so, and `domain::publish::rules::NoUnjudgedPrimitive` is the backstop that stays); Phase 2's per-row commit under the bulk lock; and the three routes.

#### D-281 [H] The truncation defect is live on `publish`, and the fix belongs in the PEP — an owner decision

- **Where**: named, not fixed — `pricing/src/api/rest/publish.rs`, `pricing/src/infra/publish.rs`, `pricing/src/authz.rs`; corrects D-279's scoping and three of its claims. Found by the sixth review over the green tree.
- **The property, stated once and correctly.** A compiled `AccessScope` is both the authorization answer and the SecureORM row filter. `RESOURCE_ID` resolves to each entity's **own** id column. **Only three of the gear's plan-side tables bind `plan_id`** — `pricing_plan`, `pricing_plan_addon_rule`, `pricing_plan_descriptor_set`. Everything else binds its own: `plan_phase`→`phase_id`, `pricing_price`→`price_id`, `pricing_price_tier_band`→`price_id`, `composite_meter`→`composite_id`, `pricing_bundle`→`bundle_id`, `pricing_bundle_component`→`bundle_id`, `pricing_audit_log`→`chain_id`. **So no route in this gear can express "that plan's subtree" as a `RESOURCE_ID` constraint.** D-279 said "four of the seven tables"; it is more than that, and the three that *do* bind `plan_id` are the whole of the exception.
- **`publish` has the live version, and it is the worst place for it.** `publish_scope` compiles `plan × publish` with `resource_id = Some(plan_id)`, and `infra::publish::assemble` reads the phase set, the composite set and the candidate rows under that scope. Under an id-shaped answer the subject arrives **empty**: the rules iterate empty vectors and the content pin frames a count of zero — on the **freezing act**, whose output is immutable on a seven-year horizon. `infra/publish.rs`'s own comment already describes that consequence for the composite set. `read_model` and `migration` read the same way.
- **`patch_plan`'s exposure is a different failure, and D-279 flattened the two.** Its write is denied inside a transaction and rolls back — it fails closed. But `answer_revision` then reads the shape back **under the same scope** and answers `200` with an **empty phase chain** for a plan that has phases, with an `ETag` computed over that truncated view. A silent misreport, not a silent write. `GET /plans/{id}` is the same.
- **The recommended resolution is one line and it is not mine to make**: `authz::SUPPORTED_PROPERTIES` advertises `RESOURCE_ID`, which is what invites the PDP to send a constraint the gear cannot honour anywhere. Removing it closes the whole class at the source rather than route by route. The consequences are gear-wide — every single-row gate stops being row-filtered and becomes tenant-filtered plus its own explicit predicate — so this is an **owner decision**, recorded here rather than taken. The last attempt to fix this class route-locally (D-278) made a loud failure silent.
- **A claim of D-279's, corrected**: "a 403 became silent data loss" is wrong about the 403. A scope denial surfaces as `RepoError::Db` → `DomainError::Internal` → **500**, both before and after — the idempotency claim is denied first, because `pricing_idempotency_dedup` binds `RESOURCE_ID` to `client_key`, a string, which no UUID constraint can match. The data-loss half stands; the status was never a denial.
- **And two about its own evidence.** The companion case D-279 added asserted nothing two neighbouring cases in the same file did not already assert more strongly, so its stated justification was false when written — it is removed. The refusal case asserted only `is_err()`, which is satisfied by `CloneSourceNotFound`, by a stale version, or by a denial at any writer: **the fourth instance of "asserts a subset that survives the bug", one paragraph after D-279 named it as the third.** It now asserts that the refusal names the scope and is *not* a not-found — the source is genuinely found, `pricing_plan` being one of the three tables that bind `plan_id`.
